# What twenty parallel audits found, 2026-09-20

## What this is

Twenty read-only agents were run over the package, and the partition this time is by
**subsystem** rather than by defect class. That is the one thing that makes this run
different from `AUDIT-2026-09-18.md`, which ran twenty agents two days earlier partitioned
by trap, one agent per recurring defect. A second pass on the same axis rediscovers the
first pass, so the axis was rotated: every one of the 193 Python files in `defumat/` was
owned by exactly one agent and read in full, and no file was read twice. Each agent was
handed a compressed list of what `OPEN.md` Part III, `MEMORY-AUDIT.md` and the 09-18 audit
already record, and was required to grep those four documents for its symbol and say in a
`known_ref` field which item it extends or that it is in none of them.

20 agents, 0 errors, 34 minutes, 914 tool calls. **92 findings: 37 bugs and 55 costs.**
One agent, `audit:symmetry-kpoints`, stalled after 830 s and was retried once; it returned
on the retry, which is what "0 errors" above means and does not hide.

**66 of the 92 say they are in none of the four records and 26 say they extend one.** The
26 are worth reading first for restatement rather than for content: five in particular are
a recorded item at a new site rather than a new item, and they are
`workflows/vc_relax.py:292` against `MEMORY-AUDIT` A3, `response/sternheimer.py:979` and
`response/velocity.py:277` against `OPEN.md` H4, `scf/density.py:337` against M4, and
`solvers/davidson.py:747` against the dtype breaches already listed in `MEMORY-AUDIT` §7.
A sibling at a new site earns its place and should say so; a restatement does not.

**There was no refutation stage, and that is the difference from the 09-18 run.** Twenty
agents was the budget and it was spent entirely on coverage, so what follows is one
agent's reading with no adversary. The 09-18 audit refuted 3 of 48, and remarked that a
refutation rate that low is evidence about the refuters rather than about the code; here
there is not even that. Nothing below was reproduced by running anything. **Every entry is
a claim about what the source says, and none of it is a number**, so none of it belongs in
`PLAN.md` until it has one.

The entries below under "What was confirmed by a second reading" were checked against the
source and against the vendored Fortran, and nothing was run to check them. Everything
else carries the agent's own confidence and nothing more.

## What was confirmed by a second reading

**`defumat/pseudo/radial.py:58`, and it is the one to take first.** On an even-length mesh
the second-to-last Simpson weight is set to `1/3` where QE's is `1`. QE's `simpsn.f90:35-51`
runs its loop to `mesh-1`, so for an even mesh `f(n-1)` already carries `2/3` from the loop
and the even-mesh closure adds `f(mesh-1)*rab(mesh-1)` on top, giving `3/3`; the Python
overwrites that slot with `1/3` instead of adding to it. The file's own comment transcribes
the closure correctly and the code contradicts it, and the other three closure weights,
`15/12`, `5/12` and the leading `1/3`, all match. So every radial integral over an **even**
mesh is short by `2/3 f(n-1) rab(n-1)`. What bounds it is that `mesh_cutoff_index` forces an
odd count for everything that goes through `msh`, so the reachable sites are the integrals
taken over a full or a PAW radial mesh instead, and finding which those are is the first
thing to do rather than the last.

**`defumat/system/builder.py:552`.** The explicit-k-list branch of `_recelled_kpoints` is
the identity. `kpoints.crystal(self.cell)` is `cell.k_to_crystal(coords)` and
`KPoints.from_crystal(..., self.cell)` is `cell.k_to_cartesian(...)`, both with the *new*
cell, so the round trip returns the cartesian coordinates it was given. The comment two
lines above says crystal coordinates are what a deformation leaves alone, which is true and
is not what the code does: an explicit `K_POINTS tpiba` or `crystal` card does not follow a
deformed cell, where the `grid` branch beside it rebuilds properly.

**`defumat/response/photocurrent.py:260`.** The shift current takes the bare diagonal of
`dH/dk` for `D^a_nm`, and `defumat/response/shg.py:180` computes the multiplet block average
of that same diagonal with a docstring naming rule D4 and quoting the mesh on which it
bites. Two siblings, one quantity, one of them fixed.

**`defumat/response/effmass.py:552`.** The mixed curvature is written symmetrically by
assignment, `curvature[:, :, a, b] = value` and `curvature[:, :, b, a] = value`, so
`_asymmetry` of it is identically zero for `method = 'eigenvalue'`. This is the project's
own "a check whose null result cannot be told from a pass" trap, in a diagnostic added to
watch a stencil that the 09-18 audit had already found wrong once.

**`defumat/forces/analytic.py:372` and `defumat/stress/analytic.py:166`.** Neither file
contains the string `gamma_only` anywhere, where `forces/energy.py`, the autodiff route
beside them, carries it through five call sites and two static arguments. The transcribed
cross-check routes therefore sum over the stored half sphere with no `2 Re(sum) - (G = 0)`
correction. What was checked is the absence, not the size.

**`defumat/response/phononq.py:973`.** `np.asarray(wavefunctions).shape[2]` is there
literally, pulling the whole ground-state block to the host to read one integer.

**`defumat/response/strain.py:441`.** The loop is `for a in range(3): for b in range(3)`
over a symmetric strain label, and the results go into a `(3, 3)` object array. The last
step is closed: `strain_tangent` at `strain.py:123` returns
`0.5 * (E_ab + E_ba)` and its docstring states `T^(ab) = T^(ba)` outright, so three of the
nine `jvp` are bit-identical duplicates.

## One finding that the second reading did not settle the way it looked

`defumat/xc/functional.py:487` was returned as a saturated-point mask applied one
derivative order too low, dropping `rho de_xc/drho` from the energy gradient. It looked at
first like an agent misreading a deliberate fix, since the mask was put there to stop a
`Z*` of `NaN` on triplet O2 and the lines around it carry a measured number. The grep that
was supposed to dismiss it does not.

The entry's own docstring states the intent in bold: *"The value and the first derivative
are exactly the expression below; only the second derivative is masked"*, and it names the
path itself, *"`scf.potential` builds `etxc` from this, `forces.energy` takes `etxc` into
the total energy"*. Both halves check out: `scf/potential.py:257` reaches
`spin_potential_and_energy_density`, whose energy half is this masked method, and
`forces/energy.py:538` puts `potential.etxc` into the total that the force differentiates.
What the code does is `jnp.where(saturated, raw(jax.lax.stop_gradient(rho)),
raw(regular))`, and a `stop_gradient` on a `where` branch zeroes **every** order on that
branch, not the second alone. So the method does reach a differentiated energy path, and
at a saturated point its first derivative is zero where the docstring says it is the
expression.

It is left alone rather than edited, deliberately. The mask is load-bearing -- removing it
naively is the `NaN` Born charge it was written against -- and `CLAUDE.md`'s own G1 entry
says which form works, which is masking *the argument the derivative is taken at* rather
than the output. What this needs is a measurement on a saturated cell, the force with and
without, not another reading.

## What to do with it

The order is this project's own. Take the `certain` bugs first and, for each, build the
cheapest input that makes the defect visible, then measure it; an entry that cannot be made
to show itself is not a finding whatever its confidence field says. The Simpson weight and
the two gamma-only analytic routes are the three that need no new physics to reproduce.

On the cost side the largest single group is the response stack, six `certain` entries in
`piezo.py`, `electrostriction.py`, `phonon.py`, `phononq.py` and `strain.py` are all the
same shape, a primal recomputed for each tangent where one `jvp` at a shared primal point
would do, which is `OPEN.md` H7 generalised beyond the elastic tensor.

`PLAN.md` section rather than here, which is this project's rule.

Left free the shell turns, because the carried density's exchange field pulls it, so the cost is iterations and the instrument is an invariance rather than a tolerance -- a global spin rotation is free on a scalar-relativistic dataset, so **2, 7 and 8** iterations at `angle1 = 0, 45, 90` against **2, 2 and 2** after. With `mixing_fixed_ns = 10` it is not a cost: the block's residual is zero while it is held (`electrons.f90:819-836` does the same), the run met `conv_thr` at iteration 6 without the freeze being released, and it **reported success with the shell on z and the density on x**, **4.11 mRy** above the right answer on a cell whose anisotropy is exactly zero. The packed-pair rule now has one owner, `spinor_ns_from_components`, with `initial_ns_noncollinear` rewritten through it bit-identical. **The reverse face took two passes**: the demotion's refusal moved into the projected frame, and it still fired for a `nspin = 1` target and for `magnetization='none'`, both of which discard the moment outright -- there was no `4 -> 1` `ns` test in the file at all. `PLAN.md` P23b | `tests/unit/test_continuation_machinery.py` (three new), `tests/unit/test_textured_seeding.py` (one new), `tests/regression/test_noncollinear_hubbard_resume.py` at **30 s**. Three of the four fail against the old code **on behaviour** -- the promotion lands on 1.0 where the in-plane answer is 0.8, the dropped-moment demotion raises, and the regression test reads 8 iterations against 2 -- and the fourth, the off-`z` demotion, fails on the new helper's import and is a new capability rather than a guard. The gate at **2854 passed, 64 skipped in 12 m 47 s** |

**"Closed" here means reproduced and fixed, and for two of the six it also means
tested.** The fast gate passed on the Simpson fix at 2817 passed and 64 skipped, and the
two refusals have a unit test that fires them. The other four are in files the *slow* set
covers and that set has not been run since the edits, which is said in the table rather
than left to be assumed.

**Nineteen closed, ten have a number of their own, one entry is downgraded and one refuted.** The other five
change no number at all, which is why they were the ones to take first: two are refusals
where there was silence, one deletes a third of a stage that was recomputing an answer it
had, one removes a host copy, and one corrects a label. The measurement still owed is the strain
stage's saving, which needs a timing rather than a reading.

**The most useful of the nine is the one whose number came back zero, and the first
explanation of that zero was wrong.** The shift current's rule-D4 fix was taken on the
strength of its sibling in `shg`, where the same substitution is worth four orders of
magnitude on silicon, and the A/B here read exactly zero on both cells. The explanation
written down at the time — a velocity diagonal vanishing by symmetry at the critical
points where these two crystals are degenerate — was labelled as an explanation that fits
and had not been checked. Checked, it is **false**: silicon has 555 of 3024 band-k pairs
sitting in a multiplet at the 0.01 Ry the spectrum uses, and the velocity diagonal spreads
by **1.60 Ry bohr** inside one against a mesh maximum of 1.12, so the diagonal is not flat
and `D^a` genuinely moves.

What is true is structural, and it is better than the guess. `v` is Hermitian and `D` is
real, so the `D`-dependent part of `r^b_mn r^{c;a}_nm + r^c_mn r^{b;a}_nm` is `z + conj(z)`,
and the integrand takes the **imaginary** part of that sum. At AlAs's worst k-point the
change moves `D` by 1.24, moves `r^{c;a}` by **1.147e2 on 3.114e2, 37 per cent**, and moves
the integrand by **3.6e-12 on 3.6e3**, the difference's own contribution having
`|Im| = 4.1e-13` against `|Re| = 66.0`. So the shift current is insensitive to `D^a` on
**every** cell, not on these two, and `shg`'s number does not carry over because `chi^(2)`
has no `Im` around the pair.

The fix stays, because `generalized_derivative` is a public entry point and is what the
sum rule is validated against, and it no longer depends on which basis inside a multiplet
the eigensolver returned. Three habits show in that sequence and the third is the one to
keep: the A/B was repeated with `jax.clear_caches()` and the order reversed, in case the
second call had reused the first's compiled executable, and the zero survived; the
explanation was then tested rather than believed because it fitted; and what replaced it
is a structural argument, which `CLAUDE.md` says to prefer wherever both exist.

## One entry refuted, and the premise was the thing to check

`defumat/hamiltonian/noncollinear.py:414` was returned as `likely`: the spinor
Hamiltonian's preconditioner diagonal omits the Hubbard term that the collinear twin
includes, so a noncollinear DFT+U run preconditions Davidson with a Hamiltonian it is not
solving. Every sentence of the reading is true. The conclusion drawn from it is not, and
what settles it is one file of the vendored source: **`pw.x` does not put the Hubbard
potential in `h_diag` either**. `PW/src/usnldiag.f90` imports nothing from `ldaU`, holds
zero occurrences of `ldaU`, `hubbard`, `vhpsi` or `v_hub`, and builds `h_diag` as
`g2kin(i) + v_of_0` (`:50`) plus the `deeq`/`deeq_nc` diagonal, in the `_nc` branch as
well as the collinear one; `c_bands.f90` calls it at `:360`, `:539`, `:660`, `:746` and
`:830` and the only thing that touches `h_diag` afterwards is `oscdft_h_diag`, which is a
different feature. `vhpsi` is applied in `h_psi.f90:244-249`, to the *operator*. So the
spinor class is the one that matches the reference and **the collinear class is the
departure** -- the asymmetry is real and points the other way.

The fix was written, tested and measured before it was reverted, and the measurement
agrees. On `ni-ldau-noncol.in`, the one committed spinor Hubbard cell where the term is not
negligible, one diagonalisation from the same starting vectors at the same threshold:
**157 against 157** steps at `ethr = 1e-6` from the starting density, 222 against 227 at
1e-10, and 197 against 209 and 310 against 306 at the converged density. There is no
configuration in which adding it helps. The collinear side is the same null from the other
direction: `ni-ldau-j0.in` gives **208 against 209** and **332 against 331**, one step in
three hundred, so removing the departure is not worth doing either. Both sides are now
pinned by a test, which is what stops the pair being made consistent in either direction
without a number. `tests/unit/test_hubbard_spinor.py`.

**Three ways the measurement was wrong before it was right**, and all three are this
project's own traps.

The first A/B ran both legs in one process and came back **bit-identical in the energy as
well as the step count**, 30.08 steps and `-85.640749327878` Ry twice over. Two different
preconditioners do not converge bit for bit through eighteen iterations; what happened is
that `jit` caches on the traced function and the argument shapes and does not re-read a
method the trace calls, so the second leg ran the first leg's executable. Outside `jit` the
same patch moves the diagonal by **0.130 Ry**. One process per leg is the fix, and the tell
that something was wrong was the *energy* rather than the steps.

The second was a whole-SCF comparison, which confounds the preconditioner with the SCF
trajectory: 18 iterations reaching 5.03e-10 against 20 reaching 7.83e-11. A preconditioner
cannot move the fixed point, only the path, so the run stops at a different iteration for a
reason that has nothing to do with how well it was preconditioned. What is comparable is one
diagonalisation from the same starting vectors at the same threshold.

The third is the one that would have gone into the record. `bn-ldau-noncol.in` gave
**identical per-k step counts at every k-point and both thresholds**, 209 against 209 and
267 against 267, and two cells agreeing that a term is worth nothing reads as strong
evidence. It is not evidence at all: the Hubbard contribution to that cell's diagonal is
**0.0036 Ry, 0.010 per cent**, against 0.1297 Ry and 0.550 per cent on nickel. BN could not
have shown a difference. Asking how large the perturbation is *before* reading a null is
what separated the two, and it is the "a check whose null result cannot be told from a
pass" trap with the instrument one layer further back.

## One entry downgraded rather than closed

`defumat/ultracell/driver.py:1404` was returned as `certain`: on a run that does not
converge, `UltracellResult.density` is the mixer's extrapolation for the next iteration
rather than the density the reported energy, eigenvalues, occupations and states all came
from. The reading is right and the severity is not. `run_scf` does the same thing --
`SCFResult.density` is `rho`, the mixed density, on its own unconverged branch -- so this
is a convention the two drivers **share**, and it is a defensible one: what a result
carries is what a continuation re-enters with, which is the argument the checkpoint's own
comment makes a few hundred lines away.

What was actually wrong is the sentence beside it, which promised that "an image built
from these is an image of the density that was reported". On the converged branch that
holds, because `density = new` there. On the unconverged branch the states solved the last
iteration's *input* potential while the reported density is a mix of that input and its
output, so an image built from them is an image of neither. The comment is corrected and
the convention is left alone; changing it would put the two drivers out of step to fix a
promise rather than a number.

It is worth recording as a class. An audit agent reading one file cannot see that its
sibling makes the same choice on purpose, so "this file does X" reads as "X is wrong"
rather than "X is the convention". The check is one grep of the sibling driver, and it
changed the verdict here from a wrong answer to a wrong sentence.

## A note on the catalogue below

The head above is written in this project's documentation voice. **The catalogue is not
edited**: each entry's `claim`, `mechanism`, `trigger`, `worth`, `how to confirm` and
`against the record` are exactly the strings the agent returned, so that a reader can tell
what was found from what was said about it afterwards. They use spaced double hyphens and
other habits the voice file bans, and they are left that way on purpose.
# Findings, as the twenty agents returned them

## Bugs, ranked by confidence

### defumat/scf/driver.py:5833 -- `run_scf (the periodic _write_checkpoint call)`

**The mid-SCF checkpoint records field_scale one reducebf decay step before the density it is written beside, so a resumed run re-enters with a field that is 1/reducebf too strong and stays that way for the rest of the run.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-driver`
- mechanism: The iteration body mixes the density (rho, becsum_state, ns_state = _mix(...)), then writes the checkpoint at :5833 with density=rho and field_scale=float(field_scale), and only afterwards, at :5857, applies field_scale *= field.reducebf. The saved density is therefore iteration i+1's input while the saved scale is iteration i's. On resume, run_scf restores field_scale from the checkpoint (resumed_scale, :5410-5412) and enters the loop at iteration resumed_at+1 = i+1, which builds calculation.potential(rho, field_scale, field, ...) with s_i where the uninterrupted run would have used s_i*r. Because the decay is cumulative the whole remaining run is shifted by one step, so every later iteration carries a field a factor 1/r larger than the uninterrupted one. The same run's two write sites disagree with each other: the unconverged exit write at :5886 runs after the last body's decay and so carries the scale for iteration completed+1, while the cadence write at :5833 carries the scale for iteration `iteration`. checkpoint.py:62 states that field_scale being saved and restored is the mechanism that keeps a reducebf run from coming back at the wrong field, which is the claim this off-by-one breaks. The `field` object itself is not stale in any written checkpoint: _refusal refuses the FEEDBACK constraints outright, and MagneticField.feedback returns self for every other constraint, so the field_scale scalar is the only part that moves between iterations.
- reached by: nspin = 2 or noncolin with a b_field or LOCAL_MAGNETIC_FIELDS card and reducebf < 1, run with checkpoint_dir set, killed and resubmitted. Not reached by a FEEDBACK (fixed-spin-moment) constraint, whose checkpoint is refused outright.
- worth: The residual symmetry-breaking field at every iteration after a resume is a factor 1/reducebf too large, 11 per cent at the common reducebf = 0.9 and compounding once per resume. The reported total excludes the field energy by convention, so the error in it is second order, about 8e-7 Ry at a 1e-2 Ry residual on this package's own reference hydrogen case; what moves first order is the converged density and the magnetization the field was there to break symmetry into, plus whether _warn_if_the_field_did_not_fade fires at all.
- how to confirm: One hydrogen atom with a LOCAL_MAGNETIC_FIELDS card, reducebf = 0.5, checkpoint_every = 1, max_iterations = 2, then resume from the same directory; read SCFResult.field_scale and history against an uninterrupted four-iteration run. Uninterrupted, iteration 3 applies 0.25; resumed from the iteration-2 checkpoint it applies 0.5. Reading driver.py:5833 against :5857 settles it without running anything.
- against the record: Extends OPEN.md:2949 ("field_scale was saved and then reset to 1.0 on resume, so a reducebf run came back at full field"), which was the other half of the same mechanism and was fixed by the 2026-09-18 audit. This half -- the write ordering against the decay -- is not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/scf/solvers.py:245 -- `newton_krylov`

**When the backtracking line search exhausts its four halvings, the step actually taken is 0.125 while `history` and the verbose line report 0.0625, so the recorded step length is half the one the solver used.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-body`
- mechanism: The loop at :225-230 evaluates `trial = x + length * direction` and only then does `length *= 0.5`. Four passes therefore evaluate at 1.0, 0.5, 0.25 and 0.125, and leave `length` at 0.0625 with `trial` still holding the 0.125 point. `x, r, psi = trial, r_trial, psi_trial` at :240 accepts that 0.125 step, and `history.append({..., "step": length, ...})` at :245 records 0.0625, which the verbose line at :250-253 prints as `lambda`. The comment at :232-239 states the intended reading explicitly -- 'it is reported in history as step = 0.0625 and the caller can see the solver was crawling' -- so the record and the code disagree about which point was taken. On a successful break the two agree, because the halving has not run yet.
- reached by: Only the exhausted branch: four halvings with no decrease in the residual norm, which is the badly conditioned regime this solver exists for and the one a `history` is read to diagnose.
- worth: No physics moves -- the accepted `x` is a real point and `accuracy_of` is evaluated at it -- so this changes a reported diagnostic rather than a converged number. What it costs is that a reader reconstructing the trajectory from `NewtonKrylovResult.history` cannot reproduce `x`, and a stall reads as twice as bad as it is. It does not scale with anything.
- how to confirm: Read the four lines 225 to 245. Or run `newton_krylov` on a case that exhausts the line search and check `x - x_previous` against `history[-1]['step'] * direction`; they differ by a factor of two.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md -- grepped for `newton_krylov` and `backtrack`; PERFORMANCE.md:1556 mentions the line-search backtracks only as a cost in diagonalisations.

### defumat/scf/fields.py:598 -- `MagneticField.site_residuals`

**The per-site residual of `constrained_magnetization = 'atomic fsm direction'` divides by |m| but not by |n|, so a target longer than one Bohr magneton reads 0.00 degrees at any angle within arccos(1/|n|) and the run reports the texture converged when it is tens of degrees off.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-occ-mag`
- mechanism: `constraint_targets` returns the raw `STARTING_MOMENTS` rows for the two `FEEDBACK_ATOMIC` schemes, deliberately and with a comment saying why (fields.py:326-346: `_orthogonalize`/Elk's `r3vo` projects against `mommtfix` itself, so only its direction is used and storing the unit would be a second convention). The driver stores that array unchanged on the object (driver.py:2265-2292, no normalisation on the way in). `site_residuals` then hands it to `_unit_cosine`, whose own docstring says "per row for unit ``n``" and which computes `sum(m*n)/|m|` with no `/|n|`, so what comes back is `|n| cos(theta)` rather than `cos(theta)`. The next line is `rad2deg(arccos(clip(cosine, -1, 1)))`, and the clip is what turns the scale error into silence in the common direction. `satisfied()` (fields.py:813-819) reads exactly this number against `FSM_ANGLE_TOLERANCE`, and that is the SCF loop's second convergence condition (driver.py:5618 calls `field.satisfied(...)`, :5936-5939 re-reads `site_residuals` against the same tolerance, :6084 puts it on the result). The sibling scheme `'atomic texture'` goes through the same `_unit_cosine` at fields.py:542 and is correct, because its own branch of `constraint_targets` divides by the modulus first (fields.py:357-359) -- so one caller normalises and the other does not, and the helper trusts both.
- reached by: `constrained_magnetization = 'atomic fsm direction'` with a `STARTING_MOMENTS` card whose rows are not unit vectors, which is every physical input: the card is in Bohr magnetons and a transition-metal target is 2 to 3, the hydrogen pair in PLAN.md P85 is 0.26. Noncollinear only; the scheme refuses a zero row at input but nothing checks the length.
- worth: Both directions are wrong and the common one is a false pass. At |n| = 2.2 the cosine clips to 1 for every angle inside 63 degrees, so the residual prints 0.00 degrees and `satisfied()` fires as soon as the density converges -- a run that has held nothing reports `converged = True` with a per-site residual of zero, which is this project's "a check whose null result cannot be told from a pass" with the null manufactured by the clip. At |n| < 1 it is the other way: a perfectly aligned moment reads arccos(|n|), 74.93 degrees at 0.26, so the residual can never reach the 0.1-degree tolerance and the run can never stop. PLAN.md P85's own table is consistent with that floor -- the three `atomic fsm direction` rows report 88.808, 138.642 and 86.342 degrees per site, every one of them above 74.93, against 0.576 for the `atomic` penalty row, which is measured with a vector norm and not through `_unit_cosine` -- so the recorded verdict that the per-atom feedback scheme is "negative" and "200 iterations without converging" is at least partly this defect rather than physics.
- how to confirm: Two probes on `MagneticField.site_residuals` with a stub `regions`, one per direction. Target `[[0, 0, 2.0]]` with a moment 45 degrees off the axis (`m_x = m_z`) must return 45 and returns 0.0, because `2.0 * cos(45) = 1.41` clips to 1. Target `[[0, 0, 0.26]]` with a moment exactly along z must return 0 and returns 74.93. The existing test at tests/unit/test_magnetic_fields.py:989 builds `targets = [[0.0, 0.0, 1.0]]`, the single length at which the bug is invisible, and it exercises `feedback` rather than `site_residuals` in any case. The fix belongs in `site_residuals`, not in `constraint_targets`, because `_atomic_step`'s `_orthogonalize` wants the unnormalised vector.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `site_residuals`, `_unit_cosine` and `fsm direction` across all four documents including PERFORMANCE.md, no hits. The nearest recorded item is PLAN.md P85's measurement table, which reports the symptom as a physics result.

### defumat/pseudo/radial.py:58 -- `simpson_weights`

**On an even-length mesh the Simpson weight of the second-to-last point is set to 1/3 where QE's is 1, so every radial integral over an even mesh is short by two thirds of that point's contribution and the weights no longer integrate a constant exactly.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `basis-radial`
- mechanism: QE 7.5's `upflib/simpsn.f90` even branch is `asum = (loop + func(1)*rab(1) - 0.25*func(mesh-2)*rab(mesh-2) + func(mesh-1)*rab(mesh-1) + 1.25*func(mesh)*rab(mesh)) / 3`, where `loop` already ran over i = 2..mesh-1 with fct = 4 for i even and 2 for i odd. The closure ADDS to what the loop gave. For even mesh, 1-based mesh-1 is odd, so the loop gave it 2 and the explicit `+ func(mesh-1)*rab(mesh-1)` brings the total to 3, i.e. a net weight of 3/3 = 1 -- which is exactly the `+ f(n-1)` (coefficient one, not one third) that QE's own comment and this function's own comment both write down. `simpson_weights` assigns rather than adds: line 57 `coefficients[mesh-3] -= 0.25/3` correctly turns the loop's 4/3 into 15/12 for f(n-2), and line 59 correctly sets 5/12 for f(n), but line 58 `coefficients[mesh-2] = 1.0/3.0` overwrites the loop's 2/3 with 1/3 instead of raising it to 1. The clean invariant is the sum rule: for mesh = 6 and rab = 1 the weights must sum to 5.0 exactly (1/3 + 4/3 + 2/3 + 15/12 + 1 + 5/12), and these sum to 4.3333.
- reached by: Any integration mesh of even length. `pseudo.msh` is forced odd by `mesh_cutoff_index`, so the vloc, rhoat, rhocore and atomic-orbital transforms are safe; `pseudo.kkbeta` is not forced odd and QE passes it to `simpson` unchanged (`beta_mod.f90:115`). In the committed test data the ONCV/SG15 and PseudoDojo datasets have even kkbeta -- Ni.pbe-nc-sg15 230, Cr.pbe-nc-sg15 242, Nb(.rel)-pbe-nc-sg15 170, Ni.rel-pbe-nc-dojo 172, Se.pbe-nc-sg15 274, I.pbe-nc-sg15 338, I.rel-pbe-nc-dojo 232 -- as do their full meshes (602 and 1372), which is what `upf.py:383`, `paw/onecenter.py:840` and `hubbard/yukawa.py:130` integrate over.
- worth: The absolute error is (2/3) * f(n-1) * rab(n-1), so it is the endpoint value of whatever is being integrated. On the SG15 files I checked the beta array is already exactly zero at indices 225-230 (its own cutoff), so the projector form factors do not move there and I cannot claim a number for those; what is wrong for certain is the quadrature itself, and it bites any even-length mesh whose integrand is nonzero near its end -- the widened PAW kkbeta the `projector_form_factors` docstring describes ('the tabulated beta is truncated abruptly rather than tapering to zero, so it is still of order 1e-3 at its own cutoff') is precisely that case. Scales with nothing; it is a fixed per-species error that no cutoff reduces, the same shape as the 1e-6 Ry `msh` off-by-one this module already documents.
- how to confirm: `np.sum(simpson_weights(np.ones(6)))` returns 4.3333 where QE's `simpson(6, f=1, rab=1)` returns 5.0 exactly; more generally `sum(simpson_weights(np.ones(n)))` must equal n-1 for every n and fails by 2/3 for every even n. A direct check against the Fortran is to run `simpson` on a linear ramp over an even mesh and compare.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped 'simpson', 'simpson_weights', 'even mesh', 'even-mesh' across all four documents including PERFORMANCE.md: zero hits)

### defumat/system/builder.py:552 -- `System._recelled_kpoints`

**The explicit-k-list branch of `_recelled_kpoints` converts to crystal coordinates and back with the *same* (new) cell, so it is the identity and the k-points keep the old cell's cartesian coordinates instead of following the deformed reciprocal lattice.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `system-builder`
- mechanism: `with_cell` builds `moved` with the new `cell.at` at line 524 and then calls `moved._recelled_kpoints()` at line 527, so `self.cell` inside that method is already the new cell. Line 552 is `np.asarray(kpoints.crystal(self.cell))`, which is `KPoints.crystal` = `cell.k_to_crystal(coords)` = `coords @ inv(bg_2pi_alat)`, and line 551 hands the result to `KPoints.from_crystal(..., self.cell)`, which is `cell.k_to_cartesian(points)` = `points @ bg_2pi_alat`. Both matrices are built from `moved.cell`, so the product is exactly the identity and `coords`, stored in units of `2 pi / alat` with `alat` static and unchanged, comes out bit for bit what it was while `bg` has moved. The method's own docstring says the opposite of what it does: "an explicit list is carried in *crystal* coordinates, which is what is fixed under a deformation". Carrying crystal coordinates needs the *old* cell for the forward conversion, which is exactly what the positions ten lines up do get -- line 518 is `self.structure.positions_crystal(self.cell)` on the original `self`, and line 519 multiplies by the *new* `cell.at`. The grid branch at line 545 is correct, because `KPoints.automatic` regenerates the mesh from `nk1 nk2 nk3` on the new cell.
- reached by: Any `System.with_cell` or `Calculator.with_cell` on a run whose k-set is an explicit list rather than an automatic grid or a band path, which is `K_POINTS` with option `tpiba` or `crystal`, or with no option at all, since `_build_kpoints` reads a missing option as `tpiba`. That includes the canonical cell: `test-suite/pw_scf/scf.in` writes a bare `K_POINTS` card, so every derived calculator built from it goes through this branch. The two entry points are the variable-cell relaxation's final SCF (`workflows/vc_relax.py:372`, on the default `treinit_gvectors = .false.` path) and each step of the `treinit_gvectors` path (`vc_relax.py:406`). `Calculator.with_positions` also routes through `with_cell`, but harmlessly, since it passes `self.system.cell.at` unchanged.
- worth: The k-set stays where it was in cartesian `2 pi / alat` while the reciprocal lattice moved, so the crystal coordinates of every point change by the deformation. Under an isotropic strain `s` every crystal coordinate scales by `s`, meaning that a two-point special-point set is displaced from the special points by `s` of a reciprocal lattice vector: at the 1.005 used in `tests/unit/test_continuation_machinery.py:877` that is half a per cent, and at the 1.05 of `tests/regression/test_elk_seed.py:225` five per cent. Nothing changes shape, so no array size and no refusal says anything happened, and the run converges and reports a total energy, a force and a stress for a sampling the input did not ask for.
- how to confirm: Host-side, no SCF: build the canonical silicon system from `test-suite/pw_scf/scf.in`, take `np.asarray(system.kpoints.crystal(system.cell))`, call `strained = system.with_cell(np.asarray(system.cell.at) * 1.05)`, and compare `np.asarray(strained.kpoints.crystal(strained.cell))` against it. They differ by the factor 1.05 where they should be equal, and `strained.kpoints.coords` is identical to `system.kpoints.coords`, which is the statement of the bug. The one-line fix is to take the crystal coordinates from the old cell, which means computing them in `with_cell` before `moved` is built, exactly as the positions are.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md as a coordinate defect. `_recelled_kpoints` appears once, in AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:325, where it is cited as being *correct* -- the entry traces the gamma-storage weight flag and notes that this method "rebuilds through `KPoints.from_crystal`, whose `_normalise` reapplies DEGSPIN before `for_spin` takes it out again, so both come out right". That is a statement about the weights only; the coordinates were not examined. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md's `with_cell` mention at line 267 is the unrelated `projectors` option defect.

### defumat/forces/analytic.py:372 -- `_projector_force`

**The transcribed `force_us` sums `<beta|psi>` and `<beta|iG|psi>` over the stored half sphere under gamma-only storage, with no `2 Re(sum) - (G = 0)` correction anywhere on the analytic force path, so the nonlocal force comes back finite, symmetric and wrong.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `forces-stress-relax`
- mechanism: `gamma_storage_is_consumable` (`scf/driver.py:908`) turns the storage on for a norm-conserving, `nosym`, non-Hubbard, collinear run, and `build_basis` (`basis/builder.py:123-147`) halves the **plane-wave** sphere while leaving both dense G sets whole. So the density, Ewald, local and core force terms of `_terms` are untouched, and exactly the two sums that run over the wavefunction sphere are wrong. At `:372` `becp = einsum("skbg,kgc->skbc", psi, conj(vkb))` and at `:373` `becd = einsum("skbg,kgc,kgx->xskbc", psi, conj(vkb), 1j*gcart_of_pw)` are raw half-sphere sums. The true full-sphere value of each is `2 Re(half) - (the G = 0 term)`, which is what `forces/energy.py:_becp_gamma` (`:617-631`) computes for the autodiff route; `becd`'s integrand obeys the same rule, since `vkb(-G) = conj(vkb(G))` and the extra `iG` makes `f(-G) = conj(f(G))` again, and its `G = 0` term is identically zero. The half sum is complex where the true projection is real, so `Re[conj(becp) (deff becd)]` at `:377-379` is off by roughly a factor of two to four **and** picks up a cross term that has no counterpart in the full sum. Nothing downstream notices: `analytic_forces` (`:61-100`) refuses only `noncolin`, potential-only, Hubbard and the tabulated augmentation, `compute_forces` (`forces/__init__.py:131-132`) adds only the potential-only and magnetic-field refusals, and `basis/gvectors.refuse_gamma_storage` is never called from `defumat/forces` or `defumat/stress`.
- reached by: `K_POINTS gamma` with a norm-conserving dataset and `nosym = .true.` -- the exact combination the gamma storage exists for -- taken with `method='analytic'`.
- worth: The nonlocal term is a large fraction of a real force, so the total comes out wrong by tens of per cent while still summing to zero over the atoms and obeying the crystal's symmetry, which is the P68 failure shape one term over. It is not confined to a cross-check: AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:235 already established that `workflows/relax.py:388` and `workflows/vc_relax.py:279` forward `force_method` untouched and that `cli.py:413` exposes `--force-method analytic`, so a gamma-only relaxation with that method walks to a wrong geometry and reports convergence. Gamma storage is what the large production slabs run, which is where the wrong geometry would be most expensive.
- how to confirm: Read `forces/energy.py:617-631` beside `analytic.py:372-373`: the correction exists in one and not the other, and `grep -n gamma defumat/forces/analytic.py defumat/stress/analytic.py` returns nothing. To measure it, run `tests/regression/test_gamma_only.py`'s cell (which already asserts the *autodiff* gamma force against the full-sphere force to 1e-10) a second time with `method='analytic'` and compare against the same cell at an explicit `K_POINTS automatic 1 1 1 0 0 0`; the fix is a transplant of `_becp_gamma` onto both projections, not a new derivation.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `_projector_force`, `analytic_forces`, `_force_us`, and `gamma` crossed with force/stress/analytic: zero hits). AUDIT-2026-09-18 fixed two gamma-storage sites, `scf/density.py` and `response/velocity.py`, and neither is this one; its analytic-force item is the separate `TabulatedAugmentation` `IndexError`.

### defumat/stress/analytic.py:166 -- `_kinetic`

**`stres_knl`'s kinetic stress sums `|c_G|^2` over the stored half sphere under gamma-only storage, so the transcribed kinetic term comes back at roughly half its value with no warning.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `forces-stress-relax`
- mechanism: `occupied = einsum("skn,skng->kg", weights, abs(psi)**2)` at `:166` runs over `planewaves`, which `basis/builder.py:146` halves when `system.kpoints.gamma_only` is consumable. The full-sphere sum is `2 Re(half) - (the G = 0 term)` -- the rule `forces/energy.py:_kinetic_energy` (`:585-590`) applies to the very same contraction, right down to broadcasting `kinetic[..., 0]` back onto the band axes. Here there is no such branch and `analytic_terms` (`:106-150`) has no gamma guard. The masking at `:165` (`kg = where(planewaves.mask, kg, 0)`) handles padding and says nothing about the missing conjugate half, and every other term in this module reads the *dense* G set, which stays whole, so the kinetic term is the only one affected -- which is what makes the error look like a plausible decomposition rather than a broken one.
- reached by: `K_POINTS gamma` on a norm-conserving `nosym` cell, reaching `defumat.stress.analytic_terms` (the term-by-term cross-check against the autodiff decomposition).
- worth: About a factor of two on the largest single contribution to the pressure. The blast radius is narrower than the force's: `compute_stress(method='analytic')` is refused by name (`stress/__init__.py:124-131`) and this module offers no total, so the wrong number reaches the cross-check test and anyone reading the term breakdown, not a vc-relax. It matters because it turns the one independent check on the autodiff stress into a check that fails for a reason that is not the autodiff stress.
- how to confirm: Compare `:166` with `forces/energy.py:585-590`, which is the identical contraction with the correction present. To measure, call `defumat.stress.analytic_terms` on `tests/regression/test_gamma_only.py`'s cell and on the same cell at an explicit `k = 0`; only the `kinetic` entry should move, and it should move by close to a factor of two.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `stres_knl`, `analytic stress`, and `_kinetic` -- MEMORY-AUDIT's five `_kinetic` hits are all `forces/energy.py:_kinetic_energy`, item A11 and the `_kinetic_tensor` note, and PERFORMANCE's one hit is the same).

### defumat/response/photocurrent.py:260 -- `generalized_derivative`

**The shift current builds the band-velocity difference D^a_nm from the bare diagonal of dH/dk, where the sibling module shg.py block-averages that diagonal over each degenerate multiplet for exactly this reason (rule D4).**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `sum-over-states`
- mechanism: Line 260 takes `jnp.real(jnp.diagonal(velocity))` and line 261 forms `delta[a][n,m] = v^a_nn - v^a_mm`, which then enters the sum rule twice: once in `_safe_ratio(velocity[c]*delta[a] + velocity[a]*delta[c], gap, tol)` at line 272 and once in `- g[a]*delta[c]` at line 269. Inside a degenerate multiplet the diagonal of an operator is not invariant under the unitary rotation the eigensolver is free in; only the block trace is. The degeneracy guard does not rescue it, because `_safe_ratio` only zeroes a pair (n,m) that is degenerate *with itself* -- it does nothing when band n sits in a multiplet with some third band n' while m is far away, which is precisely the occupied-empty pair the sum is built from. The same object one order up is `shg.band_velocity_difference` (shg.py:143-183), which exists solely to take the multiplet block average and whose docstring says of the bare form that 'no symmetry check sees it'. So the correct routine is already in the package and `generalized_derivative` does not call it.
- reached by: Any closed, unshifted k-grid -- which `require_a_velocity_sum_regime` *requires* for this quantity -- so Gamma and the zone-boundary points are always in the sum. Norm-conserving, nspin = 1 or spinor, fixed occupations. Every zincblende cell (AlAs, the committed validation case) has a threefold degenerate valence top at Gamma.
- worth: shg.py reports the measured analogue: on centrosymmetric silicon at 4x4x4 the two Delta-carrying terms read 1499 and 238 with the bare diagonal against 0.10 and 0.055 with the block average, while the other three terms sat at 0.09 -- four orders of magnitude. The shift-current tensor stays exactly -43m, still vanishes on silicon and still vanishes below the gap either way, so nothing in the existing validation would show it. The size on sigma^abc itself is unmeasured.
- how to confirm: `tests/unit/test_photocurrent_machinery.py::_random_ingredients(degenerate=True)` already builds a degenerate block at a synthetic k-point. Rotate the states inside that block by a random unitary (equivalently, conjugate `velocity` and `second` by a block-diagonal unitary that is the identity outside the multiplet), and compare `shift_integrand` summed over the block before and after: a gauge-invariant quantity must not move, and with the bare diagonal it does. Then re-run AlAs on its unshifted 6x6x6 grid with `delta` replaced by `shg.band_velocity_difference(energies, velocity, tol)` and read the change in the 4.17 eV peak.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- I grepped `generalized_derivative`, `band_velocity_difference`, `photocurrent`, `shift current` and `D4` across all four documents. The `photocurrent` hits are MEMORY-AUDIT.md:1274 (a chunking note on the cartesian pair loop), AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:171 (gamma-storage in the velocity operator) and PERFORMANCE.md:6371 (timing); none touches the Delta diagonal. OPEN.md A9 is the same rule D4 applied to a transport band-count diagnostic, a different site.

### defumat/response/effmass.py:552 -- `_by_eigenvalue`

**`EffectiveMass.asymmetry_by_spin` is identically zero for `method = 'eigenvalue'` because that route writes the off-diagonal entries symmetrically by construction, so the one free diagnostic of the stencil's own error returns a clean pass on every cell, every centre and every step size.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `sum-over-states`
- mechanism: Lines 552-553 assign `curvature[:, :, a, b] = value` and `curvature[:, :, b, a] = value` from a single four-point mixed difference, so each per-h tensor is exactly symmetric; the Richardson combination `(4 fine - coarse)/3` at line 345 is linear and preserves that, and `_asymmetry` (line 586) then computes `||M - M^T||/||M||` of an exactly symmetric matrix. The velocity route is different and the diagnostic is meaningful there: `_by_velocity` stacks `(v_b(k + h e_a) - v_b(k - h e_a))/2h` over `a` at line 424-430, so `[a, b]` and `[b, a]` are two genuinely independent estimates. The class docstring at lines 158-160 claims without qualification that the asymmetry 'is a second derivative and so is symmetric exactly; what this measures is the stencil's own error, and it is free', which is false for one of the two documented routes. This is the project's own 'a check whose null result cannot be told from a pass' trap: the discriminator returns the same constant across the whole family it is meant to discriminate.
- reached by: `effective_mass(..., method='eigenvalue')`, at any centre, any `delta`, any `richardson`. Also `method='velocity'` is unaffected.
- worth: No physical number moves. What moves is a reported diagnostic: a reader who runs the eigenvalue route as the independent cross-check the module docstring advertises reads `asymmetry = 0` and takes it as evidence the stencil converged, when it is evidence of nothing. The consequence is that the eigenvalue route -- the one with the larger truncation coefficient (5/12 against 1/12, stated at line 461-463) and the one that is basis-set sensitive at a non-Gamma centre -- carries no error indicator of its own beyond `truncation`.
- how to confirm: Run `effective_mass(..., method='eigenvalue', delta=0.1)` and `delta=0.001` on silicon at Gamma and print `asymmetry_by_spin`: both are at the float64 floor while the masses differ by per cent. The velocity route on the same pair is not. No test or document quotes this field -- I grepped `asymmetry` across `tests/`, `docs/features.tex` and `notebooks/*.md` and found only the phonon and STM uses -- so nothing currently depends on the wrong value.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:153 is the neighbouring effmass item -- the per-k-point plane-wave sphere at an L centre -- and it notes in passing that `_asymmetry` could not see *that* error either, for a different reason (the offset is equal on all three diagonal entries by symmetry). That item is now fixed: both routes build the stencil at the centre's sphere and move it with `at_kcart` (lines 410-418, 519-534). The structural blindness reported here is separate and survives the fix.

### defumat/hubbard/occupations.py:450 -- `initial_ns_noncollinear`

**The starting spinor Hubbard occupation matrix writes the 2x2 spin block into the packed index with the two spin labels swapped, so the y component of the requested starting moment is seeded with the opposite sign.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `hubbard-tddft-projwfc`
- mechanism: The packed index of `ns` is fixed by `occupation_matrix` (occupations.py:110-116), which builds `ns[2 s + t][a, c] = sum_k w conj(p_{s,a}) p_{t,c}` with `p_i = <phi_i|S|psi>` -- the FIRST spin label is the conjugated one. That is exactly QE's `new_ns.f90:402-404`, `nr(m1,m2,is1,is2) += wg CONJG(proj(...is1...)) proj(...is2...)`, flattened at `:519` as `i = npol (is1-1) + is2`, so defumat's packing and QE's are the same object index for index. For such an object the magnetization is read the way the validated noncollinear density reads it: `scf/density.py:435` writes `m_y = 2 Im(conj(up) down)`, so `m_y = +2 Im(ns[1])`, and `hubbard/energy.py:278` agrees (`Im(trace[0,1] - trace[1,0]) = 2 Im(ns[1])`). `initial_ns_noncollinear` builds `block = charge I + spin (sigma . n)` with `block[0,1] = spin (n_x - i n_y)` (lines 443-446) and then writes `ns[2 s1 + s2] = block[s1, s2]` at line 450, putting `(m/2)(n_x - i n_y)` into the slot that must hold `(m/2)(n_x + i n_y)`. QE's own seeder writes the other one: `init_ns.f90:186` sets `esin = (COS(angle2) + i SIN(angle2)) SIN(angle1)` and `:203` `ns(2) = m esin / 2`, which is `(m/2)(n_x + i n_y)` in that same slot. The correct line is `ns[2*s1 + s2] = block[s2, s1]`.
- reached by: A noncollinear DFT+U run (`noncolin = .true.` plus a HUBBARD card) whose starting moment has a y component: `angle2` not a multiple of 180 degrees, or a `STARTING_MOMENTS` vector out of the xz plane. Invisible for a moment along z or x, which is every benchmark the branch was validated on.
- worth: The correlated shell starts polarized along (m_x, -m_y, m_z) instead of the requested direction while the charge density carries the requested one -- a mirrored y component, and for a helix or cycloid in the xy plane a reversed chirality on the Hubbard shell. The function's own docstring states the consequence: nothing in the SCF turns a moment and noncollinear DFT+U is forced to `nosym`, so the starting matrix is the only steering there is, and the run converges to the mirrored texture and reports success. Independent of nk, nbnd and cell size; it is a per-slot seed.
- how to confirm: Read `hubbard/occupations.py:443-450` beside `quantum_espresso/.../PW/src/init_ns.f90:186,203` and `scf/density.py:435`. To measure it without an SCF: build `initial_ns_noncollinear` for one slot with `angle1 = 90`, `angle2 = 90` (a pure +y moment) and read the moment back with `hubbard/energy.py:278`'s own expression, `Im(ns[1] - ns[2])`; it comes out negative. Note that `tests/unit/test_textured_seeding.py:71-73` reads `m_y` as `-2 Im(block[0,1])`, which encodes the builder's convention rather than the density's, so it passes either way -- it is a check whose pass cannot be told from the defect.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `initial_ns_noncollinear`, `hubbard_potential`, `occupation_matrix` in all four documents; the only related entry is AUDIT-2026-09-18's `promote_ns` continuation item, which is about a promotion carrying `ns` across a spin change and not about the seed's spin packing)

### defumat/ultracell/driver.py:1404 -- `run_ultracell`

**On a run that does not converge, `UltracellResult.density` is the mixer's extrapolation for the next iteration rather than the density the reported energy, eigenvalues, occupations and states all came from.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `ultracell-io-sizing`
- mechanism: Inside the loop the convergence test at `:1314` branches: when `converged` is false, `:1337` rebinds `density = mixer.mix(head_in, head_out)`, and when it is true `:1341` rebinds `density = new`. The converged exit at `:1346` therefore packs `new`, which is right. The fall-through at `:1404` packs whatever `density` holds after the last iteration's `else`-less `if not converged` branch ran, which is the Anderson (or Kerker-preconditioned) step, not `new`. Everything else handed to `_result` on that line -- `levels`, `occupations`, `fermi`, `band`, `terms`, `augmentation_residual`, and `history[-1]` -- was computed from `new`, and `result.states` at `:1415` holds the `vectors` that produced `new`. So the one array a reader interrogates through `UltracellResult.modulation`, `.magnetization` and `.cell_moments()` is the only quantity on the object that is not the state the run stopped at.
- reached by: Any `run_ultracell` call that exhausts `max_iterations`. The gap is largest exactly where the object is most read: a noncollinear cell traversing the flat rigid-rotation manifold (`OPEN.md` Part VI item 3), or the `dr2 = 1.15e-1` stall `OPEN.md` Part VI item 1 records.
- worth: The difference between the two densities is one mixing step on the residual. At `dr2` just above `conv_thr` it is invisible; at a stall it is the whole residual times the mixer's extrapolation coefficients, and an Anderson step is not a convex combination, so `cell_moments()` can report a modulation amplitude outside the range of both the input and the output density -- a plausible, wrong spin-density-wave profile, with a `converged = False` flag beside it that says the run is unfinished rather than that this particular array is the wrong one.
- how to confirm: Read `driver.py:1314-1352` against `:1404`. To measure it, run any ultracell with `max_iterations` set to 2 or 3 so it cannot converge and compare `result.density` against the density `result.states.density(result.occupations)` rebuilds plus `ultracell_augmentation_charge`; the two agree on a converged run and differ by the mixing step on a stopped one. The one-line fix is to carry `new` to `_result` on `:1404`, as `:1346` already does.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `ultracell`, `run_ultracell`, `_result`, `UltracellResult` in all four; OPEN.md Part VI covers ultracell non-convergence as a *convergence* problem and says nothing about which density is reported)

### defumat/workflows/stm.py:283 -- `run_sts`

**run_sts defaults conv_thr to None and hands it straight to fixed_density_states, which divides by it, so the grid= route -- the one both the docstring and the reduced-k-set refusal tell the caller to use -- raises a TypeError unless conv_thr is passed by hand.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `workflows-magnetic`
- mechanism: stm.py:283 declares `conv_thr: float | None = None` where the sibling run_stm declares `conv_thr: float = 1.0e-6` at :92. Nothing between the signature and the call coerces it: stm.py:340-355 is `if kpoints is None and grid is not None: kpoints = whole_grid(...)`, then the two-branch solve, and the else branch at :347 passes it as the sixth positional argument, `fixed_density_states(system, pseudos, result.density, kpoints, nbnd, conv_thr, k_batch, ...)`. In nscf.py:250 that value is used as `ethr = max(ETHR_MIN, 0.1 * min(1.0e-2, conv_thr / max(1.0, calculation.nelec)))`, which is None / float. The refusal at stm.py:608-614 tells the user "Pass grid= to re-solve on the whole grid", and the Args entry at :332-339 advertises the same, so the crash sits on the recommended escape.
- reached by: run_sts(system, pseudos, result, energies=..., height=..., grid=(8, 8, 1)) with no conv_thr, or Calculator.get_sts(energies, grid=...) on a calculator whose defaults carry no conv_thr (conv_thr is in SHARED_OPTIONS, so a Calculator.from_file that picked it out of &electrons happens to paper over it). Without grid= the branch is not taken and nothing fails.
- worth: A crash, not a wrong number: TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for /: 'NoneType' and 'float', raised deep inside fixed_density_states after the k-set has been built. It is unexercised because every test that passes grid= also passes conv_thr -- tests/regression/test_ultracell_sts.py:528 and :560-561 are the only two and both do.
- how to confirm: Read stm.py:283 and :347 against nscf.py:250, or grep conv_thr in defumat/workflows/stm.py: it prints 92 (run_stm, 1.0e-6), 180, 283 (run_sts, None) and 347 and nothing in between that fills it in. A one-line reproduction is any run_sts call with grid= and no conv_thr.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `run_sts` in all four; the only hits are PERFORMANCE/OPEN.md:3524-3541, which compare run_sts' cost against pp.x run once per bias and say nothing about its signature.

### defumat/workflows/conductivity.py:143 -- `run_conductivity`

**`run_conductivity` accepts `tau` and forwards it to the fixed-density run, then rebuilds the potential without it, so a potential-only meta-GGA run crashes with a `ValueError` about a missing kinetic energy density *after* it has diagonalised three times the occupied bands at every k-point.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `workflows-core`
- mechanism: `tau` reaches `fixed_density_states` at conductivity.py:123, where nscf.py:212-224 accepts it and nscf.py:239-244 builds the band potential as `calculation.potential(density, field_scale, field, tau=tau)`. Back in `run_conductivity`, conductivity.py:143-147 calls `calculation.potential(jnp.asarray(density), field_scale, field)` with the `tau` keyword omitted; `Calculation.potential` (driver.py:3080-3099) passes that `None` straight into `v_of_rho`, whose meta branch (scf/potential.py:723-730) raises `ValueError` whenever `functional.is_meta and tau is None`. Nothing upstream refuses the regime: `require_a_conductivity_regime` (response/conductivity.py:389-416) has exactly two refusals, a spiral and a reduced k-set, and neither mentions a meta-GGA. The path is reachable through the front door, because `Calculator._call_options` (calculator.py:2037-2043) injects `tau` from the SCF result precisely because `run_conductivity` has a parameter of that name, so `calc.get_optical_conductivity()` on a `tb09` or `bj06` ground state takes this route. The comment at conductivity.py:135-142 makes the same argument for `field`/`field_scale` -- "accepting an argument and dropping it half way" -- and `tau` is the one that was not carried. `calculation.onecenter(becsum)` on the next line fails the same way for a PAW meta-GGA, since `onecenter` (driver.py:3131-3142) refuses a meta functional with `meta_c = None`, which nscf.py:246 supplies and this does not.
- reached by: `input_dft = 'tb09'` or `'bj06'` (or any functional for which `functional.is_meta` is true) plus a call to `run_conductivity` or `Calculator.get_optical_conductivity`. Independent of spin, dataset and k-set.
- worth: No wrong number -- the run dies -- but it dies after the expensive half: the fixed-density solve at `nbnd + 1` bands with `nbnd = 3 nelec / degeneracy` over the whole k-set, which on any production cell is the bulk of the wall clock. The error message names the functional and not the caller, so it reads as a refusal of the functional rather than as a dropped keyword.
- how to confirm: Read scf/potential.py:721-730 for the raise, driver.py:3080-3099 for the pass-through, and conductivity.py:121-148 for the asymmetry between the two `potential` calls; then `grep -n 'is_meta' defumat/response/conductivity.py` to confirm no earlier refusal. A run is not needed -- the three lines settle it.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `run_conductivity`: OPEN.md:1315 is H3, the three Calculations, and AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:137 and :171 are the reduced-k-set guard and the gamma-storage velocity, none of which touch `tau`)

### defumat/scf/potential.py:174 -- `scf_accuracy_terms`

**The `rho_ddot` transcription has no `tauk_ddot` term, so under a meta-GGA the kinetic energy density's residual is invisible to `conv_thr` and to the `ethr` schedule, where `pw.x` puts it in `dr2` at the same weight it gives the magnetization.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `scf-body`
- mechanism: `scf_accuracy_terms:188-207` transforms the density residual and returns the charge half (the Hartree energy, `1/|G|^2`) plus the magnetization half (the G-independent `e2 fpi / tpi^2`), and nothing else. QE's `rho_ddot` (`PW/src/scf_mod.f90`) ends with `IF (xclib_dft_is('meta')) rho_ddot = rho_ddot + tauk_ddot(rho1, rho2, gf)`, and `tauk_ddot` is `fac * sum_G |tau(G)|^2 * omega/2` with the same `fac = e2*fpi/tpi**2`, G = 0 included, exactly the form of the magnetization half. The driver does not supply it either: `accuracy_of` (driver.py:4675) adds `ns_ddot` and states that `tau` is deliberately left out because the mixing loop converges on the density alone, and the mixing loop's `_mix` (driver.py:430) takes `rho`, `becsum` and `ns` and no `tau`. The premise for leaving it out is a claim about QE that does not hold: `mix_type` carries `kin_g` (scf_mod.f90:73), `assign_scf_to_mix_type` copies it in (:323), `mix_type_AXPY` and `mix_type_SCAL` act on it (:445, :516) and `assign_mix_to_scf_type` rebuilds `kin_r` from the mixed `kin_g` (:371-373), so `pw.x` both mixes `tau` and converges on it. Here `tau` is instead recomputed from the output states every iteration (driver.py:5519) and feeds `v_x` directly, since a potential-only meta-GGA reads `tau` into the potential rather than through an energy.
- reached by: `input_dft = 'tb09'` or `'bj06'`, collinear or noncollinear, at any `conv_thr`. It cannot act for a non-meta functional, where `tau` does not exist.
- worth: A run stops, and schedules its eigensolver, on a number that omits the one ingredient of the potential that is not a function of the density. The wrong number is the reported gap or eigenvalues at a `tau` still moving, in the regime the functional exists for, and it also runs `ethr` looser than `pw.x` would from the same state. The size is unmeasured; the related audit item measured a 65 per cent `tau` inconsistency surviving one iteration on `h-fcc-magnon.in` under `tb09`, which is the scale the missing term is meant to bound.
- how to confirm: Run `tb09` on the two-atom silicon cell to `conv_thr = 1e-8`, and at each iteration compute `e2*fpi/tpi**2 * 0.5 * omega * sum |r_to_g(tau_out - tau_in)|^2` beside the reported `accuracy`. If that term is at or above `accuracy` at the iteration the run stops, the stopping test is not bounding `tau`. The QE side is one `pw.x` run of the same input with `input_dft='tb09'`, comparing its printed `estimated scf accuracy` trajectory against this one's.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md -- grepped for `tauk`, `kin_g`, `scf_accuracy`, `rho_ddot` and `not mixed`. It is the same shape as OPEN.md Y2 (`becsum` absent from `accuracy`) one ingredient over, and unlike Y2 the omission is not QE's: QE writes and comments out `paw_ddot`, but it writes and *calls* `tauk_ddot`. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:1045 states in passing that `tau` is not mixed, as a bound on a different defect, without noticing that `pw.x` mixes it.

### defumat/calculator.py:1660 -- `Calculator.get_anisotropy (and get_torque, get_first_order_soc, get_force_theorem)`

**The calculator's own `conv_thr` -- read straight out of the input file's `&electrons` -- silently overrides the force theorem's deliberately four-orders-tighter 1e-10 default, on a quantity that is a difference of band-energy sums in the fifth decimal of an eV.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `continuation-facade`
- mechanism: `electrons_defaults` (calculator.py:260-288) maps `&electrons conv_thr` onto `SHARED_OPTIONS`' `conv_thr`, so `Calculator.from_file("sr.in")` on any input that states one -- pw.x's own 1e-6 is the usual value -- lands it in `self.defaults`. `get_anisotropy` then calls `self._defaults_for(run_anisotropy, options)` with **no** `exclude`, and `_defaults_for` (calculator.py:2012-2020) returns `{**shared, **options}`, so the calculator's value wins over the callee's default for any name the signature carries. `run_anisotropy` (anisotropy.py:674), `run_force_theorem` (:467), `frozen_expectation` (:841) and `run_torque` (:976) all declare `conv_thr: float = 1.0e-10`, and `run_force_theorem`'s docstring (anisotropy.py:491-493) states why: "the whole answer is a difference of band-energy sums in the fifth decimal of an eV, and QE's own example sets diago_thr_init = 1.d-14 for the same reason". So the two legs the anisotropy differences are each diagonalised at a threshold four orders looser than the number the workflow chose, and the difference is taken between them. The facade already knows this collision in two other places and guards it in both -- `get_relaxed_anisotropy` passes `_shared_scf_options(withheld=("conv_thr",))` (calculator.py:1700) and `get_magnetoelectric_tensor` passes `exclude=frozenset({"conv_thr"})` (:1617-1623) with a comment measuring the same substitution at "a factor of 300 in the spin-orbit null" -- and these four methods have neither. A second face rides on the same call: `nbnd` is in `SHARED_OPTIONS` too, so `Calculator.from_file("sr.in", nbnd=N).get_anisotropy(soc)` forwards the *collinear* leg's band count into the *spinor* one, where `nscf.py:177`'s `nbnd = nbnd or system.nbnd or default_nbnd(...)` lets it win over the spinor input's own -- and a spinor band holds one electron where a collinear one holds two.
- reached by: Any `Calculator.from_file` whose input states `conv_thr` in `&electrons`, or any calculator constructed with one, then `get_anisotropy` / `get_torque` / `get_first_order_soc` / `get_force_theorem`. Needs nspin = 2 on the scalar-relativistic leg and a fully-relativistic noncollinear second leg, which is the only way these methods run at all.
- worth: The magnetocrystalline anisotropy is tenths of a meV to a few meV; the diagonalisation accuracy goes from 1e-10 Ry to whatever the input said, typically 1e-6 Ry = 13.6 micro-eV per band, summed over nk times nbnd occupied bands and then differenced between two directions. It stops mattering for an input that names no `conv_thr` at all, where the callee's 1e-10 survives, and for `get_relaxed_anisotropy`, which withholds it.
- how to confirm: Read `_defaults_for` at calculator.py:2012 beside `get_anisotropy` at :1660 and `run_force_theorem`'s signature at anisotropy.py:467; that is the whole chain. For the size: run one anisotropy through a calculator built from an input stating `conv_thr = 1e-6` and the same one through `Calculator(system, pseudos)` with no default, and difference the two MAE values -- the workflow's own `.drifts` will not see it, because both legs move together.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:379 (`_shared_scf_options` with `withheld`), which fixed exactly this collision for the two workflows that take their options through a `**kwargs` -- `get_spiral_scan` and `get_relaxed_anisotropy` -- and did not reach the four that take theirs by named parameter. `get_anisotropy`, `get_torque`, `get_force_theorem` and `withheld` do not otherwise appear in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/scf/continuation.py:797 -- `_convert_ns (target == 4 branch), reached from promote_ns`

**A collinear-to-spinor continuation rotates the density and `becsum` onto the target's own axis and writes the Hubbard occupation matrix along `z` regardless, so a DFT+U run starts with its correlated shell polarised on a different axis from its density -- the exact state `initial_ns_noncollinear` says "converges with the shell polarised along the wrong axis and reports success".**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `continuation-facade`
- mechanism: `_transfer` (continuation.py:333-352) resolves `direction = _common_direction(calculation)` for a `2 -> 4` carry, the unit vector the target's `angle1`/`angle2` or `STARTING_MOMENTS` name. `_SpinTransfer.apply` (:213-217) uses it: `moment = _axis(self.direction, moment.ndim) * moment[2]`. `promote_density` and `promote_becsum` both go through `apply`, so both are rotated. `promote_ns` does not -- it calls `_convert_ns(ns, source, target, calculation)` (:779), whose signature has no `transfer` at all, and whose `target == 4` branch at :797 is `jnp.stack([up, zero, zero, down])`: the two collinear channels dropped into the two diagonal spin blocks with the off-diagonal pair exactly zero, which is a moment along `z` and no transverse component. `initial_ns_noncollinear` (hubbard/occupations.py:381-404) -- what a *fresh* run of the same target builds -- takes `angle1`, `angle2` and `per_atom` precisely so the starting matrix has off-diagonal spin blocks from iteration 1, and its docstring states the consequence of not doing so: "nothing in the SCF turns a moment, so a spinor DFT+U run started with a moment along z on a species whose angle1 points elsewhere converges with the shell polarised along the wrong axis and reports success". So the continuation is the odd path out. `qe_hubbard_potential` is `alpha + U/2 delta - U n^s`, order 0.3 Ry for a typical Ni `U`, and `mixing_fixed_ns` freezes `ns_state` for as many iterations as it is set to (driver.py:5439), so the mismatch is not confined to one Hamiltonian. The same gap has a second, noisier face going the other way: for `4 -> 2` with `project` set, `_collinear_axis` accepts a moment collinear along any axis and rotates the density onto it, while `_convert_ns`'s `source == 4` branch at :800-807 measures `max|ns[1]| + max|ns[2]|` in the *laboratory* frame and raises `NotImplementedError` above 1e-8 -- so a DFT+U demotion from a state collinear along `x` is refused where the identical non-Hubbard demotion succeeds.
- reached by: `nspin = 2` to noncollinear with `lda_plus_u`, `magnetization='auto'` or `'carry'`, and a target whose moments are not along `z` -- `Calculator.with_spin(4)` on a system with `angle1`/`angle2` set, or `run_scf(starting_from=result)` into such a system. `Calculator.with_moments` escapes it, because its default is `'seed'`, which returns `None` and lets `init_ns` rebuild. The reverse face needs `nspin = 4` to 2 with a moment collinear off `z`.
- worth: A Hubbard potential split by order `U` (0.3 Ry for Ni) on the wrong spin axis in the first Hamiltonian, with no transverse component to turn it, on a functional whose purpose is choosing among metastable solutions. The plausible wrong number is a converged spinor DFT+U state polarised along `z` while the input asked for another direction, reported as success -- which is fatal for an anisotropy, where the whole quantity is the difference between directions. It stops mattering for a target whose moments are already on `z`, where `_common_direction` returns `None`.
- how to confirm: Read `_convert_ns`'s signature at continuation.py:779 beside `promote_density`/`promote_becsum` at :595-604, which both take `transfer`; then `_SpinTransfer.apply` at :213 against the `target == 4` branch at :797. To measure it: continue a converged collinear DFT+U cell into a noncollinear system with `angle1 = 90`, and compare `max|ns[1]| + max|ns[2]|` of the state handed to iteration 1 against `Calculation.starting_ns()` for the same system -- the first is exactly zero and the second is not.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:177-183 (wrong-answer item, promote_ns not given the `_SpinTransfer`), which was closed by handing `promote_ns` the transfer for the *mode* decision -- carry / seed / none -- and split "the reshape between regimes and the decision about the magnetization" into separate steps. The rotation is the third thing and stayed in the reshape half. `_convert_ns` appears in none of OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/hamiltonian/noncollinear.py:414 -- `SpinorHamiltonian.diagonal`

**The spinor Hamiltonian's preconditioner diagonal omits the Hubbard term that the collinear twin includes, so a noncollinear DFT+U run preconditions Davidson with a Hamiltonian it is not solving.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `solver-batching-ham`
- mechanism: `Hamiltonian.diagonal` (operator.py:324-333) builds `kinetic + mean(V)`, then adds `self.hubbard.diagonal(ik)` at :325-326 when a Hubbard term is present, then the nonlocal diagonal. `SpinorHamiltonian.diagonal` (noncollinear.py:414-434) builds `kinetic + mean(potential[0])` and the diagonal spin blocks of `deeq`, and never touches `self.hubbard` -- although the class declares the field at :124 with a docstring saying the four spin blocks are the quadrants of one `(nwfcU, nwfcU)` matrix, `apply` adds it at :255-259, and `matrix` adds it at :512-513. `HubbardTerm.diagonal` (hubbard/operator.py:82) exists and works on any `ndim`, since it contracts `wfcU[ik]` over its plane-wave axis, so the spinor case needs no new code. The consequence runs through `davidson_eigensolver`: `diagonal = hamiltonian.diagonal(ik)` at davidson.py:457 feeds `_precondition` at :357-371, where `x = H_ii - e S_ii`, so on a correlated shell the denominator is short by `v_ns` -- which on a transition-metal oxide is several eV, the largest single entry on that shell's diagonal -- and the correction vector is scaled wrong exactly on the plane waves the Hubbard manifold lives on. `shift` at :480 is also built from this diagonal, so it is likewise short.
- reached by: `noncolin = .true.` (or `lspinorb`) together with a `HUBBARD` card: a spinor DFT+U run, which is P62's own advertised regime. Collinear and non-Hubbard runs are untouched.
- worth: Not a wrong eigenvalue -- the preconditioner cannot change the fixed point, only the path to it -- but a wrong number of Davidson steps, and the trap it sits next to is that a badly preconditioned root can still trip `abs(e - e_prev) < ethr` early on a loose SCF `ethr`, which is the same weak-proxy argument `RESIDUAL_THRESHOLD`'s docstring makes at davidson.py:192-203. Cost scales with the Davidson step count, i.e. with `nk nbnd npwx` per extra step.
- how to confirm: Read hubbard/operator.py:82-89 against noncollinear.py:414-434 and operator.py:316-333 -- the asymmetry is on the page. To price it, run a spinor DFT+U cell (the P62 reference) with `diagonalize(..., return_steps=True)` as written and again with `self.hubbard.diagonal(ik)` added to each block, and compare the per-k step counts; the converged total must not move.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `diagonal`, `hubbard`, `noncollinear.py`, `usnldiag`, `preconditioner` in all four documents; OPEN.md M5 names `noncollinear.py:387` and `:401` (the two einsum contractions inside this same routine and `overlap_diagonal`) but is about how the `nkb x nkb` contraction is associated, not about a missing term.

### defumat/basis/gvectors.py:117 -- `modulus`

**At the one plane wave where k + G = 0 exactly, the tangent of the projector's radial-times-angular product is forced to zero, so dvkb/dk on that row is zero for every l = 1 channel where the true derivative is finite.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `basis-radial`
- mechanism: `_angular_part` (projectors.py:329-336) builds the projector column as `real_spherical_harmonics(kg, lmax)` times `projector_form_factors(p, modulus(kg))`. Both factors guard the origin by zeroing, not by one-sided continuation: `modulus` returns `jnp.where(norm2 > _TINY, sqrt(...), 0.0)`, whose tangent at the false branch is 0, and `real_spherical_harmonics` (harmonics.py:259-261 and :297-298) sets `cost`, `u`, `v` through `jnp.where(tiny, ...)` with the same effect. Separately each guard is right: the primal is correct because f_l(0) = 0 kills the finite Y_lm the harmonics return at the zero vector. But the *product* is what carries the derivative. For l = 1, f_1(q) ~ c q and Y_1m(qhat) = sqrt(3/4pi) q_alpha/q, so the product is sqrt(3/4pi) c q_alpha, a linear function of the vector q with derivative sqrt(3/4pi) c, not zero. The code computes d(Y*f) = Y*df + dY*f with df = f'(q) * dq/dk = f'(0) * 0 and dY = 0, so it returns 0. l = 0 is genuinely zero (f_0 is even in q) and l >= 2 is genuinely zero (the product goes as q^l), so l = 1 is the only affected channel -- and it is present in essentially every dataset. This is reached: `VelocityOperator.projectors`/`_operator` (velocity.py:224-228, :267-273) take `jax.jvp` of `calculation.at_kcart(kcart)`, which rebuilds `build_projector_core` with a traced kcart straight through `_angular_part`. The stress is not affected: under a strain at Gamma both k and G = 0 stay exactly zero, so the true derivative of that row is zero too.
- reached by: Any k-point that sits on the reciprocal lattice, i.e. k + G = 0 for one member of its sphere -- Gamma on a Gamma-centred mesh, Gamma on a band path, and k -/+ q/2 = 0 in a spiral. Needs a dataset with an l = 1 projector, which is almost all of them. The `_TINY` threshold is on |k+G|^2 = 1e-8, so it is the exact-zero row and not a neighbourhood.
- worth: One row of the (npwx, nkb) dvkb/dk, but it is not suppressed by 1/npwx: it enters <psi_n|dH/dk|psi_m> weighted by psi(G = 0), which is the largest single coefficient of a valence state. Everything built on the velocity operator at Gamma inherits it -- band velocities, effective masses, the Gamma contribution to the optical conductivity, chi_0's head, the Drude weight, SHG. On a mesh the damage is diluted by one k-point in nk; on a Gamma-only or effective-mass-at-Gamma quantity it is the whole answer. Scales with nothing but the number of l = 1 channels, natom * 3.
- how to confirm: Compare `VelocityOperator.matrix_elements` at Gamma against the same quantity from a finite difference of H(k) taken with the sphere frozen and k displaced off zero, on a cell with p projectors (silicon). Or, cheaper and structural: jvp the scalar `modulus(kg)[0] * f_1` chain at k = 0 and check it against the analytic sqrt(3/4pi) * f_1'(0). The fix is the one the module docstring already reaches for elsewhere -- guard by continuing from the interior rather than by a where that halves or kills the tangent, which is CLAUDE.md's clamp-tangent trap one step out.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped 'modulus', '_angular_part', 'real_spherical_harmonics', 'kg_norm' across all four; the only 'modulus' hit is OPEN.md:545-550, which is xc/functional.safe_modulus on |m|, a different site)

### defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py:239 -- `AugmentationCharge.block_matrix`

**`block_matrix` dereferences its blocks unconditionally, so the `None` blocks its own PAW caller is written to pass raise `AttributeError` and any cell mixing a PAW dataset with a norm-conserving one dies in the first SCF iteration.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
