- mechanism: `block_matrix` opens with `matrix = jnp.zeros((self.nkb, self.nkb), dtype=blocks[0].dtype)` (:239) and then reads `block.shape[-1]` (:243) for every species that has atoms. Neither line tolerates `None`. The caller at `defumat/scf/driver.py:3146-3151` builds its argument as `tuple(None if b is None else b[spin] for b in blocks)`, so it expects `None` entries to be handled downstream, and they are produced: `Calculation._species_channels` (driver.py:2381-2385) returns `None` for a species that is not ultrasoft, `scf/density.py:338-341` propagates that to `becsum[t] = None`, and `PawCorrections.energy_and_coefficients` (paw/onecenter.py:225-228) appends `None if values is None else jnp.zeros_like(values)`. So in a run where `self.paw is not None` and at least one species is norm-conserving, `blocks` carries a `None` at that species' index and `block_matrix` is the first thing to touch it. The two sibling consumers are guarded and this one is not: `AugmentationCharge.charge` (:169) skips on `q.shape[0] == 0` before indexing `becsum[t]`, and `TabulatedAugmentation.charge` (:637) skips on `self.tables[t] is None`.
- reached by: an SCF with a PAW pseudopotential on one species and a norm-conserving one on another, at any `nspin`; both `blocks[0].dtype` (norm-conserving species first) and `block.shape[-1]` (norm-conserving species anywhere with atoms) reach it, so the species order does not save it
- worth: no number, a crash: `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'dtype'` (or `'shape'`) raised from inside `Calculation.onecenter`, after the basis, the symmetry search and the first diagonalisation have been paid for. `pw.x` runs this combination with no complaint and nothing in `Calculation.__init__` refuses it, so what the user meets is a bare AttributeError rather than one of this module's named refusals.
- how to confirm: write a two-species input against the committed datasets -- `Si.pz-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF` on one site and `Si.pz-vbc.UPF` on the other, or O PAW with H `H.pz-vbc.UPF` -- and call `Calculator.get_scf()`. Reading alone settles it as far as the guard goes: `grep -n 'None' defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py` shows no `None` test anywhere in `block_matrix`, while `defumat/scf/driver.py:3148` constructs `None` entries explicitly.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. `block_matrix` is named once, in OPEN.md M1, which is about its eager per-atom scatters running outside `jit` and says nothing about `None` blocks; AUDIT-2026-09-18's tabulated-route item is about `forces/analytic.py` reading `augmentation.qgm`, a different consumer.

### defumat/system/builder.py:565 -- `System._respin_kpoints`

**Demoting a gamma-only run to `nspin = 1` returns the existing k-set untouched, and `for_spin` is one-way, so the weights stay halved and the unpolarized run integrates half the electrons.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `system-builder`
- mechanism: Line 565 is `return kpoints_for_spin(kpoints, nspin)` for a band path or a gamma-only set, and it returns the *same* `KPoints` object rather than rebuilding it. `for_spin` (`system/kpoints.py:567`) is `if int(nspin) not in (2, 4) or kpoints.spin_normalized: return kpoints`, so with `nspin = 1` it is the identity whatever the flag says: it can divide by `DEGSPIN` on the way into a polarized run and has no branch that multiplies back on the way out. The invariant every other route maintains is that an `nspin = 1` set sums to `DEGSPIN` and an `nspin = 2` or `4` set sums to one, and `_normalise` (`kpoints.py:625`) is what restores it -- it normalises to one and then multiplies by `DEGSPIN`, so the grid branch at line 545 and the explicit branch at line 607 both come out right because they pass through a constructor. The gamma and band-path branch does not, so a set built at `nspin = 2` (`weights = [1.0]`, `spin_normalized = True`) comes back from `with_spin(1)` still at `[1.0]` with the flag still True, where a freshly built unpolarized gamma set is `KPoints.gamma`'s `[DEGSPIN] = [2.0]`. The halved set is what the SCF then integrates with: AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:325 traced the same weights through the driver's gamma-storage substitution and found `_without_gamma_storage` carries them unchanged.
- reached by: `System.with_spin(1, ...)` or `Calculator.with_spin(1)` from `nspin = 2` or `4` on a run whose k-set is `K_POINTS gamma`. The band-path half of the same branch is harmless, because a path's weights are uniform and mean nothing. The demotion itself is an exercised path -- `tests/regression/test_continuation.py:195` and `tests/unit/test_continuation_machinery.py:577` both call `with_spin(1)` -- but both do it on automatic grids, which rebuild through `KPoints.automatic` and come out right, so nothing covers the gamma case.
- worth: A factor of two in the k-point weights, which is a factor of two in the electron count the density is built from. With `occupations = 'fixed'` the density integrates to `nelec / 2` against an ionic charge of `nelec`, so the Hartree and exchange-correlation terms are those of half a crystal and the total energy is wrong by Rydbergs rather than by milli-Rydbergs. With smearing the Fermi bisection is asked to reach `nelec` from half the weight and drives `ef` past the top of the band. It scales with nothing; it is one number, and the run reports success either way.
- how to confirm: Host-side, no SCF: take any `nspin = 2` system whose input carries `K_POINTS gamma`, call `back = system.with_spin(1, starting_magnetization=(0.0,))`, and read `float(back.kpoints.weights.sum())` and `back.kpoints.spin_normalized`. The sum is 1.0 where a freshly built `nspin = 1` gamma set gives 2.0, and the flag is still True. The same two reads on an automatic grid give 2.0 and False, which is the contrast that isolates the branch.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:325, which is the same one-way `spin_normalized` flag read in the other direction: that entry is about `_without_gamma_storage` dropping the flag so that `for_spin` halves a set twice, and it explicitly clears `_respin_kpoints` on the grounds that it "rebuilds through `KPoints.from_crystal`" -- which is true of the two branches it examined and not of the early return at line 565. `for_spin`, `spin_normalized` and `gamma_only` were each grepped across the four documents; the demotion direction appears in none of them.

### defumat/system/symmetry.py:726 -- `_crystallographic_translation`

**The fractional-translation filter uses a tolerance of 1e-6 where QE's `sgam_at` uses `eps2 = 1e-5`, so a hexagonal or trigonal cell whose positions are written with six-decimal thirds loses every non-symmorphic operation that `pw.x` keeps.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `symmetry-kpoints`
- mechanism: `_crystallographic_translation` accepts a component only when `abs(1.0/abs(residue) - order) <= _TOLERANCE` with `_TOLERANCE = 1e-6` (line 52). QE's counterpart is `symm_base.f90:557-569`: `ftaux(i) = ABS(1/ft_(i) - NINT(1/ft_(i)))` and `IF (ANY(ftaux(:) > eps2)) CYCLE` with `eps2 = 1.0d-5` (symm_base.f90:23). For a translation component written as 0.333333, `1/0.333333 = 3.000003`, so the residue from the integer 3 is 3.0e-6: inside QE's 1e-5 and three times outside this code's 1e-6. `find_symmetries` (line 280) calls the filter before `_maps_structure`, so the candidate is discarded, and since the only other candidate for a sublattice-swapping operation is the zero translation (which fails the structure match), the whole operation is dropped. Two more thresholds in the same family are 10x tighter than QE's: line 723 treats a component below 1e-6 as zero where QE uses `ABS(ft_(i)) > eps2`, and `_maps_structure` (line 743) matches positions at 1e-6 where `eqvect` uses `accep = 1.0d-5` (symm_base.f90:57) and `find_sym` only ever *halves* it (1e-5, 5e-6, 2.5e-6) before giving up and setting `nsym = 1`, so QE never reaches 1e-6 at all. Compounding it, `find_sym` checks `is_group(nsym)` after every pass and disables symmetry entirely if the set does not close, and nothing here checks closure, so a group that comes out short is never reported.
- reached by: Any cell whose atomic positions are written with about six decimal places and whose space group is non-symmorphic with a 1/3 or 2/3 translation. `tests/data/qe/co-hcp-anisotropy-sr.in` and `co-hcp-anisotropy-soc.in` are exactly that: `ibrav = 4` hcp cobalt with `Co 0.333333 0.666667 0.500000`, driven by `tools/gpu/p59_check.py` for the magnetocrystalline anisotropy. `is_supercell` returns False there, so the fractional-translation branch is reached and the swapping operations are the ones that need `ft = (1/3, 2/3, 1/2)`.
- worth: The operations that exchange the two hcp sublattices are dropped, so `nsym` comes out below what `pw.x` prints, `fft_factors` loses its factor of 3 on the two hexagonal axes and the dense FFT grid is smaller than QE's, and the density is averaged over a proper subgroup while the k-wedge grows. This project has already priced the same class of divergence twice: 1.7e-4 Ry on five-layer graphite from an FFT grid that differed only through `fft_fact`, and 3.2e-6 Ry from a lattice point group that came out short. The k-wedge growing is a pure cost, not an error.
- how to confirm: Read-only first: no committed `reference.out.*` exists for either `co-hcp-anisotropy-*` input, and the four referenced inputs that contain `0.333333` (`bismuthene-*-bands`) carry it in the `K_POINTS crystal_b` card while their positions are twelve-decimal (`0.333333333333`, residue 3e-12), so `tests/regression/test_shapes_against_qe.py` has never exercised this and the divergence is unmeasured rather than disproved. To settle it: run `find_symmetries` on `co-hcp-anisotropy-sr.in` and compare `nsym` and `fft_factors` against a `pw.x` run of the same file (its `N Sym. Ops.` and `FFT dimensions` lines); the one-line check is that `_crystallographic_translation((0.333333, 0.666667, 0.5))` returns False and returns True once `_TOLERANCE` is replaced by 1e-5.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `find_symmetries`, `lattice_point_group`, `atom_mapping`; the AUDIT-2026-09-18 and OPEN.md hits on `find_symmetries` are P28b's fixed-window lattice search and OPEN.md H6's duplicate symmetry search, neither of which is a tolerance)

### defumat/xc/functional.py:487 -- `Functional.spin_energy_density`

**The saturated-point mask in `spin_energy_density` is applied one derivative order too low, so it zeroes the *first* derivative of `e_xc` where it was meant to zero only the second, dropping the `rho de_xc/drho` term from every LSDA and noncollinear energy gradient at fully polarized points.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `xc-paw`
- mechanism: `spin_potential` (:518) returns `jax.grad(total)` and masks it as `jnp.where(saturated, jax.grad(total)(stop_gradient(channels)), jax.grad(total)(regular))`: the quantity returned is already the first derivative, so the `stop_gradient` kills *its* derivative, which is the second, exactly as `dmxc_lsda` defines. `spin_energy_density` (:485-487) copies that pattern verbatim -- `jnp.where(saturated, raw(stop_gradient(rho)), raw(regular))` -- but the quantity returned there is the *value*, so the `stop_gradient` kills the first derivative instead. The method's own docstring at :471-473 claims the opposite ("The value and the first derivative are exactly the expression below; only the second derivative is masked"), and `MEMORY-AUDIT.md` quotes that claim as the reason not to fuse the two calls. `_fully_polarized` (:118-121) is `|rho_up - rho_dw| >= |rho_up + rho_dw|`, i.e. one channel at or below zero, which is reached exactly rather than approached. The consumers are `scf/potential.py:257` (nspin=2 etxc) and `:310` (nspin=4 etxc) and `paw/onecenter.py:457,460`, so wherever a force or a response reaches the density through the geometry -- the NLCC and augmentation terms, and PAW's `becsum -> rho_lm` path -- the `rho de_xc/drho` half of `d(etxc)/drho` is silently zero on the saturated set. Masking only the second derivative needs a `custom_jvp` whose tangent is `f'(stop_gradient(x)) . dx`; a bare `where`/`stop_gradient` on the value cannot express it.
- reached by: nspin = 2 or nspin = 4, at points where one channel density is zero or negative: a fully polarized system (`tot_magnetization` saturated, an H atom, `rho_down = 0` identically), and any noncollinear point where `local_spin_frame` clamped `|m|` to `|n|`. Needs a dataset whose energy reaches the density through the geometry, so a core charge, an augmentation charge or PAW; a bare norm-conserving force at frozen wavefunctions never touches it.
- worth: A force or stress term that is short by `sum_r rho(r) de_xc/drho(r)` over the saturated set alone, with the total energy exactly right -- the project's own "the energy can be right while its derivative is wrong". The docstring's own 6.1e-5 Ry/bohr figure is the size on the validated LSDA cell: that measurement substituted the `(v_sigma - e)/n` identity for this expression and saw the force move, which is what a zero tangent on one side predicts, and it was read as a difference of evaluation points rather than as a missing tangent. On a saturated cell with a large core charge the affected set is the whole cell rather than a fringe.
- how to confirm: Direct and needs no SCF: `jax.grad(lambda r: jnp.sum(f.spin_energy_density(r)))` at `rho = jnp.array([[1.0],[0.0]])` against a central finite difference of `spin_energy_density` in each channel -- the autodiff returns zero in both channels where the finite difference is finite and equal to `(v_sigma - e)/n`. Then rebuild the line as a `jax.custom_jvp` carrying `raw'(stop_gradient(rho)) . drho` and re-run the LSDA force that the 6.1e-5 Ry/bohr note was measured on, plus the triplet-O2 Born charge the mask was introduced for, to check the NaN stays away.
- against the record: Extends MEMORY-AUDIT.md's "Withdrawn sub-item" (~:422-427), which cites this docstring's claim that "the potential's derivative is masked at a fully polarized point and the energy density's is not" as grounds for withdrawing a fusion proposal; the source says the energy density's first derivative is masked too, so the premise of that withdrawal is false. The duplicate *evaluation* is recorded at MEMORY-AUDIT.md:398; the correctness claim is not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/paw/gradient.py:116 -- `onecenter_gradient_correction`

**The unpolarized one-centre GGA takes `jnp.abs` of the on-site density *before* the radial gradient, where `PAW_gcxc_potential` takes the gradient of the signed density and applies `ABS` only to the argument handed to the functional.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `xc-paw`
- mechanism: QE's order is fixed and both halves are visible: `paw_onecenter.f90:762` calls `PAW_gradient(i, rhoout_lm, rho_rad, rho_core, grad)`, whose own `:1099` builds `aux = rho_rad*rm2 + rho_core*fact` with no `ABS` and radial-differentiates that; only afterwards, at `:780-781`, does it build `rho_full` and write `IF (nspin_mag==1) rho_full(ixk,1) = ABS(rho_full(ixk,1))`, and `rho_full` is what goes into `xc_gcx`. defumat writes `density = jnp.abs(rho_rad[0] / r2 + core)` at :116 and then passes that same `density` to `_gradient` at :118, whose radial component is `radial_derivative(density, paw.r)` (:260). So the radial component of `grad` is `d|rho|/dr = sign(rho) drho/dr`, not `drho/dr`. Because `sigma = sum(grad*grad)` is a sum of squares, the sign flip is invisible in `sigma` and therefore in `v1`, `v2` and the gradient-correction *energy*; it survives only in `h = v2 grad r2` (:127), whose radial component enters the potential through `radial_derivative(h_lm[0,:nlm])` in `_divergence` (:301). The comment at :114-115 shows the branch was identified correctly and the ordering was not. Separately, `jnp.abs` at a zero crossing puts a kink into the function a PAW force and stress differentiate, which is the project's first recurring trap.
- reached by: A PAW dataset with a gradient-corrected functional at nspin = 1, at any (direction, radius) of the quadrature where `rho_rad[0]/r^2 + core` is negative. Most likely on the pseudo sphere, where the augmentation charge `Q_ij` can be negative and the multipole-resolved density along a given direction can undershoot at large r; the all-electron sphere is dominated by a large positive core. Both codes bother to take the absolute value in this branch, which is the argument that the set is not empty.
- worth: Only `ddd` moves, so the SCF fixed point moves: eigenvalues shift at first order and the total energy at second order, meaning a converged-total comparison against `pw.x` is exactly the check that cannot see it. The energy at a *given* density is bit-identical between the two codes, so this hides behind every PAW-PBE energy agreement already recorded. Scales with the number of quadrature directions times the mesh inside the sphere, per PAW atom; it stops mattering entirely on a species whose on-site density never goes negative.
- how to confirm: Read it rather than run it: print `min(rho_rad[0]/r2 + core)` over the quadrature on the PS pass of a PAW PBE species (`si4-paw`, `si10-paw-pbe`) and count the negative entries. If any exist, move the `jnp.abs` from :116 to the argument of `gradient_potentials`/`gradient_energy` at :121-122, leaving `_gradient` the signed density, and compare `ddd` and the converged eigenvalues before and after; `sigma`, `v1`, `v2` and `e_gradient` must be unchanged to the last bit, which is itself the check that the repair is confined.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `onecenter_gradient_correction`, `PAW_gradient`, `gcxc`, `paw/gradient`, `radial_derivative`). OPEN.md's H1 note does name `paw/gradient.py` as carrying the duplicate energy-plus-potential pair three times over, but that is the perf item at :121-122 and says nothing about the `abs`; OPEN.md A5 touches `paw/gradient.py:191`, which is `safe_modulus` in the noncollinear branch.

### defumat/response/conductivity.py:854 -- `_drude`

**The Drude term's Fermi-surface delta is evaluated at the mirrored argument `(e - E_F)/degauss` where every other `w0gauss` call site in the package uses `(E_F - e)/degauss`, which silently changes the plasma frequency for a cold-smeared run.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `sum-over-states`
- mechanism: `w0gauss`'s own docstring (scf/occupations.py:611-616) fixes the convention as `x = (E - e)/degauss` and says in as many words that 'a sign flip here is invisible for a Gaussian and wrong for everything else'; `nesting.py:247` writes `(fermi_energy - eigenvalues)/degauss` with a comment repeating it, and `_count` (occupations.py:255) and `_entropy` (:542) both use `(ef - eigenvalues)`. Line 854 writes `(energies - fermi_energy)`. Because `w0gauss` is built as `jvp(wgauss)`, the delta it returns is the derivative of the occupation function the SCF actually converged with, and mirroring its argument breaks that identity. Gaussian, Methfessel-Paxton of every order and Fermi-Dirac are all even in x, so those are unaffected; cold smearing (`ngauss = -1`, `smearing = 'mv'`/`'cold'`) is not -- `wgauss` at occupations.py:158-161 is built on `xp = x - 1/sqrt(2)`, so its derivative is `exp(-xp^2)[1/sqrt(pi) - 2 xp/sqrt(2 pi)]`, which is 0.684 at x = 0, 0.025 at x = -1.414 and exactly 0 at x = +1.414, and goes negative beyond. Under the flip the whole asymmetric tail, including its negative lobe, lands on the wrong side of the Fermi level. The line is a literal transcription of `dielectric.f90`'s `x=(ei-efermi)/swidth`, which is harmless in Elk because Elk's `sdelta` types are all even; this package's are not.
- reached by: `occupations = 'smearing'` with `smearing = 'mv'`, `'cold'` or `'m-v'` (QE's usual choice for a metal), `degauss > 0`, `intraband = True` and `method = 'frequency'`. Gaussian, MP and Fermi-Dirac runs are unaffected, which is why the committed fcc-nickel cases would not show it.
- worth: A wrong plasma frequency tensor and therefore a wrong Drude conductivity at every frequency: `OpticalConductivity.plasma`, `.intraband` and the low-frequency part of `.sigma` and `.dielectric`. The error is a redistribution of the delta's weight across `degauss`, so it is of order the fraction of the Fermi-surface weight sitting more than one smearing width from E_F -- a few per cent to tens of per cent on a real band structure, not a factor. It is smooth in frequency and survives every symmetry check, since the tensor stays symmetric and the antisymmetric Hall part does not see it at all.
- how to confirm: Read it: compare conductivity.py:854 against nesting.py:247 and occupations.py:255. Then, on any metal, run `optical_conductivity` twice with `smearing = 'gaussian'` and `smearing = 'mv'` at the same `degauss`, and separately evaluate `w0gauss((E_F - e)/degauss, -1)` against `w0gauss((e - E_F)/degauss, -1)` on the run's own eigenvalues -- the two plasma tensors differ only under cold smearing, and the Gaussian pair is the control that says the harness is right.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- `w0gauss` appears in none of the four documents. OPEN.md B1 (lines ~660-740) is about `_drude`'s *tolerance* and the degenerate-pair guard, and its measured nickel numbers were taken at the default Gaussian; it says nothing about the delta's argument.

### defumat/tddft/spinchi0.py:225 -- `require_a_transverse_regime`

**The transverse spin susceptibility has no gamma-only refusal, so on `K_POINTS gamma` it forms its pair densities with the full-sphere `g_to_r` over a stored half sphere and returns a plausible, wrong magnon.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `hubbard-tddft-projwfc`
- mechanism: `_one_k_terms` (spinchi0.py:539-541) calls `g_to_r(psi * mask, index, grid)` on both channels and then `r_to_g(conj(field) * fields_dn, gather)`. Under gamma storage only one plane wave of each (G, -G) pair is stored, so a real-space field must be rebuilt by `g_to_r_gamma` and every plane-wave sum must be `2 Re(sum)` minus the `G = 0` term; neither is done here, so the real-space states lose their conjugate half and gain a spurious imaginary part, and every matrix element `M_G` is wrong. `require_a_transverse_regime` (lines 225-296) tests `is_paw`, `noncolin`, `nspin != 2`, `spiral`, `is_hubbard`, `magnetic_field`, `is_meta`, `source_free` and `is_reduced`, and never calls `refuse_gamma_storage`; a grep of the package shows the helper is called from exactly five places and none of them is in `tddft/spinchi0.py`. The sibling `tddft/chi0.py:304` refuses for precisely this reason, in these words: 'the pair densities are formed by transforming the states with the full-sphere g_to_r'. There is no upstream gate either -- `grep gamma defumat/workflows/magnons.py` returns nothing.
- reached by: `K_POINTS gamma`, `nspin = 2`, norm-conserving, `nosym` -- the combination that keeps gamma storage in force (ultrasoft, PAW, symmetry, a spinor and a spiral all substitute it away with a warning). That is the molecule-or-cluster-in-a-box cell, and q = 0 is the only wavevector such a run can carry, which is the Goldstone and Stoner case.
- worth: `X_0(G, G')` built from half-sphere transforms, so the Goldstone residual is O(1) rather than the per-cent-level double truncation it is meant to be, and the enhancement `lambda_max(X_0 F)` that decides where the magnon sits is wrong by roughly a factor of two. No error is raised. Independent of nk (there is one k-point); the error is in npwx, the stored half of the sphere.
- how to confirm: Read `spinchi0.py:225-296` against `chi0.py:304-311` and `basis/gvectors.py:206-236`, and grep `refuse_gamma_storage(` across the package -- five call sites, none in `tddft/spinchi0.py`. To measure: run the same cell twice, once as `K_POINTS gamma` and once as `K_POINTS automatic 1 1 1 0 0 0`, and compare `goldstone_residual`; the second is the same physics on the whole sphere.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `gamma`, `spinchi0`, `require_a_transverse`; the recorded spinchi0 items are A8, the band-loop stacking, and AUDIT-2026-09-18's magnons `for_spin` k-set defect, neither of which touches gamma storage)

### defumat/projwfc/angular_momentum.py:256 -- `_refuse_what_is_not_written`

**Site-resolved `<L>`, `<S>` and the Loewdin charge have no gamma-only refusal where the projection they are built from refuses it, so on `K_POINTS gamma` they come back small, smooth and wrong.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `hubbard-tddft-projwfc`
- mechanism: `angular_momenta` builds its orbitals with the same `build_atomic_projectors` that `projwfc.projections.atomic_projections` uses, and the overlap it Loewdin-orthogonalises with is `overlap = conj(phi) @ sphi.T` (projectors.py:355), a plain sum over the stored k + G list. Under gamma storage that sum is half the one it looks like, so `O` and therefore `O^{-1/2} S phi` are wrong, and `_site_density_matrix`'s `einsum('kgi,kbg->kbi', ...)` (line 307) is wrong by the same rule. `atomic_projections` calls `refuse_gamma_storage` at projections.py:250 with exactly this mechanism and the note that 'a Loewdin charge comes out at roughly a quarter of its value'. `_refuse_what_is_not_written` (lines 256-274) checks only `calculation.spiral` and a fully-relativistic augmented dataset; there is no gamma test here and none in `Calculator.get_angular_momenta`.
- reached by: `K_POINTS gamma`, norm-conserving, `nosym`, `nspin` 1 or 2 -- the regime that keeps gamma storage. A spinor run substitutes it away, so the spin-orbit case is safe by accident.
- worth: `AtomicMoments.charge` at roughly a quarter of the Loewdin charge and `<S>` wrong by the same overlap error, with the totals and the table printed as if they were right. `<L>` is quenched to zero without spin-orbit coupling anyway, so the module's own headline check ('a vanishing <L> on a collinear magnet') still passes and says nothing. Independent of nk (one k-point); the error is in the npwx sum.
- how to confirm: Read `angular_momentum.py:256-274` against `projections.py:248-255`, and grep `refuse_gamma_storage(` -- five call sites, none in `defumat/projwfc/angular_momentum.py`. To measure: `get_angular_momenta()` on a cell run as `K_POINTS gamma` and again as `K_POINTS automatic 1 1 1 0 0 0`, and compare `AtomicMoments.charge` against the number of valence electrons the dataset carries.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `angular_moment`, `gamma`; PERFORMANCE.md:3950 times `angular_momenta` and OPEN.md:1749 names a rotation test, neither about gamma storage)

### defumat/workflows/anisotropy.py:655 -- `cardinal_directions`

**cardinal_directions reduces its candidate moment directions with the reciprocal-space rotation rule applied to direct-lattice coordinates, so on any non-orthogonal lattice it merges directions that are not symmetry-equivalent and drops them from the set run_anisotropy computes.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `workflows-magnetic`
- mechanism: Symmetries' own class docstring (system/symmetry.py:71-77) states the convention and says it is easy to get backwards: rotations[s] is M with S a_i = sum_j M_ij a_j, a position in crystal coordinates transforms as c' = c M (i.e. M^T c as a column), and a Miller index as m' = M m. kpoints.py:147 and :222 confirm the reciprocal rule by rotating k as xkg @ rotation.T. anisotropy.py:655 builds its orbit as `images = [rotation @ lattice for rotation in rotations]`, which is M n, on a vector whose cartesian form is built two lines later as `cartesian = at.T @ lattice` = sum_i n_i a_i, i.e. a direct-lattice coordinate vector. The correct orbit is M^T n. {M} and {M^T} are both groups of the same order, so the reduction still partitions the 26 candidates into orbits and nothing raises, but they are the orbits of a different action. They coincide only when the crystal's matrices are signed permutations, which is cubic, tetragonal and orthorhombic; for hexagonal M(C3) = [[0,1,0],[-1,-1,0],[0,0,1]], whose transpose [[0,-1,0],[1,-1,0],[0,0,1]] has det +1 and trace 0 and is therefore a three-fold rotation, and the group holds only C3 and C3^2 as det=+1 trace=0 elements, neither of which it equals. Worked through on that cell: the code's orbit of (1,0,0) contains (-1,1,0), whose cartesian direction is -a1+a2 at 150 degrees from a1, while the true orbit contains (0,1,0) and (-1,-1,0) at 120 and 180 degrees. So a1 and the 150-degree direction are identified although a hexagonal crystal's [100] and [210] in-plane families are inequivalent.
- reached by: run_anisotropy(system, pseudos, density, directions="cardinal") or run_relaxed_anisotropy with the same, on a hexagonal, trigonal, monoclinic or triclinic cell. Both routes go through _direction_set (anisotropy.py:1382-1387), which is the shared resolver, so the frozen and the relaxed anisotropy are hit identically. Cubic, tetragonal and orthorhombic cells are unaffected, which is why nothing has shown.
- worth: The returned set is neither complete nor irreducible: on hcp cobalt, the module's own advertised material, the two in-plane direction families collapse to one, so MagneticAnisotropy.anisotropy, .easy_axis and .hard_axis are read off a set that never contains the second family and the basal-plane anisotropy comes out as exactly zero by omission rather than by physics. In the other direction it separates orbits that are genuinely one, which only costs extra diagonalisations.
- how to confirm: No run needed for the mechanism: read system/symmetry.py:71-77 beside anisotropy.py:653-657 and note that one says M^T for a direct-lattice vector and the other writes M. To see it change a number, call cardinal_directions on tests/data/qe's hexagonal cell (or any ibrav = 4 System), print len(directions) and the cartesian angles, then repeat with `rotation.T @ lattice` -- the two lists differ in membership, not only in order, and the count changes wherever the two hexagonal in-plane families were merged.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `cardinal_directions` and `sphere_cover` across all four documents including PERFORMANCE.md, no hit. The anisotropy rows in PERFORMANCE.md:6373-6376 and the AUDIT items at :305 and :381 are about the torque's tape, the force theorem's timing and get_relaxed_anisotropy's option forwarding, none of which touches the direction set.

### defumat/workflows/anisotropy.py:1015 -- `run_torque / frozen_expectation (via _with_quantization_axis)`

**run_torque and frozen_expectation rotate the quantization axis without the nosym requirement that _with_quantization_axis' own docstring says makes the rotation safe, so on a symmetry-reduced run the k-set is silently rebuilt as the wedge of the magnetic group of the new direction.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `workflows-magnetic`
- mechanism: _with_quantization_axis (anisotropy.py:795-833) calls system.with_spin(angle1=..., angle2=...) whenever the requested direction differs from the system's own, and its docstring at :819-822 argues this is safe because "it only runs when the direction differs from the system's own, which run_force_theorem already requires nosym for -- and a nosym grid is the complete one whatever group is asked about it". That argument holds at exactly two of its four call sites: run_force_theorem checks it at :534 and _refuse_relaxed checks it at :1213. The other two do not. run_torque calls _refuse_system at :1002, which refuses PAW-without-becsum, Hubbard, a potential-only meta-GGA, a field and a spiral, and says nothing about nosym, then calls _with_quantization_axis at :1015 with direction = cos(angle) first + sin(angle) second, which at the default angle of pi/4 essentially never equals the system's own angles, so the rebuild always fires. frozen_expectation does the same at :883. with_spin routes into System._respin_kpoints (builder.py:558-610), which for a non-nosym system takes rotations = symmetries.rotation_array() of the *magnetic* group built from the new angle1/angle2 via magnetic_symmetries, sets time_reversal = False because the run is magnetic, and rebuilds KPoints.automatic on that group. Two consequences follow. For frozen_expectation, two directions arrive on two different wedges and the direction-independence it exists to measure is then the k-sampling -- the identical defect run_force_theorem refuses by name at :534-543. For run_torque the wedge sum is exact only for quantities invariant under the group it was reduced with, and torque_at_angle differentiates sum_k w_k <psi|H(theta)|psi> in theta, where dH/dtheta carries the magnetization's derivative, an axial vector perpendicular to the direction the group was built around and not invariant under that group -- the "a response on a reduced k-set is a polar or axial vector field" trap, with nothing symmetrising it and no is_reduced guard anywhere on the path (grep of forces/torque.py finds no nosym, is_reduced or symmetr at all).
- reached by: run_torque or frozen_expectation on a noncollinear magnetic system that was not built with nosym = .true. -- an nscf input that simply omits it. Nothing reaches this today: co-tetragonal-anisotropy-soc.in and the _SMOKE_SOC text in tests/regression/test_anisotropy.py both set nosym = .true., so the whole test suite runs the safe branch.
- worth: A plausible wrong torque with no error raised, in the quantity whose whole selling point is that it cancels nothing: MagneticTorque.torque and .anisotropy_constant_mev come off a wedge that is not a symmetry of the perturbation. The direction-independence frozen_expectation reports (its docstring quotes 1.9e-6 meV) becomes a statement about two different k-sets rather than about the physics. The residual check MagneticTorque.residual does not see it -- band_energy and band_energy_check are both evaluated on the same wedge and agree.
- how to confirm: Read anisotropy.py:1002-1015 and :875-883 against :534 and :1213 and note the two entry points that reach _with_quantization_axis with no nosym clause. To measure it, take the tetragonal cobalt input, delete nosym = .true., and compare run_torque's .anisotropy_constant_mev against the nosym run; and run frozen_expectation at (0,0,1) and (1,0,0) on the same non-nosym cell, where its own claim is that the two agree to 1.9e-6 meV.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `_with_quantization_axis` and `frozen_expectation` across all four and found no hit. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:305-307 is about run_torque and is a memory finding (the reverse-mode tape holding one real-space block per k-point, no k_batch on the two calls at :1020 and :1023); it explicitly enumerates _refuse_system's refusals and does not remark on the missing nosym clause.

### defumat/workflows/dos.py:453 -- `run_dos (the tetrahedron branch)`

**The tetrahedra are indexed into a k-list reduced by a different group than the one that actually built the NSCF k-set, because `tetrahedra_for` is given the rotations and neither `time_reversal` nor `t_rev`, so a `nosym`, `noinv` or magnetic noncollinear run gets a density of states built from the wrong k-points with nothing raised.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `workflows-core`
- mechanism: `denser_grid` (nscf.py:392-413) reduces the grid through `grid_symmetry` (nscf.py:416-435), which returns the triple `(rotations, time_reversal, t_rev)` with `rotations = None` under `nosym`, `time_reversal = not noinv and not (nspin == 4 and domag)`, and the per-operation `t_rev` array. `run_dos` then calls `tetrahedra_for(scheme, nscf.kpoints, symmetries, system.cell)` passing only `symmetries`, and `tetrahedra_for` (scf/tetrahedra.py:277-284) forwards `symmetries.rotation_array()` to `build_tetrahedra`, whose `time_reversal` defaults to True (tetrahedra.py:222) and which has no `t_rev` parameter at all. `build_tetrahedra:237` then computes `equiv = grid_equivalence(grid, shift, rotations, time_reversal)`, whose return value is documented as an index *into the reduced list* -- `position[equivalent]` at kpoints.py:233-237, where `position` counts representatives. So `equiv` enumerates the wedge of a group that is not the wedge the eigenvalues live on. Under `nosym` the mismatch is largest and simplest: `grid_symmetry` gives `rotations = None`, `KPoints.automatic:490-491` takes the `monkhorst_pack` branch and the run diagonalises the **complete** grid, while `system.symmetry_group(nosym=True)` (builder.py:657-663) returns the identity alone and `build_tetrahedra` folds `k` onto `-k` anyway, so `equiv` spans roughly half the points. Every corner index is a valid index into the longer eigenvalue array, so the gather succeeds and half the k-list never contributes while the other half is counted twice. `noinv` alone and `nspin = 4` with `domag` break it the same way, the second twice over -- the operations flagged in `t_rev` are used as plain rotations *and* a global time reversal is added on top. QE cannot make this mistake: `setup.f90:643` passes `time_reversal` and `t_rev` into `tetra_init`, and `tetra_init` locates each corner by searching the actual `xk` list rather than by re-deriving the orbits.
- reached by: `occupations = 'tetrahedra'` (any variant) together with any one of `nosym = .true.`, `noinv = .true.`, or a noncollinear magnetic run; also any caller that hands `run_dos` a `kpoints=` set reduced with a group other than `system.symmetry_group(nosym=system.nosym)`. A plain nonmagnetic run with symmetry on is unaffected, because there both sides use the crystal group with time reversal.
- worth: A density of states and its Fermi level built from a subset of the k-points, each weighted by the wrong multiplicity. It is smooth, positive, integrates to the right electron count and is wrong in shape; on a metal the tetrahedron Fermi level moves with it, so every occupation downstream moves too.
- how to confirm: Host-side and with no SCF: for the run's own grid and shift, compare `grid_equivalence(grid, shift, np.eye(3)[None], time_reversal=True).max() + 1` against `len(monkhorst_pack(grid, shift)[0])` -- on a nosym run the first is about half the second, and the corner indices therefore cannot span the k-list the eigenvalues are on. Then read `tetrahedra_for` (scf/tetrahedra.py:277) and `build_tetrahedra`'s signature (tetrahedra.py:216-224) and confirm no caller in the package passes `time_reversal` or `t_rev`.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `tetrahedra_for`, `build_tetrahedra`, `grid_equivalence`, `time_reversal`, `noinv`; the only hits are PERFORMANCE.md's timing rows and an unrelated `grid_equivalence` mention at PERFORMANCE.md:4431)

### defumat/calculator.py:1555 -- `Calculator.get_berry_curvature (and get_z2, get_z2_3d, get_polarization, get_orbital_magnetization)`

**The same forwarding loosens the topology and polarization family's deliberately tighter 1e-8 diagonalisation default to whatever the input file's `&electrons conv_thr` said.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `continuation-facade`
- mechanism: `get_berry_curvature` (calculator.py:1555-1563), `get_z2` (:1756), `get_z2_3d` (:1764), `get_polarization` (:1584) and `get_orbital_magnetization` (:1565) all call `self._call_options(func, result, options)` with **no** `exclude`, so `_defaults_for` puts the calculator's `conv_thr` in ahead of the callee's. `run_berry_curvature` (topology.py:383), `run_z2` (:427), `run_z2_3d` (:467), `run_polarization` (polarization.py:71) and `run_orbital_magnetization` (orbital_magnetization.py:59) all declare `conv_thr: float = 1.0e-8`, and `FixedDensityStates` turns it into the eigensolver threshold at topology.py:268-271, `ethr = max(ETHR_MIN, 0.1 * min(1e-2, conv_thr / max(1, nelec)))`. The meaning of `conv_thr` is not different here -- that rule is pw.x's own NSCF schedule -- so what the facade overrides is a default the workflow chose deliberately tighter than the SCF's, not a name that means something else. On silicon's eight electrons the eigensolver goes from `ethr = 1.25e-10` to `1.25e-8` for an input stating `conv_thr = 1e-6`, and to `1.25e-6` for a loose 1e-4.
- reached by: Any `Calculator.from_file` whose input states `conv_thr` in `&electrons`, then any of the five topology or polarization methods. The `_call_options` calls at :1560, :1572, :1592, :1760 and :1768 are the five sites.
- worth: Two orders of eigenvector residual on the `<u_mk|S|u_nk'>` overlaps the whole of `defumat/topology/` is built from. It does **not** move a Chern number or a Fu-Kane parity, which are exact integers on any mesh by construction -- that is the design and it holds here. Where it can show is the Berry-phase polarization's phase and the Wilson-loop winding, neither of which is quantised, scaling with nk along a string. Stops mattering entirely for an input naming no `conv_thr`.
- how to confirm: Read `_call_options` at calculator.py:2022-2037 (it forwards `exclude` and the five call sites pass none) against `run_z2`'s signature at topology.py:427 and the `ethr` line at topology.py:268. To size it, compute one polarization through a calculator built from an input stating `conv_thr = 1e-4` and the same one through `get_polarization(conv_thr=1e-8)`, and difference the phases; the Chern number will agree either way, which is why nothing has caught it.
- against the record: Same root as finding 1 and the same negative grep: `get_polarization`, `get_z2` and `get_berry_curvature` appear in none of OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md in this connection. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:379 covered only the two `**kwargs` workflows.

### defumat/hamiltonian/noncollinear.py:255 -- `SpinorHamiltonian.apply`

**The spinor `apply` hands the Hubbard term the caller's raw `psi` and never masks its contribution, where the collinear twin masks both the input and the output.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `solver-batching-ham`
- mechanism: `Hamiltonian.apply` (operator.py:220-229) opens with `psi = jnp.where(self.mask[ik], psi, 0.0)` and closes with the same `where` over the whole result, so the Hubbard term at :227-228 sees a masked state and its output is masked. `SpinorHamiltonian.apply` (noncollinear.py:248-260) masks only inside `_split` (:233-241), then at :254 masks and joins the kinetic, local and nonlocal terms, and only *after* that adds `self.hubbard.apply(psi, ik)` using the unmasked argument `psi`, with no mask on the sum. Whether this moves a number depends entirely on `wfcU`: `HubbardTerm.apply` (hubbard/operator.py:71-75) is `columns @ (conj(columns)^T psi) v`, so its output is zero wherever `wfcU[ik]` is zero, and the contraction over the padding is zero only if `wfcU` is itself masked. That is the one thing I could not establish by reading -- `hubbard/projectors.py` builds the manifold and I do not own it -- so the finding is the asymmetry plus the condition it turns on.
- reached by: `noncolin` plus a `HUBBARD` card, and only if `wfcU` carries anything nonzero on the padding rows of the sphere. Inside Davidson the *input* half is harmless, since every vector handed to `apply` is already masked (davidson.py:471-475, :580); the exposed callers are the ones that pass an unmasked block, e.g. `matrix_by_application` at noncollinear.py:516-520, which builds `jnp.eye(ndim)` and never masks it.
- worth: If `wfcU` is unmasked, `H|psi>` gains a nonzero component on plane waves outside the sphere. Davidson would absorb it silently -- `_extend_projection` contracts against a masked `psi`, so the projected matrices are unaffected -- and it would show instead in `matrix_by_application`, which is the reference the whole operator is checked against, i.e. a validation fixture that agrees with a slightly different operator than the one the SCF runs.
- how to confirm: Read `hubbard/projectors.py` for whether the built `wfcU` is passed through `jnp.where(mask, ...)`. If it is, this is cosmetic and should be closed as such; if it is not, compare `SpinorHamiltonian.apply(psi, ik)` for a masked `psi` against the same with `jnp.where(_pair(mask, ik), ...)` wrapped around the Hubbard term, on a spinor DFT+U cell whose smallest sphere is below `npwx`.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `hubbard`, `mask`, `noncollinear.py`, `apply` in all four.

### defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py:363 -- `augmentation_dipole`

**`augmentation_dipole` gates on the dataset's declared `nqlc` but indexes the harmonic-product table at `pseudo.lmax`, so a dataset whose header over-declares `nqlc` past `2 lmax + 1` raises `IndexError` where QE clamps for exactly this reason.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
- mechanism: The early return is `if augmentation.qfuncl.shape[2] <= 1: return dipole` (:337), which reads the file's own `nqlc`, while the table it then indexes is `ap = harmonic_products(pseudo.lmax)` (:355) of leading dimension `(2 lmax + 1)^2`, indexed at `lp = 2, 3, 1` (:362). For `lmax = 0` that array is `(1, 1, 1)` and `ap[2]` is out of bounds. The two disagree only when the header's `nqlc` exceeds `2 lmax + 1`, and that is a case QE defends against by name: `init_us_1.f90:76-81` does `upf(nt)%nqlc = MIN(upf(nt)%nqlc, lmaxq)` under the comment "the following prevents an out-of-bound error: upf(nt)%nqlc=2*lmax+1 but in some versions of the PP files lmax is not set to the maximum l of the beta functions but includes the l of the local potential". This module has that clamp -- `_nl_of` (:821-826) -- and `build_augmentation` uses it; `augmentation_dipole` does not. It is the one path in the file that reads `augmentation.nqlc` raw.
- reached by: an ultrasoft or PAW dataset with only `s` projectors whose `PP_AUGMENTATION` declares `nqlc > 1`, reached through the Berry-phase position operator or the Born-charge / electrostriction stack (`response/efield.py:635` calls this per species)
- worth: no number, an `IndexError: index 2 is out of bounds` from inside `_augmentation_dipole` during a dielectric or Born-charge run. Only `lmax = 0` actually goes out of bounds -- at `lmax = 1` the table is `(9, 4, 4)` and `lp = 3` fits -- so the reachable set is small, which is why this is last.
- how to confirm: read `harmonic_products`'s leading dimension (`coupling.py:47`, `nlmq = (2 lmax + 1)^2`) against the `lp` values at augmentation.py:362, then grep the committed datasets for one with `nqlc` greater than `2 * max(angular_momentum) + 1`; none of the 55 in `tests/data/pseudo` has only `s` projectors and an augmentation charge, so a synthetic header edit is what would trip it.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. AUDIT-2026-09-18 touches `augmentation_dipole` only for the wording of its `_no_augmentation_section` refusal, and MEMORY-AUDIT names `_augmentation_dipole` only as the gate on the electric field's projector-velocity split.

### defumat/xc/functional.py:640 -- `Functional._spin_correlation_energy`

**A polarization that arrives with `|zeta| > 1` is clamped and evaluated, where `gcc_spin` skips the point and returns zero for the gradient correction to correlation.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `xc-paw`
- mechanism: `gcc_spin` (qe_drivers_gga.f90:1081-1092) clamps only when the incoming value already satisfies `ABS(zeta_io) <= 1`, and then tests `IF ( ABS(zeta) > 1 .OR. rho <= rho_threshold_gga .OR. SQRT(ABS(grho)) <= rho_threshold_gga )` and does `sc = 0; CYCLE`. So the `|zeta| > 1` branch is a *third* independent way for QE to zero the point, and it survives the clamp precisely because the clamp does not apply to it. defumat's :640-642 applies `clamp_polarization(..., 1.0 - RHO_THRESHOLD_GGA)` unconditionally and its `active` mask at :643-645 carries only the two density and gradient tests, so a point whose channel densities give `|rho_up - rho_dw| > |rho_up + rho_dw|` -- one channel negative -- is evaluated at `zeta = +-(1 - 1e-6)` rather than dropped. The comment at :638-639 records the clamp half of QE's rule and not the skip half.
- reached by: nspin = 2 or 4 with a gradient-corrected functional, at points where the total density exceeds 1e-6, the total gradient exceeds the same, and one channel density is negative -- a truncated plane-wave magnetization overshooting its charge in a low-density region, so a slab or an isolated magnetic atom in a box rather than a dense bulk crystal. It reaches both the plane-wave `gradcorr` path and the PAW spheres.
- worth: An extra PBE correlation energy density `rho * H` at points where QE contributes nothing, plus the matching `v1`/`v2` from the same differentiated expression. At `rho ~ 1e-6` and `H` of order 0.01 that is about 1e-8 Ry per point, so it is noise unless a large vacuum region puts thousands of points in the set; the potential error at those points is the more visible half, since it is not weighted by the density.
- how to confirm: First establish whether the regime is reachable at all, which is a read rather than a run: `XClib/xc_wrapper_gga.f90`'s `xc_gcx_` builds `zeta` from the two channel densities before calling `gcc_spin`, so check whether it can hand over `|zeta| > 1` at a point where `rho` is above `rho_threshold_gga`, or whether every such point already arrives as a trash value below threshold -- in the second case defumat's `active` gate already agrees with QE and this is a null. If it is reachable, add `& (jnp.abs((rho[0]-rho[1])/safe_density) <= 1.0)` to `active` and compare `etxc` and `v` on a magnetic slab.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `_spin_correlation_energy`, `gcc_spin`, `clamp_polarization`). OPEN.md G1 concerns `clamp_polarization`'s *tangent* at the tie and is closed; this is about which points are evaluated at all, not about the tangent.

### defumat/response/efield.py:603 -- `_screening_kernel (the "hartree" branch)`

**The RPA screening kernel broadcasts the induced Hartree potential into every density component, which for a spinor density `(n, m_x, m_y, m_z)` puts a spin-independent potential into the three magnetic-field channels where `as_potential_components` deliberately puts zero.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `sternheimer-core`
- mechanism: `_screening_kernel`'s `hartree` branch builds `v_r` from `total_charge(drho_g)` -- which correctly takes `rho_g[0]` alone when `shape[0] == 4` (`scf/potential.py:106`) -- and then returns `jnp.broadcast_to(v_r, drho.shape)` at :603, with the comment at :600-602 claiming this is "exactly as `_potential_of_rho` adds it". It is not: `_potential_of_rho` adds the Hartree term through `as_potential_components` (`scf/potential.py:408-426`), whose `nspin == 4` branch returns `stack([scalar, zero, zero, zero])` precisely because "a spin-independent potential contributes nothing" to the three magnetic components. For `nspin = 1` and `nspin = 2` the broadcast is right, which is why the discrepancy is invisible on every cell the RPA switch has been run on. The self-consistent loop would then screen the field with a spurious induced magnetic field of the same magnitude as the induced Hartree potential.
- reached by: `screening = "hartree"` on a noncollinear run carrying a magnetization (`nspin_mag = 4`) -- the RPA switch of `dielectric_tensor` and the route `workflows/tddft.py:322` takes. It is unreachable today, because both callers pass through `require_a_measured_spinor_response`, which refuses `nspin_mag == 4` outright.
- worth: No wrong number today. Its value is that the guard standing in front of it is about a different question entirely -- P83's unlocated 5.3 per cent disagreement with `ph.x` on the component along the moment, a statement about `f_xc` and the field's source term -- so when that is resolved and the refusal lifts, nothing points at this line. It is the project's own "inherit a refusal only after checking which machine it belongs to" trap set up in advance: the refusal belongs to the *full* kernel and this is the *RPA* one, which the P83 investigation itself used as the comparison (1.37741894) and which carries no `f_xc` at all.
- how to confirm: Read :600-603 beside `scf/potential.py:408-426` and `:780`/`:812`; or, with the `require_a_measured_spinor_response` guard lifted on `i-atom-soc.in`, check that the `hartree` branch's returned kernel has nonzero components 1 to 3 where `jax.jvp` of `calculation.potential` with the exchange-correlation term removed has zeros there.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `_screening_kernel`, `total_charge`, `as_potential_components` in the response context). The nearest recorded item is `require_a_measured_spinor_response` itself, which is the guard that currently hides this and is about a different defect.

### defumat/response/phononq.py:572 -- `self_consistent_response_at_q`

**The q != 0 self-consistency test measures the maximum of the potential change squared where the Gamma loop measures its sum of squares, so the same `tr2` stops the q-loop at a residual up to sqrt(3 nat N_grid) looser and `Phonons.history` records a different quantity from the one it documents.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `phonon-strain`
- mechanism: `self_consistent_response_at_q` tests `change = float(jnp.max(jnp.abs(induced - dvscf)) ** 2)` at `:572` and compares it against `tr2`, whose default on this entry point is 1.0e-14 (`:900`). `self_consistent_response` at Gamma tests `change = float(jnp.sum((induced - dvscf) ** 2))` at `:614` of `phonon.py` against the same default. For a field of N points over `3 nat` modes, `sum(x^2) >= max(x)^2` with the ratio up to `3 nat N`, so the q-loop declares convergence when `max|ddV| < 1e-7` while the Gamma loop needs `sqrt(sum) < 1e-7`, i.e. roughly `max|ddV| < 1e-7 / sqrt(3 nat N)`. On a 15^3 grid with two atoms that is about 140x in the residual. `Phonons.history` is documented as `|ddv_scf|^2 at each iteration` (`phonon.py:203`), which is the Gamma definition, and the q path fills it with the max-squared instead, so the two are not comparable and a user reading `history` off a q-phonon is reading a different quantity under the same name.
- reached by: Every phonon at q != 0. The effect grows with the dense FFT grid and with `3 nat`, so it is largest exactly where a dispersion calculation would be run and smallest on the two-atom cells P71 validated.
- worth: Not a visibly wrong number: a looser fixed point gives a `D(q)` less converged than the caller asked for, and P71's agreement with `ph.x` on its cells is already inside 0.2 cm^-1, so this is a precision inconsistency under one knob name rather than an error. What it costs is that `tr2` does not mean the same thing on the two entry points and cannot be transferred between them, and that a user tightening `tr2` to match the Gamma run is not getting the Gamma criterion. Stated plainly rather than inflated: this is the weakest of the six and may be a deliberate choice that was never written down.
- how to confirm: Read `phononq.py:572` against `phonon.py:614`, and `phonon.py:203` for what `history` is documented to hold. To size it, run P71's silicon q-phonon at the two criteria on the same cell -- `max^2 < tr2` as now, and `sum(|.|^2) < tr2` -- and compare the frequencies and the iteration counts against the committed `ph.x` reference; if the frequencies move by less than the 0.2 cm^-1 that reference asserts, the finding is a naming inconsistency and nothing more, which is the outcome to be prepared for.
- against the record: Adjacent to OPEN.md H8, which names the two fixed thresholds on this same entry point -- `threshold = 1.0e-14` for the CG and `tr2 = 1.0e-14` for the self-consistency test -- and argues they are two orders tighter than the rest of the family. H8 is about the *values* being fixed and tight; this is about the `tr2` test measuring a different norm from the one the Gamma loop measures under the same name, which H8 does not mention. Grepped `self_consistent_response_at_q` in all four documents: no other hit.

### defumat/topology/berry.py:264 -- `kubo_curvature`

**The Kubo route picks its branch on an attribute named `hamiltonian` that means two unrelated things, so a PlaneWaveStates carrying a fixed-density Hamiltonian is sent down the tight-binding path and crashes instead of using the plane-wave one written for it.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `topology-transport`
- mechanism: `kubo_curvature` reads `hamiltonian = getattr(states, 'hamiltonian', None)` (berry.py:264) and treats a non-`None` value as a differentiable callable `H(k)`: `jax.vmap(lambda k: jnp.linalg.eigvalsh(hamiltonian(k)))(points)` at :285 and `jax.jacfwd(hamiltonian)(k)` inside `_kubo_point` at :338. That is `ModelStates.hamiltonian` (states.py:253, `eqx.field(static=True)`, the model's own function of crystal k). But `PlaneWaveStates` has a field of the *same name* (states.py:~437) holding a `defumat.hamiltonian.operator.Hamiltonian` -- an `eqx.Module` of arrays, not a callable -- set by `build_plane_wave_states(hamiltonian=...)` and reachable through `DFTSource.states(keep_hamiltonian=True)` (workflows/topology.py:171, :293), which exists for the orbital magnetization. A state set carrying both that and a velocity operator never reaches the `isinstance(states, PlaneWaveStates)` dispatch two lines below at :266, which is the correct route; it fails inside a vmap with a TypeError that names neither field. The shipped path is safe only by accident: `chern_number` passes `keep_velocity=True` and nothing else (invariants.py:135), so `hamiltonian` is `None` there.
- reached by: A caller that builds one state set for both quantities -- `source.states(points, keep_velocity=True, keep_hamiltonian=True)` -- and then asks for `berry_curvature(states, mesh, method='kubo')`. No entry point in the package does this today, so it is a latent trap rather than a live defect.
- worth: A crash, not a wrong number: the failure is loud and happens before any physics. Its cost is that the two fields will drift further apart, and the next feature that legitimately wants a Hamiltonian and a velocity operator on the same states finds the Kubo route silently unavailable. Independent of nk, nbnd and every other size.
- how to confirm: Read states.py's `PlaneWaveStates` field list beside `ModelStates`' at :253 -- the two `hamiltonian` fields and their docstrings are three hundred lines apart and say different things. To see it fire, call `DFTSource.states(mesh.flat(), keep_velocity=True, keep_hamiltonian=True)` and pass the result to `kubo_curvature`. The fix is to dispatch on `isinstance(states, PlaneWaveStates)` first, or to rename one of the two fields.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `kubo_curvature`, which appears only at OPEN.md:801-814 about the degeneracy tolerance and the one-mask `jnp.where` form, and AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:933-935, which walked the same path for a *dataset* refusal and found the docstring wrong but says nothing about the dispatch.

### defumat/workflows/ultracell.py:189 -- `run_ultracell_stm (the augmentation-sphere guard)`

**The tip heights handed to the augmentation-sphere guard are divided by the ultracell's own extent along the imaging axis, where the guard reads them as unit-cell crystal coordinates, so on an ultracell modulated along that axis the guard is asked about a plane that is not the one the tip is on.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `workflows-core`
- mechanism: ultracell.py:189 builds `sampled = list(np.asarray(geometry.flat())[:, axis] / scale[axis])` with `scale = ultracell.shape`, and :192 hands it to `_refuse_an_augmented_plane`. `geometry.flat()` is in unit-cell crystal coordinates running over `[0, n_i)` -- that is the convention the module docstring states and the reason :199 samples at `geometry.flat() / scale`, since `sample_miller` wants the ultracell's coordinates to match the ultracell's Miller indices. The guard, though, wants a *unit-cell* coordinate: `_refuse_an_augmented_plane` (workflows/transport.py:1208-1213) takes `spacing = system.cell.volume / surface_area(system.cell, axis)`, the unit cell's interplanar spacing, and computes `|offset - round(offset)| * spacing` against `system.structure.positions_crystal(system.cell)`, so the right argument is `s mod 1`, not `s / n`. The two agree only at `n_axis = 1`, which is why the comment at :170-178 ("asked on the unit cell's coordinates -- every copy of the ultracell holds the same atoms at the same heights") is true of the intent and not of the line. The same expression is at :343 for the transmission's tip and at :487 for the spectrum's; the transmission is partly protected because `_refuse_a_stacked_ultracell` forces `n = 1` along `exit_axis`, but `axis` there defaults to `exit_axis` and may be given separately, and the spectrum is not protected at all -- its docstring says explicitly that a modulation along the stacking axis is fine for a spectrum.
- reached by: An ultrasoft or PAW ultracell modulated along the axis the image or spectrum is taken along -- `run_ultracell_stm`/`run_ultracell_sts` with `ultracell.shape[axis] > 1` -- and, for the transmission, `axis != exit_axis` with the ultracell modulated along `axis`. A norm-conserving dataset returns at transport.py:1206-1207 and never reaches it.
- worth: Both directions are live and the quiet one is the bad one. A tip at unit-cell coordinate 1.8 of a two-cell modulation is asked about at 0.9 and can be refused although it is in the vacuum, which is loud; a tip at 0.4, inside a sphere, is asked about at 0.2 and can pass, which leaves a Tersoff-Hamann image built from smooth states inside the augmentation region -- short by the whole augmentation charge, positive, smooth and unmarked, which is exactly what the guard exists to prevent.
- how to confirm: Read ultracell.py:189 and :192 against `_refuse_an_augmented_plane` at workflows/transport.py:1196-1221, and note that `spacing` there is built from `system.cell` -- the unit cell -- so its `height` must be a unit-cell coordinate. Host-side, for a two-cell modulation along `axis`, print `geometry.flat()[:, axis]` and compare `s / n` against `s % 1` for a tip at `height = 1.8`: 0.9 against 0.8, which lands on a different plane of the unit cell.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `_refuse_an_augmented_plane` and `_refuse_a_stacked_ultracell`: no hits in any of the four)

## Costs, ranked by confidence

### defumat/scf/driver.py:2814 -- `Calculation.at_kpoints (and at_strain, at_kcart, at_spiral_q)`

**Four of the five movers rebuild the projectors without passing lazy=, so a run started with projectors='rebuild' silently reverts to the stored (nk, npwx, nkb) vkb the dial exists to avoid, while still reporting that the dial is on.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-driver`
- mechanism: ProjectorCore.at_positions takes lazy: bool = False (projectors.py:191-217) and materialises the whole (nk, npwx, nkb) array through _apply_phases unless lazy is asked for. Only two of driver.py's seven call sites pass it: __init__ at :1631 and at_positions at :2444, both as lazy=self.projector_storage == 'rebuild'. at_strain (:2670), at_kpoints (:2814), at_kcart (:2918) and at_spiral_q (:3027 and :3067) call it with qq= alone and so take the eager default. Because copy.copy carries projector_storage across, the returned calculation still says 'rebuild' -- which is what run_scf prints at :5251 and what sizing.py's estimate keys on (MEMORY-AUDIT:1186 already records that sizing does not model the dial) -- while vkb is resident again. at_kpoints is the clean anchor: every band-structure and NSCF run built on a rebuild calculation goes through it, no derivative is involved, and the docstring's whole argument is that this method exists to make a many-k sub-run affordable. at_cell reaches at_strain and then at_positions, so a vc-relax step materialises vkb transiently before the lazy set is restored.
- reached by: Any run that asked for the dial -- projectors='rebuild' or DEFUMAT_PROJECTORS=rebuild, which is not the default ('store', batching.py:608) -- that then takes a band path, an NSCF, a stress, a velocity operator or a spiral E(q) scan. Largest on a many-k, many-projector cell: a slab, a fully-relativistic dataset with nh in the twenties, or a spiral scan that walks at_spiral_q per wavevector.
- worth: Scales as nk x npwx x nkb x 16 bytes, resident under every stage rather than inside one. MEMORY-AUDIT's own figure for the 45-atom NiBr2 slab is 13.96 GB of vkb, against a 12.10 GB wavefunction set -- the entire saving the dial was measured to buy. It stops mattering on a one-k or few-projector cell, where vkb is megabytes.
- how to confirm: Build a Calculation with projectors='rebuild' and call at_kpoints on a longer k-list, then read calculation.projectors.is_lazy (projectors.py:90) -- it is True before and False after -- or bracket jax.live_arrays() around the call the way MEMORY-AUDIT:1137 brackets the SCF. Reading driver.py:2444 against :2670, :2814, :2918, :3027 and :3067 settles it without running anything. Whether the fix for at_strain and at_kcart is to pass lazy= or to refuse the combination by name needs a derivative through a lazy set, which I could not establish by reading.
- against the record: Extends MEMORY-AUDIT A13 ("vkb is materialised for every k-point and sits outside the k_batch dial", MEMORY-AUDIT:1016-1190), which records the dial, its 13.96 GB and that sizing.py does not model it. That the four movers drop the dial is not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/scf/mixing.py:637 -- `local_tf_preconditioner._screen.dot (and operator, :629)`

**The Krylov inner product and the operator apply multiply device arrays by host numpy arrays (`weight`, `g2`, `nonzero`), so every one of the roughly five hundred inner products per SCF iteration re-uploads a full `ngm`-length array, and `dot` is not jitted so each one is three unfused kernels over `ngm`.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-body`
- mechanism: `g2` (:591), `weight` (:598-599) and `nonzero` (:597) are built with `np.asarray`/`np.zeros_like` and stay numpy. `dot` (:637) evaluates `jnp.sum(weight * jnp.real(jnp.conj(a) * b))`: a JAX array's `__array_priority__` makes numpy defer, so `weight` goes through `jnp.asarray` and a fresh `device_put` on every call, and nothing memoises that by identity. The same happens at `operator` (:630) via `g2 * _to_sphere(...)`, at `dv = g2 * _to_sphere(...)` (:634), at `dv.at[~nonzero].set(0.0)` (:635) and at `directions.append(residue / (g2 + agg0))` (:676), where `g2 + agg0` is additionally a fresh host allocation of length `ngm` per direction. `dot` carries no `jax.jit`, so `conj`, the multiply, `real`, the multiply by `weight` and the reduction are five separate dispatches each allocating a full `ngm` temporary, where one jitted function would fuse them into a single reduction. Per Krylov step of width `m` the loop calls `dot` `m + 2` times (the new row and column of `aa`, `bb`, and the residue norm), and with `LOCAL_TF_MMX = 12` and `LOCAL_TF_REFRESHES = 4` that is about sixty steps.
- reached by: `mixing_mode = 'local-TF'` on a large dense grid -- the inhomogeneous slab the mode exists for. `kerker` does not reach this code and neither does any other mixer.
- worth: Scales with `ngm` and with the Krylov step count, not with `nk`, `nbnd` or `natom`. At `ngm` of order a million it is tens of megabytes re-copied per inner product, a few gigabytes of host-to-device traffic and several hundred unfused `ngm`-length temporaries per SCF iteration. It is a few seconds rather than the whole 730 s gap, so it is a contributor to be separated out rather than the explanation; on a small cell it does not matter at all.
- how to confirm: Profile one `local_tf_preconditioner` call at production `ngm` with the FFTs and the transfers separated, which is the first step OPEN.md already asks for; then hoist `g2`, `weight` and `nonzero` to `jnp` once at build time and wrap `dot` in `jax.jit`, and re-time the same call. The output must be bit-identical.
- against the record: Extends OPEN.md Part VIII item 4, `local-TF costs about 730 s an iteration` (line 3415), which lists four candidate mechanisms -- the Python loop, `dot`'s `float()` host sync, `_alpha` in numpy, and no batching -- and says which dominates is unmeasured. The host *upload* of the numpy operands and `dot` being unjitted are a fifth and sixth mechanism, on neither that list nor anywhere else in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/scf/locals.py:76 -- `_minimum_image_distances`

**Building the per-atom spheres allocates a host-side `(ngrid, nat, 8, 3)` float64 array and its square at the same time, so the one-off construction peaks at tens of GB on exactly the cells the packed storage layout was introduced to make reachable.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `scf-occ-mag`
- mechanism: `build_local_regions` calls `_minimum_image_distances(points, positions, at)` with `points` the whole dense FFT grid, so `difference` is `(ngrid, nat, 3)`, `cartesian` is another, and line 76 broadcasts to `shifted` at `(ngrid, nat, 8, 3)`; line 77 is `np.sqrt(np.min(np.sum(shifted**2, axis=-1), axis=-1))`, and `shifted**2` is a second array of that shape alive beside `shifted` while the sum runs, with the `(ngrid, nat, 8)` reduction on top. That is 2 x 24 x 8 bytes x nat x ngrid plus 64 x nat x ngrid. `_qe_weights` (line 214) then allocates `np.zeros((nat, npoint))` float64 plus three boolean masks of `(npoint,)` per atom, and only after that does `build_local_regions` collapse it to the packed `owner`/`taper` pair. So the dense `(nat, ngrid)` array the packed layout exists to avoid is materialised in full on the way to building the packed one. This is reached by every run with `nspin_mag in (2, 4)`, not only a constrained one: driver.py:5703-5710 calls `calculation.site_moments(rho_out)` each iteration for `report_mag`, and that goes through `local_regions()` (driver.py:2295-2318), which calls `build_local_regions` unconditionally when the magnetic field did not already build it.
- reached by: Any collinear-spin or noncollinear run whose cell is large. Scales as nat x ngrid on the dense grid, with a 24x constant from the eight minimum-image corners and three cartesian components. It does not depend on the scheme -- `smooth` and `qe` share the distance builder.
- worth: Using PERFORMANCE.md P77's own cell estimates: the 45-atom NiBr2 cycloid at ~2e6 dense points gives `shifted` at 17.3 GB with a second copy beside it, so roughly 40 GB transient, against the 24 MB that table quotes as what makes the readout "unconditional instead of a flag"; the 157-atom slab at ~4e6 points gives ~120 GB per copy, which no machine here has. The h10 case P77 does time at 795 ms is already 491 MB per copy, so the cost is visible in the measurement that was taken and was read as wall clock rather than as memory. The reference form is O(1) in this term: `make_pointlists` loops atoms and points and evaluates the eight corners per point, so chunking the grid axis, or looping atoms with the running `taken` mask `_qe_weights` already keeps, reproduces the packed result bit for bit at a fixed working set.
- how to confirm: Read the shapes off line 76 and multiply, or instrument `build_local_regions` with `resource.getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss` before and after on `h10-chain-noncolin` (nat = 10, ngrid = 256000) and check it moves by about 1.2 GB; the same run with a 45-atom cell is the one that will not finish.
- against the record: Extends PERFORMANCE.md P77 ("What a per-atom moment costs to report, and what the spheres cost to store", lines 5125-5160), which records the construction wall clock (42 ms and 795 ms) and the dense-versus-packed *storage* table and says nothing about the construction's transient peak. Grepped `_qe_weights`, `pointlist`, `scf/locals` and `local_regions` in OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md and MEMORY-AUDIT.md: no hits.

### defumat/calculator.py:598 -- `Calculator.get_scf (the cache test ahead of _adopt)`

**The cache test returns before `_adopt` runs, so `get_scf(projectors='rebuild')` on a calculator that already holds a matching ground state is a silent no-op: the memory dial is never adopted and every later method still runs with the projector table resident.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `continuation-facade`

Line 598 then returns `self._scf` on a match. `self._adopt(options)` is at :605, below that return. `_adopt` (:501-513) is the only thing that writes `projectors` into `self.defaults` and drops `self._calculation`, and `Calculator.calculation` (:458-468) reads `self.defaults.get("projectors", "default")` when it builds the `Calculation`. So the ordering works in the direction the comment at :583-589 was written for and fails in the other: with a cached SCF, `get_scf(projectors='rebuild')` returns the cache, never reaches `_adopt`, leaves `defaults['projectors']` at `'default'`, and keeps the existing `Calculation` with its resident `vkb`. The comment at :601-604 asserts the opposite -- "`_adopt` below already drops the cache when one changes" -- which is true only on a miss. - reached by: Any session that converges an SCF and *then* discovers it needs the memory dial -- `calc.get_scf()`, then `calc.get_scf(projectors='rebuild')` before a force, a stress or a response. It is the natural order on a slab, because the SCF is what fits and the derivative is what does not.

Scales with nk times npwx times nkb. It stops mattering on a fresh calculator, where the first `get_scf` misses and `_adopt` runs normally.
- how to confirm: Read calculator.py:590-605 in order -- the `SETUP_ONLY_OPTIONS` filter, the early `return`, then `_adopt`. To confirm without a run: `calc.get_scf(); calc.get_scf(projectors='rebuild'); calc.defaults.get('projectors')` returns `'default'`, and `calc._calculation` is the same object it was before.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:263-269, which found the *other* face of this dial -- `_adopt` writing a key `Calculator.__init__` rejected -- and closed it by adding `SETUP_ONLY_OPTIONS` and stripping it from `merged`. ### defumat/solvers/davidson.py:747 -- `starting_vectors`

**The random starting guess is generated in float64 and assembled in complex128 whatever `config.Precision` is in force, because `jax.random.uniform` is called with no `dtype`.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `solver-batching-ham`
- mechanism: davidson.py:747-751 draws `real` and `imaginary` with `jax.random.uniform(keys[i], (nbnd, ndim))` and no `dtype` argument. The package enables x64 in `__init__.py:27`, so JAX canonicalises the unspecified float to float64 -- which is exactly what `config.py`'s module docstring forbids (`no dtype literals in compute code`, every array's dtype from the `Precision` instance). `real + 1j * imaginary` is then complex128, and only the following `.astype(dtype)` brings it back to the policy's complex64 under `SINGLE`. So the *value* is right and no number moves; what is wrong is that the largest array a cold start allocates is built at twice the requested width, and the threefry generation behind it produces two uint32 words per element instead of one. The array is `(nbnd, ndim)`, the same block the file's own working-set fit prices at `4.20 nbnd npwx zc` (davidson.py:84).
- reached by: Any run whose first SCF iteration has no previous wavefunctions, i.e. every cold start, and it is only visible under `precision = 'single'` -- which is the GPU performance mode `config.py` exists to keep viable.
- worth: Scales with `nbnd npwx` (times `npol`, since `ndim` is `2 npwx` for a spinor). At the 157-atom slab's shapes the docstring quotes -- `nbnd = 1020`, gamma storage -- one `(nbnd, npwx)` block is 1.28 GB at complex64 and this path builds it at complex128, so the transient is 2.55 GB plus two float64 draws, against 1.28 GB intended. XLA may fuse the chain into one complex64 store, in which case only the doubled PRNG work survives; it stops mattering entirely in double precision and after the first iteration, where `psi0` is not `None`.
- how to confirm: Read davidson.py:747-751 -- no `dtype` is passed, and `config.DEFAULT_PRECISION` never reaches this function, which takes `dtype` as an argument and uses it only on line 750. To price the fused case, compile `_every_k` with `psi0 = None` under `SINGLE` and read `memory_analysis().temp_size_in_bytes` before and after adding `dtype=` to both `jax.random.uniform` calls; the eigenvalues must be unchanged in double precision, where the cast is a no-op.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `starting_vectors`, `random.uniform`, `PRNGKey`, `dtype` in all four; AUDIT-2026-09-18 records one dtype literal fixed in `response/sternheimer.py` and notes 119 remaining, with the rule that a dtype finding is only worth making inside a jitted path where it blocks float32, which this is.

This module's own `species_atomic_charge` docstring sizes that class of work at 'eight radial integrations over 36257 G-vectors for one silicon species, which was 91% of the analytic force', so it is not free. Paid once per `run_scf`, so it stops mattering as soon as the SCF takes more than a couple of iterations, and it never touches the inner loop.

### defumat/basis/planewaves.py:161 -- `build_plane_wave_basis`

**The per-k plane-wave selection tests every G of the smooth set against the wavefunction cutoff, although the smooth set reaches 4*ecutwfc and so holds about eight times as many G as can possibly be inside, and it does this on the host once per k-point.**
