- mechanism: The loop at :159-169 runs `kg2 = np.sum((k[ik] + g)**2, axis=1)` over the whole `(ngms, 3)` array for every k-point, then `keep = kg2 <= gcutw`, then `np.nonzero`. `g` comes from the smooth set, whose cutoff is `4 * ecutwfc` (builder.py:329), so its radius in G is twice the wavefunction sphere's and its count is roughly eight times `npw`. The list is sorted by |G|^2 by construction -- `generate_gvectors` asserts it and `smooth_subset` re-checks it -- so everything past the first index with |G| > sqrt(gcutw) + |k| is certainly excluded, and a single `np.searchsorted` on the sorted |G|^2 would bound the scan. Nothing about the result changes; the same indices come out in the same order.
- reached by: Every run at build time, and again on every `at_kpoints` and every `at_spiral_q` -- so once per point of an E(q) scan and once per k-set a topological invariant asks for. Worst on a run with many k-points and a large dual, i.e. exactly the ultrasoft and PAW cases where ngms is largest relative to npw.
- worth: Host-side, single-threaded numpy, scaling as nk * ngms where only nk * npw is needed -- about a factor of eight of the arithmetic plus the boolean and the `nonzero` over the same length. On a 64-k slab with ngms of a few hundred thousand that is a second or two of setup per rebuild; on a two-atom cell it is invisible. It stops mattering the moment nk is small or the run is a single SCF, and it never touches a compiled path or a peak working set.
- how to confirm: Time `build_plane_wave_basis` on a many-k ultrasoft input and compare against the same loop with `g` sliced to `np.searchsorted(g2_sorted, (np.sqrt(gcutw) + |k|)**2)` rows; the returned `indices`, `mask` and `npw` must be identical element by element.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped 'build_plane_wave_basis'; the two hits are AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:153 and :225, both about the gamma_only default and the |k+G|^2 snap, neither about the scan length)

**The two radial transforms this module writes for itself, `dV_loc/d(G^2)` and `d rho_core/d|G|`, call their jitted kernels on the whole dense G list at once, bypassing the `CHUNK = 4096` blocking that every transform in `pseudo/formfactors.py` goes through -- including the two called from the same list comprehensions.**

At `msh ~ 900` that is roughly 7 KB of transient per G-vector per intermediate, so about 1.4 GB across the four live arrays at `ngm = 50k` (eight-atom silicon scale) and tens of GB on a slab, against the ~36 MB the same transform costs blocked at 4096 -- a factor of `ngm/4096`. It stops mattering on a small cell at a low `ecutrho`, where `ngm` is a few thousand and the whole vector is under one chunk anyway. ### defumat/response/efield.py:417 -- `dielectric_tensor (the self-consistency test), with TR2 at :160`

**The response loop's convergence test is an un-normalised sum of squares compared against `tr2 = 1e-14`, where `mix_pot.f90` divides the same sum by the square of the number of elements, so this code converges its screened response roughly nine orders of magnitude tighter than the `ph.x` run it is timed against.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `sternheimer-core`
- mechanism: `change = float(jnp.sum((proposed - dvscf) ** 2))` at :417 is tested against `tr2` at :430, and `TR2 = 1.0e-14` is documented at :158-160 as "the quantity dfpt_kernels prints and tests". It is not that quantity. `LR_Modules/mix_pot.f90:77-85` computes `dr2 = dnrm2(ndim, vout, 1)**2` and then `dr2 = (sqrt(dr2) / ndimtot)**2`, i.e. the same sum divided by `ndimtot**2`, with `ndimtot = ndim = 2 * ndim_pot` reals; `PHonon/PH/dfpt_kernels.f90:226` sets `ndim_pot = npert * dfftp%nnr * nspin_mag` and `:438` tests it against `npert * tr2_ph / npol`. QE's bar on the raw sum is therefore `3 * tr2_ph * (2 * 3 * nnr * nspin_mag)**2`; on the committed silicon epsilon cell (`nnr` of 15^3 = 3375, `nspin_mag = 1`, `npert = 3`) that is `3e-14 * 20250**2`, about `1.2e-5`, against this code's `1e-14` on the same sum. Nothing anywhere in the loop applies the `1/ndimtot**2`, and `history.append(change)` at :418 and the `verbose` line at :420 then print an unnormalised number under `ph.x`'s own `|ddv_scf|^2` label.
- reached by: Every self-consistent response that goes through this loop: `dielectric_tensor`, and so `get_dielectric_tensor`, the Born charges, and the RPA route `workflows/tddft.py:322` takes. The gap widens with the dense FFT grid, because the sum grows with `nnr` while the threshold does not, so a large cell is held to a proportionally stricter standard than a small one.
- worth: Three consequences, in order. (1) Iteration count: the loop runs to a tolerance about `1.2e9` times tighter than `ph.x`'s, which at a per-iteration contraction of roughly 0.3 is some thirteen to seventeen extra passes, and the observed gap is exactly that size -- `PERFORMANCE.md:1867` records 18 iterations here against QE's 5. Each pass is three Sternheimer solves at `nk x nbnd` CG blocks, so the cost scales with `nk`, `nbnd` and `npwx` and is most of the wall clock of a dielectric or Born-charge run. (2) The same `tr2` is a different physical tolerance on every cell, since the sum scales with the FFT grid, so a tolerance tuned on silicon does not transfer. (3) On a cell where `1e-14` on an absolute sum is unreachable inside `max_iterations = 40` -- and the CG's own fixed `1e-12` floor puts a bound on how small the residual can get -- `converged = False` is reported on a tensor that is fine, and downstream consumers refuse on that flag (`response/elastic.py:137` refuses an unconverged strain response by name).
- how to confirm: Read `mix_pot.f90:77-85` beside `dfpt_kernels.f90:226` and `:438` and compare with `efield.py:417`; then run `si-epsilon.in` with `tr2` set to `3e-14 * (2*3*nnr*nspin_mag)**2` and check that `epsilon` is unchanged to the digits `PERFORMANCE.md` quotes against `ph.x` (13.806646) while the iteration count drops toward QE's 5.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped for ndimtot, mix_potential, ddv_scf, tr2). It is not a new item in PERFORMANCE.md either -- it contradicts one and supersedes another. PERFORMANCE.md:4835-4836 states "the two sides are converged to the same place ... the ph.x input carries tr2_ph = 1.0d-14 and defumat runs at its matching tr2 = 1e-14. So neither column is buying speed with a looser solve"; the two thresholds are not matching and this side is buying nothing, it is paying. And PERFORMANCE.md:1867 explains the 18-versus-5 iteration gap by the fixed 1e-12 CG threshold plus linear mixing, which omits what looks like the largest term.

### defumat/response/velocity.py:277 -- `VelocityOperator._operator`

**Every application of the velocity operator rebuilds `newd` -- the dense-grid integral of the potential against the augmentation charge -- although `D_ij` does not depend on `k` at all, so an ultrasoft or PAW run pays it three to nine times per call family where once would do.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `sternheimer-core`
- mechanism: `_operator` at :267-277 does `moved = self.calculation.at_kcart(kcart)` and then `moved.hamiltonian(self.v_scf, self.ddd_paw, hubbard)`. `Calculation.hamiltonian` (`scf/driver.py:3777-3778`) computes `total = v_scf + as_potential_components(self.vltot, ...)` and `deeq = self.coefficients(total, ddd_paw)`, which for an ultrasoft dataset is `_newd` (`driver.py:3210`), a contraction of `Q_ij(G)` against the potential over the whole dense G set for every species and every spin channel. Neither `total` nor `deeq` carries `kcart`: `at_kcart` moves only `|k+G|^2` and `vkb(k)`, which is precisely what this module's own docstring says at lines 11-16. Because `potential` is a concrete array rather than a tracer, `_newd`'s `jax.jit` cache saves the compilation and not the result, so each eager `jax.jvp` dispatch executes it again. `_tangent` is entered once per direction by `apply`, `apply_s` and `both`, so `band_velocities` and `matrix_elements` each pay three, `generalised_matrix_elements` pays three from `both` plus three more from `augmentation_connection` (:387, which calls `calculation.hamiltonian(...)` again and also rebuilds `_augmentation_dipole` per direction), and `second_matrix_elements` pays six through `apply_second`'s nested `jvp`. The same object is also already sitting in the Hamiltonian the caller built before constructing the operator.
- reached by: Any ultrasoft or PAW dataset reaching the velocity operator: `band_velocities`, `matrix_elements` (the Kubo and SHG stacks), `generalised_matrix_elements` and `second_matrix_elements`. A norm-conserving run is unaffected -- `Calculation.coefficients` returns `None` on its first line -- which is why this has not shown up in a timing.
- worth: Pure setup waste, `nh^2 x ngm x natom x nspin_mag` per redundant evaluation and independent of `nk` and `nbnd`, so it is worst exactly where `ngm` is large and the band set is small: a slab or a fully-relativistic dataset, where `nh` is in the twenties and `ngm` in the millions. Five to eight of the six-to-nine evaluations per call family are removable by building the k-independent `deeq` once in `__init__` and handing it to `hamiltonian`, or by hoisting `augmentation_connection`'s Hamiltonian and dipole out of the direction loop. It stops mattering on a norm-conserving dataset and on any cell where the eigensolver, not the operator, dominates.
- how to confirm: Read `scf/driver.py:3777-3778` and `:3210` beside `velocity.py:267-277`; then time `VelocityOperator.matrix_elements` warm on `alas-epsilon-us.in` (ultrasoft) against the same call with `deeq` hoisted, and confirm bit-identity of the result.
- against the record: Extends nothing directly and is not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md. It must be read against PERFORMANCE.md:4509, which records a *null*: "Sharing the three velocity tangents through jax.linearize instead of three jvp calls gives a bit-for-bit identical answer in 6.33 s against 6.35 -- XLA had already deduplicated the common primal." That measurement had no power to see this. It was taken on the P54 SHG cell (PERFORMANCE.md:4446-4450), which is `tests/data/qe/alas-shg.in`, whose species are `Al.pz-vbc.UPF` and `As.pz-bhs.UPF` -- both norm-conserving, so `coefficients` returned `None` and `newd` never ran. It is also a different claim: that item is about sharing *tangents* inside one trace, this is about a *primal* recomputed across separate eager dispatches, which XLA cannot deduplicate.

### defumat/response/phononq.py:818 -- `frozen_force_constants`

**The frozen Hessian at q != 0 materialises 3 nat separate full-size zero wavefunction blocks and then drives 3 nat zero-tangent jvp of the energy gradient through them, so the stage costs twice what it needs in time and holds 3 nat wavefunction blocks of zeros at its peak.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `phonon-strain`
- mechanism: `frozen_force_constants` fills `empty = np.empty((nat, 3), dtype=object)` with a fresh `jnp.zeros_like(solver.psi)` at `:821`, one per mode, and hands the whole object array to `_force_constants` as its `dpsi`. Each entry is a distinct allocation of shape `(nspin, nk, nocc, npwx)` complex, and all `3 nat` of them are alive together for the duration of the call. Inside `_force_constants`, the norm-conserving branch (`phonon.py:1379-1385`) runs two jvp per mode: `jax.jvp(frozen, (positions, density), (tangent, drho))`, which is the frozen Hessian and is wanted, and `jax.jvp(electronic, (psi,), (states,))`, whose tangent `states` is `jnp.zeros_like(psi).at[:, :, :nocc].set(dpsi[row, cart])` and is therefore identically zero here. JAX does not know the tangent is zero at trace time, so the second jvp is traced and executed in full -- a complete forward-over-reverse pass through `frozen_energy`'s local potential, Ewald, nonlocal and XC terms -- and returns zeros that are then added. `frozen_force_constants` is the only caller that passes an all-zero state tangent, which is why no other path pays this.
- reached by: Any phonon at q != 0, which is the only caller of `frozen_force_constants`; norm-conserving insulator on a full k-grid, the one regime `require_a_two_sphere_regime` admits.
- worth: Time: the wasted jvp is of the same kind and roughly the same cost as the one beside it, so about 2x on the frozen-Hessian stage, scaling as 3 nat times (nk nbnd npwx + the dense FFT grid). Memory: 3 nat times nspin nk nocc npwx times 16 bytes -- 7 MB on P71's two-atom silicon, which is why it has never shown, and about 4.7 GB on a 16-atom cell at nk = 64, nocc = 32, npwx = 3000. It stops mattering when the self-consistent solves dominate, which on the two-atom cells P71 was validated on they do by two orders.
- how to confirm: Read `phonon.py:1371-1386` beside `phononq.py:815-827`: the `states` tangent is provably zero because every `empty[atom, cart]` is `zeros_like`. To size it, run P71's q-phonon through `tools/benchmark.py` warm, then again with the `electronic` jvp skipped when the state tangent is known zero and with one shared zero array in `empty`; the matrix must be bit-identical.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md. Grepped `frozen_force_constants` across all four documents: no hit. MEMORY-AUDIT.md:2224 records a different `phononq.py` item (`:953`, keeping the full-nbnd `psi_kq` alive, judged too small); that entry is about `psi_kq` retention, not about `empty` or the zero-tangent jvp.

**`dynamical_matrix` computes `overlap_derivatives` and then calls `orthogonality_states`, which computes the identical object a second time, so an ultrasoft or PAW dynamical matrix pays for 6 nat overlap-derivative jvp where 3 nat are needed.**

### defumat/response/phononq.py:973 -- `dynamical_matrix_at_q`

**`np.asarray(wavefunctions).shape[2]` materialises the entire ground-state wavefunction block on the host to read one integer, and JAX caches that host copy on the array for its lifetime.**

- reached by: Every call to `dynamical_matrix_at_q` that does not pass `nbnd` explicitly, which is the normal path.
- worth: One full copy of the wavefunction block: nspin x nk x nbnd x npwx x 16 bytes, retained rather than transient. On the CPU backend here the numpy array aliases the single device buffer and it costs approximately nothing, which is why the committed cells never show it; on the GPU this code is declared to run on unchanged it is a real transfer of order 1 GB on a 16-k, 100-band, 40000-npwx run and a retained host copy beside it. No number changes on either platform -- this is before any trace and every tangent downstream goes through `jnp.asarray`.
- how to confirm: Read `phononq.py:973` against `phonon.py:383`, which reads the same integer off the device array. Confirm the retention claim the way AUDIT-2026-09-18 confirmed it at `elastic.py:171`: JAX caches `np.asarray`'s result in `_npy_value`.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18.md's `elastic.py:171` item (`nocc = np.asarray(response.dpsi[0, 0]).shape[2]`, lines 1065-1067), which was found and fixed for exactly this reason -- a 1.02 GB retained host copy on GPU to obtain one integer. That audit was partitioned by defect class and reached `response/elastic.py`; it did not reach `response/phononq.py`, and this is the same defect at a second site. Distinct from MEMORY-AUDIT.md:2224, which records `phononq.py:953` keeping the full-nbnd `psi_kq` alive (a different array and a different line, judged too small).

### defumat/response/strain.py:441 -- `_frozen_density_response`

**The frozen-state density response loops the second strain label over all three values instead of over `range(a, 3)`, so three of its nine density jvp are bit-identical duplicates -- six of eighteen once the occupied block is present.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `phonon-strain`
- mechanism: `_frozen_density_response` runs `for a in range(3): for b in range(3):` at `:440-441` and builds its tangent as `strain_tangent(a, b)`. `strain_tangent` (`:127-138`) returns `0.5 * (E_ab + E_ba)`, which is symmetric by construction, so `strain_tangent(a, b)` and `strain_tangent(b, a)` are the same array and the two jvp return the same result. Each of those calls is `jax.jvp(mixed, (zero, psi), ...)` where `mixed` rebuilds the whole density at the strained cell -- `sum_band` over nk and nocc on the smooth grid, `to_dense`, and `moved.augmented(...)` with `mixed_becsum` for an ultrasoft or PAW dataset. When `ort` is not `None` the body takes a second jvp per (a, b), so the count goes from 12 needed to 18 run. Every other loop in the file already has the right bound: `_bare_strains` (`:341`), `overlap_derivatives` (`:375`) and the solve loop in `_self_consistent_response` (`:474`) all write `for b in range(a, 3)`.
- reached by: Every `strain_response` call, so every elastic-constant, electrostriction and elasto-optic run; the ultrasoft and PAW path pays six extra rather than three, because the `ort` half doubles the body.
- worth: Three (norm-conserving) or six (ultrasoft/PAW) redundant full density builds per run, each scaling as nk x nocc FFTs of the smooth grid plus `to_dense` plus, for an augmented dataset, the `(nh^2, ngm)` augmentation sum. It is a once-per-run setup cost, not a per-iteration one, so against roughly 6 strains x niter x 30 CG steps of `h_psi` it is well under a per cent on the measured cells; it grows with the FFT grid and with the augmentation table rather than with the solve, so a large-grid ultrasoft cell with few k-points is where it would show.
- how to confirm: Read `strain.py:440-457` against `:341` and `:474`, and `strain_tangent` at `:127-138` for the symmetry of the tangent. Change the bound to `range(a, 3)` and fill `[b, a]` from `[a, b]`, as the three loops above it do; the returned `frozen_drho`, `moved_drho` and both `becsum` object arrays must be bit-identical.
- against the record: Distinct from OPEN.md H2, which is `strain.py:507` -- the per-iteration *kernel* loop inside `_self_consistent_response`, where nine `jax.jvp` of `v_of_rho` run on a `symmetrised` array whose `[a,b]` and `[b,a]` entries are equal. This is a different loop in a different function, run once per response rather than once per iteration, and its per-call work is a density build rather than a potential linearisation. Grepped `_frozen_density_response` in all four documents: no hit.

### defumat/response/electrostriction.py:425 -- `_second_order_energy_at`

**Three sites in the second-order energy take three separate `jax.jvp` calls at the same primal point for three different tangents, so two of every three primals -- including a full `sum_band` over the whole k axis -- are computed and thrown away.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `piezo-electrostriction`
- mechanism: At :398 `dbecsum` is three `jax.jvp(lambda states: mixed_becsum(moved, states, weights), (psi,), (pcu[axis],))`, at :425 `drho` is three `jax.jvp(raw_density, (psi,), (pcu[axis],))`, and at :457 `kernel` is three `jax.jvp(lambda r: moved.potential(r).v_scf, (rho,), (drho[axis],))`. Every one of them shares its primal argument with the other two and keeps only `[1]`. `raw_density` is the expensive one: it calls `sum_band` over the whole k axis and all occupied bands, then `to_dense` and `moved.augmented`, and it calls `mixed_becsum(moved, states, weights)` a second time -- which `parts` at the head of the function has already computed at the same point. `jax.linearize` evaluates the primal once and applies the tangent map three times. Nothing is jitted around this: `susceptibility_strain_derivative` calls `jax.jvp(epsilon, ...)` directly, so there is no XLA CSE across the three calls, and because that outer `jvp` is live each discarded primal is a primal-plus-outer-tangent pair rather than a primal alone.
- reached by: Every `electrostriction` run; the `mixed_becsum` half of it doubles again on a PAW dataset, where the one-centre branch at :398 runs at all.
- worth: Per call: two wasted `sum_band` (nk x nbnd smooth-grid FFTs plus `to_dense` and `augmented` on the dense grid), two wasted `v_of_rho` on the dense grid, and on PAW two wasted `mixed_becsum` (calbec over nk x nbnd x nkb), plus the fourth `mixed_becsum` that `parts` and `raw_density(psi)` duplicate between them. `_second_order_energy_at` runs six times per tensor, once per Voigt strain, each under the outer `jvp`. It stops mattering on a cell with few k-points and a small dense grid, where the solves rather than the assemblies dominate.
- how to confirm: Rewrite the three sites with `jax.linearize` and assert the returned `(3, 3, 3, 3)` is bit-identical on `tests/data/qe/si-electrostriction.in`; time the warm second call of `electrostriction(..., elastic=False)` before and after, and read the `sum_band` line of `tools/benchmark.py` to bound what two of six are worth.
- against the record: Extends OPEN.md H4, which names `phononq.py:464`, `phonon.py:621`, `efield.py:523` and `strain.py:507` and does not name `electrostriction.py`. It is the larger site of the family because what is duplicated here is `sum_band` and `mixed_becsum`, not only `v_of_rho`. Grepped `_second_order_energy_at`, `raw_density` and `second_order_energy` in OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md and PERFORMANCE.md: no hit.

### defumat/response/piezo.py:624 -- `constraint_strain_term`

**The `add_for_charges` sandwich is rebuilt once per field direction, so the strained calculation and the whole `(nk, npwx, nkb)` projector array are differentiated eighteen times per tensor where six would do.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `piezo-electrostriction`
- mechanism: `constraint_strain_term` takes one `commutator` and runs six `jax.jvp(sandwich, (zero,), (strain_tangent(a, b),))` at :690, one per independent strain. Inside `sandwich`, `moved = calculation.at_strain(strain)`, `vkb = moved.projectors.vkb` and `qq` are all independent of `commutator`; so is `left`, which contracts `vkb` against `occupied`. Only `right` reads the commutator. But the function is called from inside the per-axis loop of `clamped_ion_piezoelectric` (:845) and again from inside the per-axis loop of `piezoelectric_zstar_eu_style` (:1058), so the strained rebuild and its forward tangent are paid three times over. Stacking the three commutators on a leading axis of `sandwich` and contracting `left.conj()`, `qq` and the stacked `right` in one einsum would return a `(3,)` result from the same six jvps.
- reached by: An ultrasoft or PAW dataset -- the function returns `np.zeros((3, 3))` immediately when `not calculation.is_ultrasoft`, so a norm-conserving run pays nothing. Both routes are affected, `method='autodiff'` and `method='zstar_eu'` alike.
- worth: Twelve avoidable `at_strain` rebuilds per tensor, each carrying the forward tangent of `vkb`, which is `(nk, npwx, nkb)` complex, plus the radial and reciprocal-space tables `at_strain` regenerates when the cell moves. It scales with nk x npwx x nkb and with ngm through the radial transforms, and it stops mattering when the Sternheimer solves dominate, which is the few-projector, many-band regime.
- how to confirm: Add a `(3, ...)` leading axis to `commutator` in `sandwich`, return `(3, 3, 3)` in one pass, and assert the tensor is unchanged to round-off on `tests/data/qe/alas-piezo-tiny.in`; time the warm second `piezoelectric_tensor` call at both `method` settings before and after.
- against the record: Adjacent to MEMORY-AUDIT.md's "vkb materialised for every k-point outside k_batch", which is about the array's size and not about repeating its derivative. Grepped `constraint_strain_term` in OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md and PERFORMANCE.md: the term is discussed as physics (OPEN.md around 4049 and the `_multiplier_strain_term` entries) and its threefold repetition is not recorded.

### defumat/response/electrostriction.py:382 -- `_second_order_energy_at`

**`S|psi>` is applied over the whole k axis twice in every evaluation of the second-order energy, because `_project_conduction` rebuilds the array the caller has already built four lines above it.**

The cost is real on ultrasoft and PAW, where `apply_s` is a projector contraction (`operator.py:236-239`); on a norm-conserving dataset `apply_s` short-circuits at `has_overlap` and the waste is a masked copy of `(nspin, nk, nocc, npwx)` walked through `map_k`.
- worth: One extra `S|psi>` per evaluation: nk x nocc x npwx x nkb on an augmented dataset, and a full wavefunction-sized copy otherwise. `_second_order_energy_at` runs six times per tensor under the outer `jvp`, so twelve applications where six would do. It stops mattering once nkb is small relative to nbnd, which is the norm-conserving case.

### defumat/response/piezo.py:1382 -- `piezoelectric_tensor`

**Under `method='zstar_eu'` the three field perturbations are built twice and the first three are discarded unread.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `piezo-electrostriction`
- mechanism: :1382 builds `_perturbations` unconditionally, one `_bare_plus_induced` per cartesian direction. :1400, inside `if method == "zstar_eu":`, builds the identical three again as `perturbations` from the same `internals["solver"]`, `internals["bare"]`, `internals["dvscf"]` and `internals["onecentre"]`, and passes those. `_perturbations` is read only by the `else` branch at the end of the function, so on the `zstar_eu` path it is dead. - worth: Three dense-to-smooth FFT interpolations per spin channel, plus, on an augmented dataset, three `jvp`s of `newd` -- which contracts `Q_ij(G)` over ngm for every atom and every `nh^2` pair, so natom x nh^2 x ngm. Small beside a Sternheimer solve and free to remove; it stops mattering on a norm-conserving run, where only the FFTs are paid.

### defumat/response/shg.py:630 -- `second_harmonic`

**The frequency axis is pinned to float64 by `np.asarray(frequencies, dtype=float)`, so the whole frequency contraction promotes to complex128 whatever `config.dtypes` says -- while `conductivity.py:572`, doing the same job, casts to `precision.complex`.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `sum-over-states`
- mechanism: Line 630 is `frequencies = jnp.asarray(np.asarray(frequencies, dtype=float))`, and x64 is enabled package-wide, so the array stays float64 regardless of the precision policy. It is then used at lines 495-496 inside `_chi_at_k` to build `zv1` and `zv2`, whose other operands (`d.e` from the eigenvalues, `eta = precision.as_real(broadening)`) are policy-typed: float32 + float64 promotes to float64, and the `1j` makes the result complex128. The `(nw, nb, nb)` resonance arrays and both `einsum` contractions at lines 515-516 then run in double. `photocurrent.py:467` is the identical line, feeding `argument` and the two smeared deltas at lines 388-391. The correct form is two lines away in the same subsystem: `conductivity.py:572` builds `zomega` and ends it `.astype(precision.complex)`.
- reached by: Any float32 precision policy. Under the default float64 policy nothing changes, which is why no test would show it.
- worth: Memory and time in the frequency contraction scale with `nw x nbnd^2` per k-point in flight, and float32 mode buys nothing there -- the arrays stay double-sized and the einsums stay double-precision. It stops mattering the moment the assembly is not the bottleneck, which `PERFORMANCE.md:4462` says is the usual case for SHG (2.3 s of 22.6, the rest being `dH/dk` and the NSCF), so this is a blocked capability rather than a measured loss. No number changes in float64.
- how to confirm: Print `frequencies.dtype` and `zv1.dtype` inside `_chi_at_k` under a float32 policy, or diff shg.py:630 and photocurrent.py:467 against conductivity.py:570-572.
- against the record: Extends AUDIT-2026-09-18's dtype note -- it fixed one literal in `response/sternheimer.py` and recorded that 119 remain, with the rule that a dtype finding is only worth making when it is inside a differentiated or jitted path and would change a number or block float32. This one is inside `sum_k` and blocks float32. The specific sites are not named there; I grepped `shg`, `second_harmonic`, `photocurrent` and `dtype` across all four documents.

### defumat/projwfc/angular_momentum.py:333 -- `_site_density_matrix`

**The site density matrix is built over the whole `natomwfc x natomwfc` block when `_contract` reads only the per-shell diagonal blocks, and the projector array is round-tripped through numpy and conjugated once per spin component, holding three or four host copies of `(nk, npwx, natomwfc)`.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `hubbard-tddft-projwfc`
- mechanism: Line 333's `np.einsum('kb,kbi,kbj->ij', band_weights[a], coefficients[a], coefficients[b].conj())` fills all `natomwfc^2` entries of `density`, at a cost of `nk nbnd natomwfc^2` per spin block. `_contract` (lines 380-392) then reads only `density[np.ix_(indices, [0,1], indices, [0,1])]` for each `(atom, wfc, l)` group returned by `_shells`, that is only the block-diagonal `sum_shells (2l+1)^2` entries, because `L` connects neither two `l` nor two radial shells on an orthonormal set -- which line 396's docstring says in as many words. So the useful fraction is about `ldim/natomwfc`, roughly one part in a hundred on a large cell. Separately, line 244 wraps `build_atomic_projectors(...)` in `np.asarray`, forcing a host copy of the `(nk, npwx, natomwfc)` complex array that was just built on device, and lines 300 and 307 then evaluate `projectors.conj()` -- a full numpy copy -- once per spin component before pushing it back to device inside `jnp.einsum`, and that einsum walks the entire k axis at once rather than through `map_k`/`calculation.k_batch`. The sibling `projwfc/projections.py:337-340` keeps the array on device and goes through `map_k`, so the right pattern already exists next door.
- reached by: Any `get_angular_momenta()` call; it bites with cell size. Harmless at the nickel case PERFORMANCE.md records (`natomwfc = 18`), costly once `natomwfc` reaches the hundreds -- a 100-atom transition-metal cell or a slab.
- worth: Compute scales as `nk nbnd natomwfc^2`, i.e. quadratic in natom where the consumer is linear: about a hundredfold more arithmetic than the contraction needs on a cell with `natomwfc` in the low thousands, which turns seconds into minutes. Memory scales as `nk npwx natomwfc` per copy, and there are three to four live: 13 GB per copy on a 157-atom slab at `npwx ~ 1.5e5`, `natomwfc ~ 1400`, `nk = 4`. Both stop mattering below about `natomwfc = 50`.
- how to confirm: Read `angular_momentum.py:244`, `:300`, `:307`, `:333` against `_contract` at `:380-392` and `_shells` at `:396-415`, and against `projwfc/projections.py:337-340`, which does the same gather through `map_k` on a device array. To measure: time `get_angular_momenta()` warm on a cell with `natomwfc` near 200 against one near 20 at fixed `nk` and `nbnd`, and watch `%M` on the first.
- against the record: extends PERFORMANCE.md:3966, which records the contraction as 'a few einsums on (18, 2, 18, 2)' and attributes the 1.2-1.4 s to the orbital build -- true at `natomwfc = 18` and silent about the scaling; the copies and the un-batched k walk are not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `_site_density_matrix`, `angular_moment`, `build_atomic_projectors`)

### defumat/ultracell/grid.py:250 -- `Ultracell.g2`

**`|G+Q|^2` on the ultracell box is built through two full `(*box, 3)` float64 intermediates, about ten times the size of its own result, and `run_ultracell` evaluates it twice.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `ultracell-io-sizing`
- mechanism: `np.meshgrid(..., indexing="ij")` at `:251` returns three full `(m1, m2, m3)` arrays, 24 B per box point, and `np.stack(..., axis=-1)` at `:250` allocates a fourth `(m1, m2, m3, 3)` of the same 24 B/point while they are still alive. `cartesian = fractional @ bg` at `:254` allocates another 24 B/point with `fractional` still referenced, and `np.sum(cartesian ** 2, axis=-1)` at `:255` materialises the square before reducing. Peak is roughly 72 to 80 B per box point against an 8 B/point result. The box is `N` times the unit cell's dense grid, so this is host-side numpy at `N * prod(dense_grid)`. It is called twice in `run_ultracell`: `hartree_kernel` at `driver.py:1028` does `ultracell.g2(cell)` and `box_kerker` at `driver.py:1086` does it again at `mixing.py:64`, so the whole build happens a second time on a fresh set of intermediates.
- reached by: Every `run_ultracell` call, before the first iteration; the size is set by the ultracell FFT box, so it bites when `N` is large -- which is the regime the method exists for. `run_ultracell`'s own docstring names a `10^4`-cell ultracell as the wall a one-dimensional modulation hits first, at 6.4 GB for the density.
- worth: Scales with the FFT grid (`N * prod(dense_grid)`), not with `nk`, `nbnd` or `ngm`. At the docstring's `10^4`-cell case with `nspin_mag = 4` (about 2e8 box points) the transient is roughly 16 GB against a 1.6 GB result, twice, and it lands before any iteration has run -- so a run can die in setup on a box whose iteration would have fitted. At the sizes actually measured so far (a 21-cell slab, a few million box points) it is a few hundred MB and does not matter. `|G+Q|^2` is separable: accumulating the three cartesian components one at a time from `(m1,1,1)`/`(1,m2,1)`/`(1,1,m3)` broadcasts holds at most two arrays of the result's size, and computing it once in `run_ultracell` and passing it to both `hartree_kernel` and `box_kerker` removes the second build entirely.
- how to confirm: Read `grid.py:245-255` and count the live arrays; then `driver.py:1028` and `mixing.py:64` for the two call sites. To measure, wrap `Ultracell.g2` in `tracemalloc` or `/usr/bin/time -v` on `run_ultracell` for a supercell of `(40, 1, 1)` on the silicon cell of `tests/data/qe/si-ultracell.in` and watch host RSS before the first iteration.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (MEMORY-AUDIT.md contains no occurrence of `ultracell` at all; `box_kerker` appears once in OPEN.md, at Part VI item 3, about the rigid spin rotation and not about cost)

### defumat/io/elk_density.py:282 -- `_find_muffin_tin`

**The 27-image sphere search allocates a `(n_open, 27, 3)` float64 array for every atom in a Python loop, and because most grid points are interstitial the loop never shrinks.**

- confidence: certain, subsystem: `ultracell-io-sizing`
- mechanism: `offsets = (cart[open_][:, None, :] - centres[ias]) + shifts[None, :, :]` at `:282` is 27 * 3 * 8 = 648 B for every still-open point, and `r2` at `:283` adds another 216 B/point. `open_` is cleared at `:293` only for points that landed inside atom `ias`'s sphere, and the muffin tins cover a small fraction of the cell, so `open_.any()` stays true and `n_open` stays near the full grid for all `natmtot` passes. The whole thing runs once over the full target grid at `density_on:620` and again over the inside-points subset inside `evaluate_at:477` (reached from `density_on:623`), and a third and fourth time whenever `characteristic_function`/`sharp_interstitial_charge` are called.
- reached by: `ElkState.density_on` on any calculation, and `evaluate_at` on a grid; the peak is set by the dense FFT grid the seed is reconstructed on and by `natmtot`.
- worth: Scales with the dense FFT grid times `natom`. On a 60^3 dense grid with four atoms it is about 140 MB of host transient per atom pass and four passes; on a 150^3 grid with ten atoms it is about 2.2 GB per pass, ten passes, plus the second call. Time is `O(natmtot * npoints * 27)` in numpy. It stops mattering on the small hydrogen-like cells this path currently refuses anything but -- `density_on:575` refuses `nspin_mag != 1` and `:649` refuses a state whose integral is more than half `nelec` away, i.e. anything with a core -- so today it is a seconds-to-tens-of-seconds and hundreds-of-MB cost rather than a wall, and it becomes one if the core-difference route named in that refusal is ever written. Chunking the point axis, as `evaluate_at:506` already chunks the interstitial Fourier sum, bounds it at a constant without changing a number.

### defumat/scf/driver.py:5549 -- `run_scf (the Hubbard occupation_matrix call inside the attempt loop)`

**A DFT+U run contracts the wavefunctions against the Hubbard projectors after the store has already been parked off the accelerator, so every SCF iteration pays a full-set transfer back -- or fails on an incompatible memory kind -- where the dial exists to avoid exactly that.**

- mechanism: park_wavefunctions(wavefunctions, wfc_store) at :5527 rebinds the store, and with wfc_store='host' returns jax.device_put(psi, pinned-host sharding) (batching.py:705-724). The comment directly above it names that line "the last read of the store in this iteration" and lists what runs afterwards -- the energy terms, v_of_rho, the mixer, PAW's one-centre terms, the checkpoint write -- none of which touches the wavefunctions. But :5549, twenty lines below the park and inside the same attempt loop, calls calculation.occupation_matrix(wavefunctions, wg), which is occupation_matrix(self.wfcU, wavefunctions, weights, ...) at :1908 and contracts <wfcU|psi> over the whole (nspin, nk, nbnd, npol npwx) set. Nothing fetches it first: fetch_wavefunctions appears only at :5489, above the diagonalisation, and at :6052 in the result. resolve_wfc_store defaults to 'host' on every backend that is not CPU (batching.py:637), so this is the accelerator default rather than an opt-in, and on CPU park_wavefunctions returns psi untouched, which is why the hazard is invisible here.
- reached by: A run with a HUBBARD card on an accelerator, or on any backend with DEFUMAT_WFC_STORE=host. Invisible on CPU, where the park is a no-op by construction.
- worth: One round trip of nspin x nk x nbnd x npol x npwx complex per SCF iteration -- 2.19 GB per k-point on the 45-atom NiBr2 shapes MEMORY-AUDIT uses -- or an outright error on a backend that refuses a pinned-host input to a jitted computation. It scales with nk, nbnd and npwx and stops mattering wherever the store already lives on the device.
- how to confirm: On a GPU, run a DFT+U cell with DEFUMAT_WFC_STORE=host and bracket the iteration with a transfer or timing counter, or simply assert getattr(wavefunctions.sharding, 'memory_kind', None) inside the is_hubbard branch. Reading :5527 against :5549, with batching.py:705-742, establishes the ordering without running anything; what a pinned-host input does to a jitted contraction on each backend is the part a read cannot settle.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- park_wavefunctions and fetch_wavefunctions appear in none of the four documents, and MEMORY-AUDIT:2248's occupation_matrix entry is about the k_batch signature default, not the store.

**The stress is computed from the parked wavefunction store without fetching it, where the SCFResult two statements later does fetch, so tstress on an accelerator differentiates through a host-resident set.**

### defumat/scf/ewald.py:200 -- `_real_kernel (with build_ewald:119)`

**The Ewald real-space neighbour list is sized by the whole cell's body diagonal rather than by the atoms' own extent, so on a vacuum-padded slab `_real_kernel` materialises a `(nat, nat, ntrans, 3)` array whose entries are almost all masked to exactly zero.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `scf-body`
- mechanism: `build_ewald:119` sets `radius = rmax + pair_separation_bound(at, positions)`, and `pair_separation_bound` (system/cell.py) returns the longest body diagonal of the cell, deliberately, so the list survives a relaxation. `lattice_translations` then returns every lattice vector inside that radius, roughly `(4/3) pi D^3 / V`, which for a slab with `c >> a` is about `4.19 c^2 / a^2`. `_real_kernel:200` broadcasts `tau[:, None, None, :] - tau[None, :, None, :] + translations[None, None, :, :]` over that whole list. The structural proof of the waste is the mask two lines below: `keep = (square > 1e-16) & (square <= rmax**2)` with `rmax = 4/sqrt(alpha)`, about 2.4 bohr, so a translation can contribute only if it brings some pair within 2.4 bohr, meaning only translations shorter than (the atoms' actual maximum separation + 2.4) ever survive. On `benchmarks/al-slab.in` (5.303, 5.303, 31.0 bohr, 16 bohr of vacuum) the diagonal is 31.9 and the list is roughly 180 vectors where the atoms span 15 bohr in z and about 40 suffice; the ratio is the square of the vacuum fraction, so it grows fast. The same list is shared with `vdw/grimme.py`, which cites this kernel's shape verbatim.
- reached by: Any cell whose atoms occupy a small fraction of it: a slab, an adsorbate, a molecule in a box. It is worst where `c/a` is largest, and it does not appear on a bulk cell, where the diagonal is the right bound anyway.
- worth: Scales as `natom^2 * ntrans` in both memory and time, with `ntrans ~ 4.19 (c/a)^2` for a slab. On a 45-atom cell with about 200 bohr of vacuum and a 7-bohr in-plane lattice the list is a few thousand vectors where a few tens are live, and `separations` alone is about 150 MB of float64, present on the forward path and again on the reverse tape of every force (`driver.py:2472`, `at_positions`) and every stress (`driver.py:2706`, `at_strain`). It stops mattering on a bulk cell and on anything with fewer than about ten atoms.
- how to confirm: Print `len(calculation.ewald_sum.translations)` and `np.count_nonzero(keep)` for `benchmarks/al-slab.in` and for a slab with 200 bohr of vacuum, and compare against `lattice_translations(at, rmax + max_pairwise_separation(positions))`. The memory side is one `jax.jit(...).lower(...).compile().memory_analysis()` of `forces.forces` on the same two cells.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md. PERFORMANCE.md:454 records the `(nat, nat, ntranslations)` broadcast, but as a win against a Python double loop over atom pairs and with no mention of how the translation count is chosen or what it costs on a slab; the module docstring's own forecast, 'a few dozen translations rather than a few', is the sentence this contradicts.

### defumat/scf/tetrahedra.py:99 -- `BISECTION_STEPS`

**The tetrahedron Fermi search runs 200 bisection steps where the bracket has already collapsed to adjacent float64 values after about 55, so roughly three quarters of the occupation step's work each SCF iteration is provably a no-op.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `scf-occ-mag`
- mechanism: `_bisect` (line 386) brackets with the global min and max of the corner energies and runs `jax.lax.fori_loop(0, 200, step, ...)` with no early exit, which is deliberate and is what keeps it on device. The bracket halves exactly once per step, so from a span of order 1 to 100 Ry it reaches the float64 spacing at those energies after about 53 halvings; from there `0.5 * (low + high)` rounds to `low` or `high`, the `jnp.where` reassigns the same pair, and every later step is a fixed point. The docstring at line 96 states the premise itself -- "200 halvings put the bracket far below float64 resolution, so the result is the exact root of the discretised count and does not depend on the number" -- which is exactly the statement that the tail steps cannot change the answer. Each of those steps is not cheap: `integrated_states` calls `_occupied_fraction` on the whole `(nspin, ntetra, nbnd, 4)` sorted-corner array, and that function evaluates all three energy branches unconditionally before selecting with two nested `jnp.where`, so it is of order ten temporaries of `(nspin, ntetra, nbnd)` per step. `ntetra` is 6 nk1 nk2 nk3 on the **full** grid, not the wedge. The driver calls this every iteration (driver.py:4444).
- reached by: Every `occupations = 'tetrahedra'`, `'tetrahedra-lin'` or `'tetrahedra-opt'` SCF run, at every iteration; worst on a dense grid, which is what the method is chosen for.
- worth: Scales with ntetra x nbnd x nspin. On a 20^3 grid at nbnd = 30 and nspin = 2 the array is 2 x 48000 x 30 = 2.9M elements, so 200 steps move of order 460 GB through memory per Fermi search where 64 steps would move 150 GB, and the two must agree bit for bit. The same constant in defumat/scf/occupations.py:196 is the cheaper sibling -- its count is over `(nk, nbnd)` rather than over tetrahedra -- but that path compounds it differently: for Methfessel-Paxton and cold smearing `_bisect_fermi` (line 360-366) always evaluates the true-function fallback bisection as well as the Gaussian guess, because both arms of a `jnp.where` run under jit, so it pays 400 count evaluations plus 100 Newton steps of three autodiff evaluations each whether or not the refinement succeeded.
- how to confirm: Assert inside `_bisect` that `(low, high)` at step 64 equals `(low, high)` at step 200 on `al-tetrahedra`, or run `tetrahedron_occupations_spin` on that cell at `BISECTION_STEPS = 200` and at 64 and diff `ef` bitwise; then time the pair with the compilation already paid.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `BISECTION_STEPS`, `efermit` and `bisect` across all four. PERFORMANCE.md:450 records moving the *occupations* Fermi bisection on-device ("200 host round trips") and PERFORMANCE.md:3779-3810 flags the Becke-Roussel 80-step fixed-length bisection as worth revisiting; neither is this loop and neither questions the step count.

### defumat/scf/tetrahedra.py:757 -- `tetrahedron_occupations_spin`

**The spin path builds the sorted corner energies twice per channel per SCF iteration, once eagerly for the Fermi bisection and once inside a separate jit for the weights, with no compilation boundary that lets XLA notice they are the same.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `scf-occ-mag`
- mechanism: `tetrahedron_occupations_spin` is a plain Python function. Its unconstrained branch calls `_stacked_corners` (line 709), which runs `_sorted_corners` per channel -- the `(ntetra, nntetra, nbnd)` gather `eigenvalues[tetra.corners]`, the einsum against `wlsm`, an `argsort` over the length-4 axis and a `take_along_axis` -- outside any jit, and stacks both channels so `(nspin, ntetra, nbnd, 4)` is alive at once. It hands that to `_bisect`, then at line 763 calls `tetrahedron_weights_at` per channel, which is its own `@jax.jit` and whose first act (line 653) is `_sorted_corners` on the same eigenvalues. XLA's CSE cannot reach across the two, because the first evaluation happened in eager dispatch and the second is a separate compiled unit that takes only `(tetra, eigenvalues, weights, ef)`. The non-spin `tetrahedron_occupations` (line 677-681) has the same duplication textually but is one `@jax.jit` with the weights call inlined, so CSE probably covers it there and I am not claiming that path. The driver reaches only the spin entry point (driver.py:4444), including at nspin = 1.
- reached by: Every tetrahedron SCF run, every iteration, both the constrained and the unconstrained branch. Worst for `occupations = 'tetrahedra-opt'`, where `nntetra` is 20 rather than 4 and the gather is five times larger.
- worth: The duplicated gather is ntetra x nntetra x nbnd doubles per channel: on a 20^3 grid at nbnd = 30 that is 48000 x 20 x 30 x 8 = 230 MB built twice per channel per iteration, with the argsort over it each time. It stops mattering on a coarse grid or a small nbnd, where the gather is under a few MB and the eager dispatch overhead dominates instead. Hoisting `e_sorted` and `order` out of `tetrahedron_weights_at` into an argument -- which is how `tetra.f90` splits it, `efermit` and `opt_tetra_weights_only` both taking the sorted corners -- removes the second build and lets the whole spin path be one jit.
- how to confirm: Count the ops: `jax.make_jaxpr` on a wrapper that inlines `tetrahedron_occupations_spin` and grep for `sort`; there should be one per channel and there are two. Or time `tetrahedron_occupations_spin` on `al-tetrahedra` warm against a hand-hoisted variant that passes `e_sorted`/`order` through.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `_stacked_corners`, `_sorted_corners` and `tetrahedron_occupations` across all four. OPEN.md B2 touches `_average_degenerate` in the same function's callee and is about weight conservation, not cost; PERFORMANCE.md:1297-1335 times the tetrahedron DOS and not the occupation call.

### defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py:263 -- `_species_integrals`

**`_species_integrals` conjugates the resident `(nh, nh, ngm)` augmentation table, which materialises a full second copy of the largest array in the run -- the same doubling `MEMORY-AUDIT` A9 removed from `_species_charge` and did not look for here.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
- mechanism: The contraction is `jnp.einsum("ijg,ag->aij", jnp.conj(qgm), shifted)` with `shifted` of shape `(nat, ngm)`. `jnp.conj` is an elementwise producer feeding a `dot_general` operand, so XLA has to hand the dot a contiguous buffer and the conjugated `(nh, nh, ngm)` array is materialised beside the resident one. It is algebraically free to move the conjugation to the small operand: `sum_g conj(Q_ijg) S_ag = conj(sum_g Q_ijg conj(S_ag))`, and since this routine takes `jnp.real` of the result the outer conjugation drops out entirely, leaving `volume * jnp.real(jnp.einsum("ijg,ag->aij", qgm, jnp.conj(shifted)))` -- identical arithmetic with the conjugation on `(nat, ngm)` instead of `(nh, nh, ngm)`. `_species_cross_integrals` (:270) is the same expression and needs the outer conjugation, but on its `(nat, nh, nh)` result rather than on the table. This is `OPEN.md` M4's shape (`calbec` conjugates the large operand) in a routine M4 does not name, and it is the exact cost A9 measured and fixed one method above.
- reached by: any stored-route ultrasoft or PAW run, that is every augmented cell under `AUG_MAX_BYTES`; `_newd` (driver.py:501-511) calls `integrals` once per potential component, so `nspin_mag` times per SCF iteration -- four times on a noncollinear run
- worth: scales as `nh^2 * ngm` per distinct dataset, the same product the `AUG_MAX_BYTES` gate is written against, so by construction up to 2 GiB and on `bismuthene-soc-small` the 1.04 GiB `MEMORY-AUDIT` A9 records for that cell's `qgm`. It stops mattering on the tabulated route, where `qgm` is `()`, and on a small cell where the table is tens of MB. Time cost is one extra pass over the table per call on top of the dot.
- how to confirm: the instrument A9 used: compile `jit(_species_integrals)` on the bismuthene-soc-small shapes and read `memory_analysis().temp_size_in_bytes`, before and after moving the conjugation onto `shifted`, and check the returned `deeq` is bit-identical. Reading settles the algebra; only the compile settles whether XLA:CPU fuses the conj into the dot operand.
- against the record: extends MEMORY-AUDIT.md A9 (`_species_charge` builds a second full-size `(nh, nh, ngm)` beside the resident one -- marked **done**, and scoped to `charge` alone) and OPEN.md M4 (`calbec` conjugates the large operand). Grepped for `_species_integrals` and `jnp.conj(qgm)` across OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md, PERFORMANCE.md and AUGMENTATION-NEXT.md: zero hits in all five.

### defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py:728 -- `_tabulated_charge`

**The tabulated route's scan body contracts `becsum` into the structure factor first -- the association `AugmentationCharge.charge` documents as rejected -- so it holds a second `(nh, nh, chunk)` array beside the one `build` just made, on exactly the path that is chosen because memory is scarce.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
- mechanism: The body does `weighted = jnp.einsum("aij,ac->ijc", becsum, phase_chunk)` (:728) and then `jnp.einsum("ijc,ijc->c", build(gcart_chunk), weighted)` (:729). `weighted` is `(nh, nh, chunk)` and has to be live at the same moment as `build`'s own `(nh, nh, chunk)` output. `AugmentationCharge.charge`'s docstring (:139-161) argues against precisely this order and `_species_charge` (:250-254) takes the other one -- `einsum("aij,ijg->ag", becsum, qgm)` then `einsum("ag,ag->g", channels, phases)` -- whose intermediate is `(nat, ngm)`, smaller by `nh^2 / nat`. The same rewrite applies here unchanged: contract `becsum` with `build(gcart_chunk)` into `(nat, chunk)` and multiply by `phase_chunk`. The leading flop count is identical (`nat * nh^2 * chunk` either way) and the second contraction drops from `nh^2 * chunk` to `nat * chunk`. `_aug_chunk` (:485-495) sizes the chunk so that `nh^2 * chunk * 16` lands near 256 MB, so the extra array is a quarter of a gigabyte by construction, and the docstring there -- "the intermediate this decides is `(nh, nh, chunk)`" -- describes one of the two that exist. The `jax.checkpoint` on the body means the pair is built twice per derivative, once forward and once in the recompute.
- reached by: any cell that takes the tabulated route, that is `nh^2 * ngm * itemsize > DEFUMAT_AUG_MAX_BYTES` (2 GiB) summed over distinct datasets -- the vacuum-padded slabs the route exists for, and anything run with `DEFUMAT_AUG_MAX_BYTES` lowered
- worth: the per-chunk working set roughly halves: the intermediate goes from `nh^2 * chunk` to `nat * chunk`, a factor `nh^2 / nat` -- 77 for the 45-atom NiBr2 slab's Ni dataset (`nh = 34`, `nat = 15`), where `_aug_chunk` gives `chunk = 8192` and the array is about 151 MB against 2 MB. It scales with `nh^2` and the chunk size and is independent of `ngm`, so it does not grow with the cell, and it stops mattering entirely on the stored route.
- how to confirm: compile `jit(TabulatedAugmentation.charge)` on the NiBr2 or `si8-us-1k` shapes with `DEFUMAT_AUG_MAX_BYTES=1` and read `memory_analysis().temp_size_in_bytes` before and after the reassociation, with the returned `rho_aug` compared to one ulp. The shape claim itself is settled by reading :728-729 against :250-254.
- against the record: extends MEMORY-AUDIT.md A1, which covers `_tabulated_charge`'s scan **residual** (`(nchunks, nh, nh, chunk)`, fixed by the `jax.checkpoint` now at :719) and not the forward intermediate, and MEMORY-AUDIT.md A9, which made this same reassociation in `_species_charge` alone. Neither names `weighted` or the tabulated body's association.

### defumat/pseudo/upf.py:291 -- `read_upf`

**`read_upf` has no memoisation, so an input that writes one species per magnetic site re-parses the same multi-megabyte UPF once per label and keeps that many independent copies of its radial tables resident.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
- mechanism: `calculator.py:13` and the five CLI entry points build `tuple(read_upf(pseudo_dir / s.pseudo_file) for s in system.structure.species)`, one call per species label with no cache anywhere between. Each call runs `path.read_bytes()`, a regex substitution over the whole file (`_upf_document`, :263-266), an `ET.fromstring` of the result, and then `_numbers` on every section, which does two full string copies (`.replace("D", "E").replace("d", "e")`, :521) before `np.fromstring`. The `Pseudopotential` objects that come out are distinct, so `qfuncl`, `r`, `rab`, `vloc`, `rho_atom` and the PAW partial waves are held once per label rather than once per file. That this duplication is real at the scale that matters is not my inference -- `_dataset_key` (augmentation.py:780-796) exists to catch it one layer further down, and its own docstring says "a fifteen-site helix arrives here as fifteen identical datasets". The deduplication there covers `Q_ij(G)` only; `build_paw` (paw/onecenter.py:731-737) likewise builds `_build_species` once per label, off these fifteen separate objects.
- reached by: a noncollinear or spiral input whose `angle1`/`angle2` force one `ATOMIC_SPECIES` entry per magnetic site -- the standard way to write such an input, and the shape `_dataset_key` was written for; also any sweep that rebuilds a `Calculator` from file per geometry
- worth: scales with the number of species labels, not with `nat`, `nk` or `ngm`. Per duplicate label it is one full XML parse of the file (several MB of text through ElementTree, host-side, before any physics starts) and one resident copy of the radial arrays, of which `qfuncl` dominates at `nbeta^2 * nqlc * mesh * 8` -- about 6.7 MB for a `nbeta = 10`, `nqlc = 7`, `mesh = 1200` nickel dataset, so roughly 100 MB of host memory and a dozen parses for a fifteen-site helix. It is setup cost only and stops mattering entirely on an input with one label per element.
- how to confirm: time `Calculator.from_file` on a fifteen-label helix input against the same cell written with one label, with no SCF -- the difference is the parse. For the memory half, sum `sum(a.nbytes for a in (p.r, p.rab, p.vloc, p.augmentation.qfuncl))` over the returned tuple and compare against the number of distinct `path` values. An `lru_cache` keyed on the resolved path and its mtime is the fix; keying on the path alone is what `_dataset_key`'s docstring warns against.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md -- grepped `read_upf` across all four, zero hits. MEMORY-AUDIT's "Calculator/run_scf host-side retention" item is about converged states and wavefunctions, not about the pseudopotential objects.

### defumat/system/builder.py:601 -- `System._respin_kpoints`

**A change of spin regime re-runs `expand_to_subgroup` from the lattice point group on a k-list `build_system` has already expanded, so the set is split a second time and carries duplicate representatives of the same orbits.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `system-builder`
- mechanism: `build_system` (line 1099) expands an explicit `K_POINTS` list from the lattice's point group to the crystal's, which is `irreducible_BZ`'s job and is right. Line 601 does the same thing again on the *result*, with `lattice_point_group(self.cell.at)` as the source group once more, because `_respin_kpoints` has no way of knowing the list is no longer one representative per lattice orbit. `expand_to_subgroup` (`kpoints.py:241`) works per input point: for each one it takes the whole lattice orbit `full`, resets `assigned`, and emits one representative of every crystal-group sub-orbit of `full` with weight `w * len(sub) / len(full)`. It only ever emits representatives and never merges two inputs that lie in one lattice orbit, so applying it to an already-split list emits the full set of sub-orbit representatives once per input point. The arithmetic is monotone in both directions: a promotion to a smaller group re-splits, and a demotion to a larger group keeps every representative the smaller group produced, so a round trip `nspin = 1` to 4 to 1 on an explicit list ends with the magnetic wedge's point count rather than the nonmagnetic one. The weights are exactly right regardless, which is why nothing catches it: with a lattice orbit of size `N` splitting into `S1` and `S2` under the build-time group and into `T_i` under the target's, the second pass puts `(w_P + w_Q) |T_i| / N = w |T_i| / N` on each `T_i`, which is what one pass would have given.
- reached by: Three conjuncts. An explicit `K_POINTS tpiba` or `crystal` list, which is what `test-suite/pw_scf/scf.in` and most of `tests/data/qe` are written with, since a missing option reads as `tpiba`. A build-time group that is a proper subgroup of the lattice's, which is any crystal whose point group is below its Bravais lattice's and, in particular, any `nspin = 2` run with a moment, where `collinear_symmetries` filters the group. And a regime change to a third group, which is `with_spin` or `with_moments` -- the 2 to 4 promotion the continuation stack exists for. An automatic grid is unaffected, because that branch regenerates the mesh with `KPoints.automatic`.
- worth: Scales with `nk`, multiplied by the number of build-time sub-orbits per lattice orbit, which is bounded by the index of the build-time group in the lattice's, so up to a factor of two or four on the cells this matters for. Every duplicated k-point is a full diagonalisation in every SCF iteration, a full band's worth of wavefunction storage, and a term in every sum over k, so it is paid on the whole run rather than at setup. It stops mattering as soon as the build-time group *is* the lattice's group, where the first expansion was a no-op and the second one is too, and it never costs anything on an automatic grid.
- how to confirm: Host-side, no SCF, and it is a count rather than a number: take a cell whose crystal group is a proper subgroup of its Bravais lattice's, give it an explicit `K_POINTS crystal` list, build it at `nspin = 2` with a `starting_magnetization`, and compare `system.with_spin(4, angle1=(90.0,)).kpoints.nk` against the `nk` of the same input file read straight in at `nspin = 4` with the same angles. The two should agree and the first is larger, and folding the first set by symmetry shows the surplus points are images of ones already present. The fix is to record on `KPoints` which group it was reduced with, the way `reduced` already records *that* it was reduced, and expand from that group rather than from the lattice's.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. `expand_to_subgroup` appears in none of the four. `_respin_kpoints` appears twice and neither is this: OPEN.md:567 is item A6, the three rules for "is this run magnetic", closed 2026-09-11 and about `is_magnetic` alone, and AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:325 is the gamma-storage weight flag.

### defumat/system/symmetry.py:806 -- `symmetry_maps`

**Building the symmetry maps materialises two full `(nsym, ngm, 3)` int64 arrays, about twice the memory of the `(nsym, ngm)` table they produce, and the int64 is inherited from `rotation_array()` rather than chosen.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `symmetry-kpoints`
- mechanism: `gvectors.miller` is int32 (`basis/gvectors.py:186`) but `Symmetries.rotation_array()` is `np.array(..., dtype=int)`, i.e. int64, so `np.einsum("gc,sdc->sgd", miller, rotations)` at line 806 promotes and allocates `(nsym, ngm, 3)` int64. Line 807 then computes `rotated % grid`, a second array of the same shape, while `rotated` is still bound by name, so both are live when the fancy index into `lookup` runs. The same shape appears again on the phase side at line 813: `miller @ translations.T` is `(ngm, nsym)` float64, the `-2j*pi*` multiply makes a complex128 copy, `np.exp` a third, and `jnp.asarray` of the transposed view a fourth. Nothing in the routine works one operation at a time, although it is host-side setup where a 48-iteration loop would cost nothing.
- reached by: Any symmetric run whose dense G set is large: a slab or a large-vacuum cell with `ngm` in the millions and a group of 24 or 48 operations. It is paid once per `Calculation`, at setup, before any wavefunction exists.
- worth: Scales with `nsym * ngm` on the **dense** G set. At S1's sizing (`ngm = 3 536 849`, 48 operations) the two int64 intermediates are 4.07 GB each, about 8 GB transient against the 4.07 GB resident result S1 already prices, and the phase construction adds a similar amount. It moves no peak on the run S1 measures, because this is setup and that run's peak is 32.30 GB at the eigensolver (and that run is a spiral, hence `nosym`, so S1's own caveat carries forward); it binds only where `ngm` is in the millions while the wavefunction working set is under about 8 GB, which is a large-vacuum cell with few bands. Casting the rotations to int32 and building the permutation one operation at a time takes the transient to tens of MB.
- how to confirm: Print `rotated.dtype` and `rotated.nbytes` inside `symmetry_maps` on a slab-sized dense grid, or watch RSS across the single call; `sizing.py` does not model it, which is why it is invisible in the budget.
- against the record: extends OPEN.md Part S item S1 ("The symmetry maps are `(nsym, ngm)` int64 plus `(nsym, ngm)` complex, resident for the run"), which prices the resident table and the per-iteration `(nsym, ngm)` temporary in `apply_symmetry_maps` but not the construction transient or the int64 promotion at line 806

### defumat/paw/symmetry.py:294 -- `BecsumSymmetry.apply_atom_displacement`

**The displacement symmetriser stacks `nsym` whole copies of the per-species `dbecsum` before contracting, where the einsum could gather inside the reduction.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `symmetry-kpoints`
- mechanism: Line 294 builds `gathered = jnp.stack([values[inverse[s]][:, :, :, sources[s]] for s in range(self.nsym)])`, shape `(nsym, nat, 3, nspin, nat_t, nh, nh)`. Unlike the fancy-index forms in `apply`, `apply_directional` and `apply_strain`, this is an explicit `jnp.stack` of `nsym` separately gathered arrays, so the full `nsym`-fold expansion is a real array whatever XLA does with the einsum that follows, and it is the reverse-mode residual as well when the response is differentiated. The comment at line 289 justifies the loop on readability and asserts `nsym` is at most 48, which is exactly the factor being paid.
- reached by: A PAW phonon or displacement response on a symmetric cell with several atoms of one PAW species; it runs once per response iteration per species.
- worth: Scales with `nsym * nat * nat_t * nh^2` and is a pure multiplication of the input by `nsym`. With a scalar-relativistic PAW dataset (`nh` 8 to 18, since the response stack refuses noncollinear PAW and never sees a fully-relativistic `nh` of 34), ten atoms of one species, `nh = 18` and 48 operations, the input block is 10*3*1*10*324 = 972 kB and the stack is 48 times that, about 47 MB per species per call. Tens of MB rather than GB, so it moves a peak only on a cell with many atoms of one PAW species.
- how to confirm: Size `gathered.nbytes` for the cell in hand, or read the compiled module's `memory_analysis()` for the response iteration; the cheap structural check is that the other three `apply_*` methods reach the same average without an explicit `stack`.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `BecsumSymmetry` and `apply_atom_displacement`; the three hits on `BecsumSymmetry` are all the already-fixed `(nsym, nh, nh, nh, nh)` pair operator, a different array)

### defumat/system/kpoints.py:142 -- `irreducible_wedge`

**The orbit walk is a scalar Python loop over grid points, operations and images, and `grid_equivalence` runs the identical loop a second time for the same grid and group.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `symmetry-kpoints`
- mechanism: Lines 142-156 iterate over every grid point, then over every rotation, then over one or two images, calling `grid_index` (line 133) per image. Each call allocates four small NumPy arrays (`rotated * counts - offsets`, `np.rint`, the comparison) and then returns to Python scalars through a generator of `int(x) % n`, so the per-image cost is NumPy dispatch overhead rather than arithmetic. `grid_equivalence` (lines 218-232) repeats the same nested loop with its own copy of `grid_index`, deliberately duplicated per its own docstring, so a DOS or nesting run that needs both the wedge and the fold-back map pays it twice. The whole thing is integer arithmetic on the full grid and vectorises exactly: rotate all points against all operations in one einsum, take the residue, and walk only the assignment.
- reached by: A dense k-grid: a tetrahedron DOS, a nesting function or an optical spectrum on a 32^3 or 48^3 grid, where the complete grid rather than the wedge sets the loop count.
- worth: Scales with `nk * nsym`, where `nk` is the *complete* grid rather than the wedge. Negligible for an SCF grid (a 4x4x4 grid is a few thousand `grid_index` calls), but a 48^3 grid is roughly 110k outer iterations and a few hundred thousand `grid_index` calls per pass, order seconds, doubled when `grid_equivalence` follows. It is setup-only and stops mattering the moment the NSCF behind it costs minutes, which is why it is last here.
- how to confirm: Time `irreducible_wedge((48, 48, 48), (0, 0, 0), rotations)` on a 48-operation group in a fresh process and time `grid_equivalence` with the same arguments beside it; both are pure NumPy and need no JAX, so the measurement is free of the compilation-cache effect.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `irreducible_wedge` and `grid_equivalence`; the AUDIT hit on `irreducible_wedge` is the `is_reduced` weight-spread defect and the PERFORMANCE.md hit on `grid_equivalence` credits it for the 24x unfolding saving, neither about the loop's own cost)

### defumat/response/sternheimer.py:979 -- `_perturbed_coefficients (and paw_response at :1017, SternheimerSolver.__init__ at :292)`

**The ultrasoft and PAW perturbation re-linearises `newd` at a potential that never moves and throws the primal away on every call, and `paw_response` does the identical thing to the PAW one-centre primal, three times per self-consistency iteration each.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `sternheimer-core`
- mechanism: `_perturbed_coefficients` builds `potential = v_scf + as_potential_components(vltot, nspin_mag)` at :1006-1007 -- the converged ground-state potential, fixed for the life of the run -- and then takes `_, int3 = jax.jvp(calculation.coefficients, ...)` at :1009-1013 or :1023-1025, discarding the primal. That primal is exactly the `deeq` the ground-state Hamiltonian was built with, a full `Q_ij(G)` contraction over the dense G set per species per channel. `local_perturbation` calls this every time it is built, and `efield.py:388` builds it once per direction per iteration through `_bare_plus_induced`. `paw_response` at :1017-1035 is the same shape one level down: `jax.jvp(lambda parts: calculation.onecenter(parts)[1], (becsum_,), (dbecsum,))` at a frozen `becsum_`, called at `efield.py:412` three times per iteration, and its primal is the radial Poisson plus Gauss-Legendre quadrature of `_paw_onecenter`, which `PERFORMANCE.md:3740` measures at over 2 s for the fold alone on NiBr2. As an aside on the same duplication, `SternheimerSolver.__init__:292` computes `calculation.onecenter(becsum)[1]` although both callers have just computed it -- `efield.py:305` and `make_sternheimer:1403` -- so every solver construction runs the PAW one-centre twice.
- reached by: Ultrasoft or PAW only; norm-conserving returns `None` at :983 and PAW-less returns `None` at :1030. Reached by the dielectric and Born-charge loops and by every other consumer of `local_perturbation`.
- worth: `3 x n_iter` discarded `newd` primals at `nh^2 x ngm x natom x nspin_mag` each, plus `3 x n_iter` discarded one-centre primals at the cost of a full `PAW_potential`; at the 18 iterations `PERFORMANCE.md` records that is 54 of each. On a PAW cell where the one-centre fold alone is seconds, this is minutes of a run. The fix is `jax.linearize` once at solver construction and applying the stored linear map thereafter, which is exactly OPEN.md H4's prescription. It stops mattering on a norm-conserving dataset, where both are `None`.
- how to confirm: Read :1006-1013 and :1023-1025 beside `driver.py:3210`; then check whether the primal survives by lowering the `jvp` and reading `memory_analysis()`, or simply time one `dielectric_tensor` on `si-epsilon-paw.in` against the same run with a `jax.linearize` cached at construction, asserting the tensor is bit-identical.
- against the record: Extends OPEN.md H4 ("Four screening kernels re-linearise v_of_rho at a point that never moves"), which names `phononq.py:464`, `phonon.py:621`, `efield.py:523` and `strain.py:507` -- all four of them `v_of_rho`, none of them these two. `_perturbed_coefficients`, `paw_response` and `response_becsum` return nothing from a grep of OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md or PERFORMANCE.md; `_paw_onecenter` appears there only as a memory item (MEMORY-AUDIT.md:82, OPEN.md:151) about the tape it stacks under `grad`, not about a primal recomputed at a frozen point.

### defumat/response/sternheimer.py:700 -- `SternheimerSolver.response_becsum`

**`response_becsum` takes a second `jvp` of `_raw_becsum` whose result the `jvp` inside `response_density` has already computed, so a PAW run builds `dbecsum` twice for every direction of every self-consistency iteration.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `sternheimer-core`
- mechanism: `response_density` at :697 is `jax.jvp(self.density_at, (self.psi,), (dpsi,))`, and `density_at` at :647 calls `becsum_ = self._raw_becsum(states, weights)` and then feeds it to `calculation.augmented`, so the density's tangent already carries `dbecsum` as an intermediate. `response_becsum` at :709-711 then runs `jax.jvp(self._raw_becsum, (self.psi,), (dpsi,))` from scratch. `_raw_becsum` is `becsum_of(states, vkb, weights, species_channels, k_batch)` -- a `calbec` over the whole `(nk, nbnd, npwx, nkb)` block plus the per-atom outer product -- or `_noncollinear_becsum` for a spinor, and the `jvp` costs primal plus tangent both times. `efield.py:390-393` calls the two back to back inside the direction loop.
- reached by: PAW only, since `becsum_response` is collected at `efield.py:392` under `if onecentre is not None` and `onecentre` is non-`None` only when `solver.ddd_paw` exists. A plain ultrasoft run already has the augmentation charge's whole share inside `response_density` and never asks for this.
- worth: One extra `calbec`-sized contraction plus its tangent per direction per iteration: `nk x nbnd x npwx x nkb`, so it grows with the k-set, the band count and the projector count, and on a heavy-element PAW dataset `nkb` is large. At `3 x 18` calls that is 54 duplicated `becsum` builds per dielectric run. Removable by having `density_at` return `(rho, becsum_)` and taking one `jvp` of the pair. It stops mattering for norm-conserving and plain ultrasoft, where the second call never happens or returns `()`.
- how to confirm: Read :647, :697 and :709-711 together, then time `dielectric_tensor` warm on a PAW cell (`si-epsilon-paw.in`) against a variant where `response_density` returns both tangents from one `jvp`, and assert `induced_density` and `epsilon` are bit-identical.
- against the record: Not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `response_becsum`, `_raw_becsum`, `response_density`; only `paw_response`'s neighbours and MEMORY-AUDIT's separate one-centre tape item come back, which are about memory under `grad` rather than a duplicated forward evaluation).

### defumat/response/phononq.py:690 -- `ewald_dynamical_matrix`

**The reciprocal half of `d2ionq` contracts three operands in one einsum, which forces an intermediate three times larger than the factorisation needs, and it is built twice per call.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `phonon-strain`
- mechanism: `reciprocal(shift)` ends with `jnp.einsum("ga,gij,gb->abij", phases, weighted, jnp.conj(phases))`, where `phases` is `(ngm, nat)` and `weighted` is `(ngm, 3, 3)`. The summed index `g` appears in all three operands, so any pairwise path must keep it until the last contraction; opt_einsum's greedy path contracts `(ga, gij) -> gaij` first, materialising `(ngm, nat, 3, 3)` complex. The sum factorises exactly -- `D_ab,ij = sum_g (factor_g phase_ga gq_gi)(conj(phase_gb) gq_gj)` -- so two `(ngm, nat, 3)` arrays and one `einsum("gai,gbj->aibj")` reach the same answer with a third of the intermediate. `reciprocal` is called twice, once at `q` and once at zero (`:696-697`), so the allocation happens twice per `ewald_dynamical_matrix`. The real-space half beside it has the same shape one dimension over: `vectors` is `(nat, nat, ntranslations, 3)` at `:706` and `outer` is `(nat, nat, ntranslations, 3, 3)` at `:722`.
- reached by: Every phonon at q != 0, once per call, through `frozen_force_constants`.
- worth: Reciprocal half: ngm x natom x 9 x 16 bytes per call, twice -- about 2 MB on P71's two-atom silicon and about 460 MB per call at ngm = 200000 and natom = 16. Real-space half: natom^2 x ntranslations x 9 x 8 bytes, which at natom = 32 and a few thousand translations is of the same order. Both are transient peaks inside one function rather than resident, and both are irrelevant below about ten atoms, which is every cell this phase has been run on; they scale with ngm x natom and natom^2 x ntranslations respectively, so a large cell is exactly where a dispersion would want to go and exactly where this bites.
- how to confirm: Read `phononq.py:684-697` and `:704-724`. The einsum path can be read off `jnp.einsum(..., optimize=True)`'s contraction list, or measured with the compiler's own `memory_analysis()` on a lowered `ewald_dynamical_matrix` at two cell sizes to check the intermediate scales as ngm x natom x 9. The factorised form must return the matrix bit-identical up to floating-point reassociation, so compare against the current one at 1e-14 relative rather than exactly.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. Grepped `ewald_dynamical_matrix` and `d2ionq` across all four: no hit. MEMORY-AUDIT.md's augmentation-table entries are about `Q_ij(G)` under `jax.grad`, a different array in a different module.

### defumat/response/electrostriction.py:617 -- `_position_response.operators`

**The strained operators are built three times per Voigt strain on an augmented dataset -- once inside `residual`, once inside `tail`, and once more inside `_epsilon_at` -- at the same geometry, states and density and under the same tangents.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `piezo-electrostriction`
- mechanism: `operators(geometry, states, density)` at :617 builds `moved.potential(density).v_scf`, `mixed_becsum`, `moved.onecenter(parts)` and `moved.hamiltonian(...)`, and constructs a `VelocityOperator` on top. `residual` calls it at :627 and is `jvp`'d at :690; `tail` calls it again at :701 and is `jvp`'d at :712 with the same `(geometry, states, density)` primals and the same `(tangent, dpsi, drho)` tangents, the only new leg being `solution`. The two cannot be merged into one `jvp` because `tail` consumes the solve that `residual`'s output feeds, but `jax.linearize(operators, geometry, psi, rho)` once and reusing the primal-and-tangent pair in both would remove the second build. `_second_order_energy_at`, reached through `_epsilon_at` for the same Voigt strain, then builds the same four objects a third time at the same strained point.
- reached by: Ultrasoft or PAW only for the second copy -- `tail` runs only when `_augmentation_dipole(calculation) is not None`. The third copy, through `_epsilon_at`, is paid on every dataset. PAW is where it hurts most, because `onecenter` is the radial Poisson plus the spherical quadrature.
- worth: Per Voigt strain, one avoidable `v_of_rho` on the dense grid, one `mixed_becsum` over nk x nbnd x nkb, one `onecenter` (per-atom radial quadrature, the PAW cost), one `hamiltonian` build and one `VelocityOperator` construction, each with its forward tangent. Six Voigt strains per tensor. It scales with natom for the one-centre half and with nk x nbnd for the rest, and stops mattering on a norm-conserving cell where only the third copy exists.
- how to confirm: Replace the two `operators` calls with one `jax.linearize` and assert `d(eps)/dx` is bit-identical on `tests/data/qe/si-electrostriction.in` and unchanged to round-off on the ultrasoft cell `test_electrostriction.py` uses; time the warm second call.
