- against the record: Extends OPEN.md H4 for the `v_of_rho` half and goes past it for `onecenter` and `mixed_becsum`, which H4 does not cover. Grepped `_position_response` and `operators` in OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md and PERFORMANCE.md: no hit.

### defumat/topology/kubo.py:122 -- `velocity_matrices`

**The Kubo Berry curvature holds five wavefunction-sized (nk, nband, npol*npwx) blocks at once to produce two (nk, nband, nband) matrices, and k_batch cannot bound them.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `topology-transport`
- mechanism: `velocity_matrices` puts the whole diagonalised band set on the k axis at once -- `psi = jnp.asarray(states.all_coefficients)[None]` at kubo.py:121 -- and hands it to `VelocityOperator.both`, which is `_tangent` (velocity.py:254-265) wrapping `jax.jvp` around `_operator`. `_operator` returns the *pair* `(applied(False), applied(True))`, so an eager `jax.jvp` materialises two primal blocks (H|psi>, S|psi>) and two tangent blocks (dH/dk|psi>, dS/dk|psi>) alongside `psi` itself: five full blocks live at the instant `_tangent` returns, of which the caller keeps two. Back in `velocity_matrices`, `bra = psi.conj()` (kubo.py:123) adds a sixth before the einsums contract `dh` and `ds` away. The k dial does not reach any of this: `over_kpoints` (velocity.py:387) walks k through `map_k`, but what it returns is the *stacked* full-k block, which is exactly the structural point MEMORY-AUDIT A14 makes about `matrix_elements`. `plane_wave_kubo` then pays it four times per curvature -- `velocity_matrices` for `bg[d1]` and `bg[d2]` (kubo.py:233-234) and `augmentation_connection` for each (`:236-237`), the latter running its own jvp over the same `all_coefficients`.
- reached by: `chern_number(source, method='kubo')` or `berry_curvature(states, mesh, method='kubo')` on a `PlaneWaveStates`, i.e. any plane-wave Kubo curvature map; worst for a spinor (npol = 2) run, where `ndim = 2 npwx`, and it is unrelated to the dataset being augmented -- the augmentation only adds the two further jvps.
- worth: Scales as nk * nband * npol * npwx. On a bismuthene-like spinor cell (npwx ~ 2700, npol = 2, so ndim = 5400) with `DFTSource`'s default nband ~ 32, one block on the default 12x12 mesh is 144 * 32 * 5400 * 16 = 398 MB, so ~2.0 GB inside the jvp. The quantity's whole purpose is the smooth Omega(k) map a plaquette sum cannot give, and that wants 40x40: nk = 1600 gives 4.4 GB per block and ~22 GB live, which is where it stops fitting on this machine. It stops mattering on a model state set (the `ModelStates` branch never touches this code) and on a small norm-conserving cell where npwx is a few hundred.
- how to confirm: Sum `jax.live_arrays()` nbytes immediately after `velocity.both` returns, or take `memory_analysis().temp_size_in_bytes` of the compiled `_operator` jvp, on `chern_number(source, shape=(12,12), method='kubo')` for a spinor cell -- the same instrument MEMORY-AUDIT A14 prescribes. A13's fix applies unchanged here: push the `psi.conj() . (.)` contraction into `over_kpoints`' body inside the jvp, which is exact because `psi.conj()` has no `kcart` in it and the einsum is linear in the tangent, leaving only a chunk's `(nchunk, nband, ndim)` tangent live.
- against the record: Extends MEMORY-AUDIT A14 (`VelocityOperator.matrix_elements` holds four full-k blocks), whose recorded sites are velocity.py:302 and the `second_matrix_elements` at :356. `velocity_matrices` and `topology/kubo.py` as a memory site appear in none of OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped); OPEN.md:814 mentions topology/kubo.py:139 only about the one-mask degeneracy form.

### defumat/transport/substrate.py:135 -- `exit_overlap`

**The exit plane's Miller-index grouping is a lexsort rebuilt for every k-point, every tip height and every spin channel, although it depends only on the sphere and the axis, and the segment sum under it uses np.add.at.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `topology-transport`
- mechanism: `exit_overlap` collapses the sphere onto its shadow on the surface reciprocal lattice with `_, group = np.unique(inplane, axis=0, return_inverse=True)` (:135), a lexsort of `npwx` two-vectors, and then accumulates with `np.add.at(b, (slice(None), slice(None), group), blocks * phase[...])` (:140), NumPy's unbuffered scatter-add. Neither `inplane` nor `group` depends on `height`, on the coefficients or on the spin channel -- only `phase = exp(2 pi i h3 s3)` (:133) does, and that is a pure elementwise multiply. The caller repeats the whole function: `workflows/transport.py`'s `gram_at` (:861-868) loops `for ik in range(nk)` calling `exit_overlap`, and `_assemble_momentum` calls `gram_at` once for the exit plane and once per tip height inside `for ispin in range(nspin)` (:870-874), so the identical lexsort and the identical scatter pattern are rebuilt `nspin * (nheights + 1) * nk` times. The map's own `_assemble` pays one per k-point on top. The grouping is cacheable on `(id(miller[ik]), axis)` for the whole sweep, and the scatter is a segment sum that `np.argsort(group)` once plus `np.add.reduceat` does in one pass over contiguous memory.
- reached by: `run_momentum_transport` with a `heights=` sweep -- which is the call `decay_constants`/`decay_identity` exist for and therefore the one a user actually makes -- and, at one plane each, `run_vertical_transport` and `run_stm`'s transport cross-check.
- worth: Scales as nk * nbnd * npol * npwx * nheights for the scatter and nk * npwx * log(npwx) * nheights for the lexsort. On the graphene cell PERFORMANCE.md:6383 records (36 k-points on the whole 6x6x1 grid, 20 bands, npwx ~ 2000) a ten-height sweep is 396 lexsorts and ~16 million scattered adds, against a transport assembly measured at 6.8 s of a 36.7 s run -- so it is a fraction of the assembly, not of the NSCF. It stops mattering at one height on a small sphere, and it grows with the slab thickness, since npwx is what a vacuum region inflates.
- how to confirm: Time `exit_overlap` alone on one k-point of `benchmarks`-sized graphene with `%timeit`-style repeats, split between the `np.unique` line and the `np.add.at` line; the second half cannot be settled by reading, because NumPy 2.4 may or may not take a fast path for a `(slice, slice, array)` index. Then compare against a version that hoists `group` out of the height loop and replaces the scatter with `np.add.reduceat` on a group-sorted copy.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped for `exit_overlap`, `add.at`, `transport/substrate` and `gram_at`, and the only transport entries are PERFORMANCE.md:6383 (the absolute P66 timing, which reports the 6.8 s assembly without breaking it down) and OPEN.md's S-series, none of which names this file.

### defumat/ultracell/hamiltonian.py:137 -- `ultracell_matrix`

**The matrix build transforms the whole ultracell FFT box in both directions per ket, where the wavefunction lives on the sphere's sticks and `basis/sticks.py` already implements the partial transform `h_psi` uses.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `ultracell-io-sizing`
- mechanism: `column` scatters `N npwx` sphere coefficients into the full `(npol, N Nd1, N Nd2, N Nd3)` box at `:135-136`, takes a fused `jnp.fft.ifftn` over all three axes at `:137`, multiplies pointwise, takes a fused `jnp.fft.fftn` at `:144`, and then gathers only the same `N npwx` entries back at `:145`. Both of those are the case `basis/sticks.py` was written for: the `z` pass only has to run on the columns the sphere's `xy` shadow occupies. The ultracell keeps the unit cell's occupancy ratio exactly -- an ultracell column is `(n1 G1 + q1, n2 G2 + q2)`, so the occupied columns are `n1 n2` times the unit cell's out of `n1 n2` times as many -- so the shadow is the same 'under a fifth' `sticks.py`'s docstring quotes. `defumat/ultracell/density.py:81` and `:146` do the same whole-box `ifftn` per occupied state.
- reached by: Every ultracell iteration, in the term `PERFORMANCE.md` itself names as dominant: `2 npol N nbnd` ultracell transforms per `k0` per iteration for the matrix, which is `2 npol N^2 nbnd` unit-cell transforms, plus `N nbnd` more for the density.
- worth: Scales with the FFT grid times `N nbnd nk0` per iteration and is the whole iteration, so a fractional saving here is a fractional saving on the workflow. The honest bound is the one `sticks.py` measured in the unit cell -- 1.13x on eight-atom silicon and 1.02x on sixteen atoms -- because only the `z` pass shrinks while the pointwise multiply and the `xy` pass stay full-box, and the scatter has to be paid either way. It stops mattering where the sphere is a large fraction of the box, which the ultracell never is. This is `OPEN.md` M7's shape at three more sites, and unlike `sum_band` these sites are the dominant cost rather than a side term.
- how to confirm: Read `hamiltonian.py:134-146` beside `basis/fft.py:190-220` (`sticks_to_r`/`r_to_sticks`) and `basis/sticks.py:54` (`build_sticks` takes an `fft_index` and a mask, which `box_index[ik].reshape(-1)` and `padding[ik].reshape(-1)` already are). To measure, time `run_ultracell` on `benchmarks`-scale silicon at `supercell = (4, 1, 1)` and `(8, 1, 1)` with the two transforms swapped, warm, median of three -- and check the converged total moves only in the last bits, which is the acceptance test M7 already states.
- against the record: extends OPEN.md Part III M7 (`sum_band` transforms the whole FFT box where `h_psi` uses sticks, `defumat/scf/density.py:62`); M7 names only that site. Not otherwise in AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md -- `sticks` appears in PERFORMANCE.md only at the unit-cell measurement (lines 604, 811-828) and never beside `ultracell`.

### defumat/ultracell/augmentation.py:473 -- `ultracell_becsum`

**`becsum` by Q-difference is computed as an `O(N^2)` loop of gathers and einsums where it is a cyclic cross-correlation over the Q index, which `ifftn`/`fftn` does in `O(N log N)` -- the same fold `response/nesting.py` already replaced.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `ultracell-io-sizing`
- mechanism: `sum_index[q, d]` is the Q index of `Q_q + Q_d`, which is addition of the integer triples modulo `(n1, n2, n3)`, so the block at `:475` is `sum_q conj(M[q]) M[q (+) d]` with `(+)` the cyclic shift -- a cross-correlation over the three Q axes. The loop at `:473` evaluates it one `d` at a time, and `partner = mixed[sum_index[:, d]]` at `:474` materialises a full permuted copy of `mixed`, shape `(N, N nbnd, nat, nh)` complex, on every one of the `N` passes. The driver then runs the whole thing per block and per `k0` (`_ultracell_becsum`, `driver.py:576-605`) and `_becsum_copies` immediately transforms the result to R-space with `becsum_per_copy` anyway, so the natural route is one `fftn` of `mixed` over the three Q axes into `a(R)`, the pointwise `sum_j w_j conj(a_j(R)) a_j(R)` per copy, and one `ifftn` back -- which lands directly in the form `becsum_per_copy` wants. `spinor_ultracell_becsum:524` has the identical structure.
- reached by: Every ultracell iteration on an ultrasoft or PAW dataset, which `require_an_ultracell_regime` explicitly accepts (P88 stages 5 and 6).
- worth: Scales as `blocks * nk0 * N^2 * nbnd * nat * nh` in flops against the matrix build's `N^2 nbnd` full-box transforms, so the ratio is roughly `N nat nh / (prod(cell_grid) log)`. On the 21-cell PAW cases with a 15^3-ish dense grid that is a couple of per cent of the iteration and is not worth doing; it grows linearly in `N` relative to the matrix build, so it only becomes a real term in the hundreds of cells. The per-pass `(N, N nbnd, nat, nh)` copy is a few MB at those sizes. I am reporting it because the defect is algorithmic rather than a coefficient, and because the transform route is the one this repository already chose for the same fold elsewhere -- not because it would move today's numbers.
- how to confirm: Read `augmentation.py:460-480` against `becsum_per_copy:359-377` and `defumat/response/nesting.py`'s `ifftn(|fftn(g)|^2)` substitution. To measure, `tools/benchmark.py` or a `%timeit` around `_ultracell_becsum` against `ultracell_matrix` for one iteration of `tests/data/qe/si-ultracell-paw.in` at `supercell = (2,1,1)` and `(8,1,1)`, and check the ratio moves as `N`.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `ultracell_becsum`, `ultracell_deeq`, `ultracell_matrix`, `onecentre_deeq`, `becsum_per_copy`: zero hits in all four; PERFORMANCE.md's ultracell section at line 5738 records only the whole-run pair against the supercell and the `2 N^2 nbnd` transform count)

### defumat/workflows/vc_relax.py:292 -- `run_vc_relax (the ionic loop)`

**The two reference drops MEMORY-AUDIT A2 applied to `run_relax`'s loop were never applied to `run_vc_relax`'s, so at every ionic step's SCF peak the variable-cell loop holds two more `Calculation` objects and one more set of wavefunctions than the fixed-cell loop does.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `workflows-core`
- mechanism: At the head of the body, `result = run_scf(...)` (vc_relax.py:292) rebinds `result`, which keeps the *previous* step's `SCFResult` -- and therefore its wavefunctions -- alive for the whole duration of the call that is meant to replace it. relax.py:370-380 fixes exactly this with a bare `result = None` before the call and says why in a comment sized at 7.7 GB. Separately, `previous = current` (vc_relax.py:345) is set at the end of the body and is not dropped after `_advance` returns at :346; relax.py:465-468 does `del previous` immediately after its own `_extrapolate`, with the ordering argument spelled out. So entering step N+1 the live set is `base` (genuinely required, since `_advance` builds every step as `base.at_cell(...).at_positions(...)`), `previous` (step N, dead the moment `_advance` returns), `current` (step N+1, in use) and the step-N `result`. `run_relax` at the same point holds one Calculation and no stale result. The post-loop half of this was A3 and is fixed at vc_relax.py:367 (`base = previous = current = None`); that line is after the loop and does nothing for the per-step peak.
- reached by: Any `calculation = 'vc-relax'` of more than one ionic step. It scales with natom through `nkb` and with `nk * nbnd * npwx * npol` for the stale wavefunctions, and is worst where `at_cell` is expensive, which is exactly the variable-cell case: A3 records that `at_cell` routes through `at_strain` and rebuilds the projector core `(nk, npwx, ncs)`, `kg` `(nk, npwx, 3)`, the kinetic term, the whole augmentation, `vloc_species`, `vltot` and `rho_core`.
- worth: At the NiBr2 scale A3 already costed, one Calculation is 8.20 GB (PERFORMANCE.md:4589) and one SCF state is 2.19 GB per k-point, so the stale `previous` plus the stale `result` is of order 10 GB on top of the step's true peak -- the same order as the 7.7 GB A2 measured for `run_relax`. It stops mattering on a small bulk cell, where a Calculation is megabytes. The fix costs nothing: no recompilation, since `at_cell`/`at_positions` output shapes do not change between steps.
- how to confirm: Read vc_relax.py:290-347 beside relax.py:368-380 and :464-469 -- the two drops are present in one file and absent in the other. To measure rather than read it, bracket `jax.live_arrays()` totals or `peak_bytes_in_use` across two ionic steps of a vc-relax, the way P74 bracketed the SCF, with and without a `del previous` / `result = None` pair.
- against the record: extends MEMORY-AUDIT.md A2 (`run_relax`'s two reference drops, fixed there) and A3 (`vc_relax` holds four Calculations *into the final SCF*, fixed at vc_relax.py:367); the in-loop peak is in neither -- A3's site list names `previous = current` at :326 but its whole arithmetic and its one-line fix are about the block after `:333`

### defumat/workflows/ultracell.py:196 -- `run_ultracell_stm (_box_coefficients)`

**An ultracell image transforms the density box twice and rebuilds the box-sized `|G + Q|^2` table twice, where the channel transforms alone would give both answers by linearity.**

- confidence: likely, subsystem: `workflows-core`
- mechanism: ultracell.py:195 calls `_box_coefficients(field, states, system)` on the summed charge and :196 calls it again on the whole `density` whenever it has more than one channel. Each call (ultracell.py:639-679) does two box-sized things: `ultracell.g2(system.cell)`, which builds `fractional` and `cartesian` of shape `(*box, 3)` in float64 on the host (ultracell/grid.py:245-255) before reducing them, and `np.flatnonzero` over the flattened result; and `r_to_g` (basis/fft.py:64-68), which is a full `jnp.fft.fftn` of the field over the whole ultracell box followed by a gather. Neither the `g2` table nor the index set depends on the field, so the second call recomputes both; and `field` is `density[0] + density[1]` for a collinear run (ultracell.py:172), so its transform is the sum of the two channel transforms the second call already produces. The same two lines are absent from the spectrum route, which samples the states directly. In passing, `nspin_mag = int(np.asarray(result.density).shape[0])` (ultracell.py:166, and again at :492) copies the whole ultracell density off device to read one integer.
- reached by: `run_ultracell_stm` on any run with more than one density channel -- every collinear-spin or noncollinear ultracell, which is what the entry point is mostly for, since its own docstring says the spin image is "the one an ultracell run is usually for". A `nspin_mag = 1` run takes the `channels = None` branch and pays once.
- worth: Scales as the unit-cell FFT grid times `N` (the number of cells) times `nspin_mag`. It is invisible at the scale PERFORMANCE.md:6284 timed -- 2.47 s for an eight-cell silicon image -- and is gigabytes on the box the module's own docstring names, "a slab's box is 10^8 points": there `g2` alone allocates two `(*box, 3)` float64 arrays, about 2.4 GB each, twice over, and a four-channel `r_to_g` holds 6.4 GB of complex box. Nothing here scales with `nbnd` or `nk`, so it is pure overhead on top of the loop that produced the states.
- how to confirm: Read ultracell.py:195-196 with `_box_coefficients` at :639-679 and `Ultracell.g2` at ultracell/grid.py:237-255; the index set is a pure function of `(ultracell, system.ecutrho)` and the second transform's first row is the first transform. To size it, print `np.prod(states.ultracell.grid)` for the intended slab and multiply by 24 bytes for `g2`'s two intermediates and 16 for each complex box.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `_box_coefficients`, `ultracell.g2` and `run_ultracell_stm`; the only hits are PERFORMANCE.md:6250 and :6284, which time the image as a whole and do not break it down)

### defumat/scf/density.py:337 -- `becsum (and spinor_becsum:503/:505)`

**`becsum` conjugates the large operand of its projection, materialising a second full `(npwx, nkb)` copy of `vkb` per k-point per spin channel, where conjugating the band block instead would copy the smaller array.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `scf-body`
- mechanism: `becsum:337` computes `jnp.einsum("gc,bg->bc", projectors.conj(), state)`. `projectors.conj()` is an elementwise op feeding a dot operand, and OPEN.md M4 establishes for this codebase that XLA does not fuse an elementwise op into a dot operand on CPU, because the dot is a library call -- so `vkb.conj()` is a separate `(npwx, nkb)` complex buffer. On a cell with many projectors `vkb` is larger than `psi`: at `npwx ~ 1e5`, `nkb ~ 3e3` and `nbnd ~ 400` it is `(1e5, 3e3)` against `(400, 1e5)`, about seven times bigger. The identity `sum_G conj(beta_G) psi_G = conj(sum_G beta_G conj(psi_G))` lets the conjugate go on the bands instead, and the result being `(nbnd, nkb)` its own conjugate is negligible. `spinor_becsum` does the same at :503 and :505. The allocation happens inside `one_k`, so it is one k-point's worth at a time under the `k_batch` dial, once per k per spin per SCF iteration.
- reached by: Ultrasoft or PAW with many projectors and a large plane-wave sphere; it is inert for a norm-conserving cell, where `species_channels` is all `None` and `becsum` is never reached with a real contraction.
- worth: A transient of the size M4 measured for the identical buffer on the 157-atom slab, 0.70 GB, plus one conjugate pass over `npwx * nkb` complex numbers per k per spin per iteration. It scales with `npwx * nkb`, so with `natom` through `nkb`; it stops mattering wherever `nbnd > nkb`, which is most small cells.
- how to confirm: Compare `memory_analysis()` of the compiled `Calculation.becsum` on `benchmarks/si2-us-1k.in` and on a projector-heavy cell, before and after moving the conjugate onto `state`. The `becsum` arrays must be bit-identical, since the transform is exact.
- against the record: Extends OPEN.md Part III M4, `calbec conjugates the large operand where the same package's project conjugates the small one`, which names only `defumat/hamiltonian/operator.py:155` and `_becp`. The `scf/density.py` sites are not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. M4 grades itself as a time item rather than a memory one at this size, and the same grading applies here at a lower call frequency, which is why this is last.

### defumat/scf/checkpoint.py:229 -- `save_state`

**Every array of the result is pulled to the host into one `payload` dict before `np.savez` is called, so a checkpoint's peak is the whole wavefunction set twice -- once device-resident on the live result, once on the host -- and on an accelerator the host copy is then retained on the arrays for their lifetime.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `continuation-facade`
- mechanism: `save_state` loops `_ARRAYS` at checkpoint.py:227-230 and does `payload[name] = np.asarray(value)` for each, then `np.savez(scratch, **payload)` at :245. Nothing is released between the two: the dict holds host copies of `wavefunctions`, `density`, `potential`, `potential_change`, `ns` and `tau`, plus one entry per `becsum` species, while the `SCFResult` they came from is still alive on the caller's side and still device-resident. The docstring at :215-218 names the size -- "`nspin nk nbnd npwx` complex, which is tens of gigabytes on a large cell" -- but says it about the *file*, not about the working set at the moment of the write. On the CPU backend this is close to free, because JAX aliases the single-device buffer for `np.asarray` and marks the result non-writeable; on GPU, which this project declares as the target, it is a genuine transfer, and AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:1067 records the further half of the mechanism for exactly this call shape: JAX's current `ArrayImpl` caches the host array in `_npy_value` for the life of the array, so the shadow does not go away when `payload` does. `checkpoint_every` makes it periodic rather than once: a mid-SCF checkpoint pays it beside the live Davidson working set.
- reached by: Any run with `checkpoint_dir` set, on an accelerator. Worst at the cadence rather than at the end, because a mid-SCF write lands beside the solver's own buffers.
- worth: One extra full copy of `nspin nk nbnd npwx` complex, plus the two grids, at the instant of the write -- 12.10 GB on the 45-atom NiBr2 slab by MEMORY-AUDIT's own figure for the same array. It scales with nk times nbnd times npwx and is unaffected by the `k_batch` dial, since the result carries the whole axis. It is a non-event on CPU, which is why it has not been seen here.
- how to confirm: Read checkpoint.py:227-245 -- the dict is fully built before `np.savez` is reached. To measure it, take `memory_stats()` immediately before and after a `save_state` of a converged result on a GPU, or compare `/proc/self/status` VmHWM across a run with `checkpoint_every` set and one without, same commit, warm cache both times.
- against the record: MEMORY-AUDIT.md:1950-1990 covers the **load** side -- `load_state`'s `jnp.asarray` arrays pinned by `starting_from` and `resumed_state` for the whole call, 12.10 GB on NiBr2 -- and cites `save_state`'s docstring only for the array's size. The save-time host copy is not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/scf/continuation.py:907 -- `_same_kpoints / promote_wavefunctions`

**A spiral-to-spiral continuation at a different `q` carries the converged states over as the first Rayleigh-Ritz span, although the two runs' plane-wave spheres are centred on different points, so the coefficients are indexed on Miller indices that no longer mean the same plane waves.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `continuation-facade`
- mechanism: `_check_spiral` (continuation.py:393-459) refuses a carry only when `source != target` as booleans -- one run is a spiral and the other is not -- and its docstring states "Spiral to spiral is allowed at any `q`". `_same_kpoints` (:907-919) then compares `system.kpoints.coords` against the target's, which for a spiral are the base k-points and are identical at every `q`; it never reads `spiral_q`. `promote_wavefunctions` (:817) checks only `psi.shape[1] != nk` and `psi.shape[-1] != source_npol * npwx`, and a spiral's state length is `2 npwx` on both sides. `Calculation.starting_wavefunctions` (driver.py:4209-4221) validates the same two numbers and nothing else. But a spiral's spheres are `|k +- q/2 + G|^2 < ecut` (`workflows/spiral.py:61-63`, which states it outright: "The wavefunctions therefore cannot be carried from one step to the next -- they are coefficients on a basis that no longer exists"), so at a new `q` the retained G-set and its ordering both change. The span is still a valid variational subspace, so the Rayleigh-Ritz and Davidson above it stay correct -- what is lost is the warm start the transfer exists to provide, silently, with `_dropped`'s warning never firing. Worse, it is inconsistent: when the two `q` happen to give different `npwx` the shape check drops it with a warning, and when they happen to coincide it does not.
- reached by: A hand-written `run_scf(starting_from=result)` into a system with a different `spiral_q`, which is what a manual sweep over `q` looks like. `run_spiral_scan` (spiral.py:300-304) passes no `starting_from` at all and `relax_spiral_q` knows the rule (spiral.py:483), so neither workflow reaches it -- this is the front-door route only.
- worth: The Davidson steps the transfer promises to save, paid back with interest: a scrambled span is worse than the pseudo-atomic orbitals `_dropped` falls back to, and the fallback is exactly what should have happened. Scales with nbnd times npwx per k-point in wasted first-iteration work. It stops mattering the moment `promote_wavefunctions` is given a `q` comparison, since `_check_grid` already establishes that everything else matches.
- how to confirm: Read `_same_kpoints` at continuation.py:907 and `promote_wavefunctions`'s two checks at :852-856 -- neither touches `spiral_q` -- against `_check_spiral`'s own statement at :424-427 that spiral to spiral is allowed at any `q`. To see it: continue one spiral SCF into a system whose `spiral_q` differs and count the first iteration's Davidson steps against the same run with `wavefunctions=False`; the carried span should be the slower of the two.
- against the record: `promote_wavefunctions` appears in MEMORY-AUDIT.md:557-566 only as an allocation question -- whether the promotion allocates a fresh `(nk, ...)` block or returns `psi`/`psi[0]` -- and says nothing about which basis the coefficients belong to. `_same_kpoints` and `_check_spiral` appear in none of OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md.

### defumat/solvers/davidson.py:340 -- `_at_width`

**The width ladder puts four copies of the subspace solve and of its two `(nbnd, ndim)` Ritz rotations in one executable, on exactly the single-k large-cell route where this file's own working-set fit was taken.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `solver-batching-ham`
- mechanism: `_at_width` at :352-354 lowers to `jax.lax.switch(index, [... for m in widths])` with `widths = _width_ladder(nvecx, nbnd)`, four rungs at `DAVID_NDIM = 4`. Each branch runs `solve`'s inner `at(m)` (:505-532), which contains a `generalised_eigh` -- a Cholesky, two triangular solves and an `eigh` -- and then `coefficients.T @ psi[:m]`, `@ hpsi[:m]` and `@ becq[:m]`, the first two of which each produce an `(nbnd, ndim)` block. `_band_ladder`'s docstring (:319-337) states this cost explicitly for its own ladder (`each rung is a separate copy of the whole h_psi subtree in the compiled program`) and gives `BAND_RUNGS` as the dial; `_width_ladder` (:301-316) carries no such note and no dial, and `narrow` is not a caller-settable argument of `davidson_eigensolver_all` -- `_every_k` decides it at :792 from the k-batch, so on a single-k cell it is always on. Whether the four branches share their output slots is the open half: this project has one measurement saying XLA does not share a `(nbnd, npwx)` slot across the arms of a `cond` (PERFORMANCE.md 2026-09-04, `the buffer assignment gives while.4 and while.5 a (nbnd, npwx) slot each`), but that was a `cond` over two `while_loop`s, which is not the same object as a `switch` over four straight-line branches.
- reached by: `nk == 1` or `k_batch == 1`, which is the CPU default and the mode the 157-atom slab and every single-k benchmark run in. The batched route takes `narrow = False` and compiles one branch, so it is unaffected.
- worth: If the slots are not shared, up to three surplus `(nbnd, ndim)` blocks in the one contiguous XLA temp buffer that decides whether a large run starts -- 3.8 GB at the slab's `nbnd = 1020`, `npwx = 156346`, complex128, against a fitted total whose middle term is `4.20 nbnd npwx zc`. If they are shared, the cost is HLO size alone, which is what the rematerialisation pass works on and is the second half of the same 2026-09-04 argument. The fit itself is the checkable consequence either way: it was measured with `narrow` in force and `sizing.py` budgets runs from it without knowing that `k_batch` decides `narrow`, so the same cell at `k_batch = 2` compiles to a different temp size than the model assumes.
- how to confirm: Compile `_every_k` on `si64` or the slab shapes at `k_batch = 1` and read XLA's `memory-usage-report` buffer assignment for how many `(nbnd, ndim)` slots the `switch` holds; then recompile with `narrow` forced to `False` in `_every_k:792` and refit `memory_analysis().temp_size_in_bytes`. The eigenvalues must be unchanged to round-off, which is what `narrow`'s own docstring at :406-414 already claims.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md -- grepped `_at_width`, `_width_ladder`, `narrow`, `lax.switch` in all four; PERFORMANCE.md's 2026-09-04 row is the precedent for the buffer-duplication mechanism but is about the robustness `cond`, which that pass removed, and neither it nor MEMORY-AUDIT's `Already lean: the Davidson subspace` line at :2199 mentions the ladder.

### defumat/pseudo/augmentation.py:178 -- `AugmentationCharge.integrals`

**The empty-species branch of `integrals` builds its zero block with no dtype, where both of its two siblings set one explicitly, and `block_matrix` then takes the whole `(nkb, nkb)` coefficient matrix's dtype from it.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `augmentation-upf-soc`
- mechanism: `:178` is `result.append(jnp.zeros((len(atoms), q.shape[0], q.shape[0])))`. x64 is enabled package-wide, so that is float64 whatever `config.dtypes` says, while every other block in the tuple carries `phases.real.dtype`. `block_matrix` then does `matrix = jnp.zeros((self.nkb, self.nkb), dtype=blocks[0].dtype)` (:239), so when the first species is the norm-conserving one -- `q.shape[0] == 0` is exactly that case, from the `jnp.zeros((0, 0, ngm))` at :1017 -- the assembled `D_ij` is float64 and every `.at[].set` of a float32 block upcasts into it. The two siblings already take the other route and one of them says why: `cross_integrals` passes `dtype=self.phases.dtype` (:221) and `_tabulated_integrals`'s carry carries the comment that a hardcoded float64 accumulator "under a float32 precision policy... does not run at all" (:770-774).
- reached by: `precision = 'single'` on a cell that mixes a norm-conserving species, listed first, with an ultrasoft or PAW one -- `pt2-soc-force.in` and `alas-us.in` are the committed shapes that come closest
- worth: no wrong number in float64, which is why this is a perf item: under a float32 policy the `(nkb, nkb)` nonlocal coefficient matrix is silently built double and drags every contraction against it back to double, so the run does not get the float32 it asked for. Scales with `nkb^2` for the matrix and with whatever `h_psi` then promotes. It cannot be seen in any float64 validation, which is the reason it has survived.
- how to confirm: build a two-species ultrasoft calculation under the single-precision policy and print `Calculation.coefficients(...).dtype`, or simply read the three sibling constructions at :178, :221 and :770 side by side -- the asymmetry is on the page.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md. AUDIT-2026-09-18 records one dtype literal fixed in `response/sternheimer.py` and notes 119 remaining; this one is inside `jit(_newd)` and changes what precision the run executes in, which is the bar that note sets.

### defumat/system/symmetry.py:644 -- `symmetrize_atom_displacement_density`

**The phonon response's wedge average forms a `(nsym, nat, 3, ngm)` complex intermediate on the dense G set, `nsym` times the field it is averaging.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `symmetry-kpoints`
- mechanism: Line 644 multiplies `phases[:, None, None, :]` into a triple-broadcast gather of `field_g`, producing `(nsym, nat, 3, ngm)` complex before `jnp.einsum("sij,sajg->saig", ...)` reduces over `s`. `Calculation.symmetrize_atom_displacement` (driver.py:3617-3630) hands it the dense-grid response for one spin channel at a time, once per response iteration. Whether the intermediate is ever materialised depends on whether that method runs under `jit`: eagerly it is a real allocation, and under `jit` XLA may fuse the gather into the `s` reduction, in which case what is left is the repeated dense-grid gather rather than the storage.
- reached by: A Gamma-point phonon on a symmetric cell with more than a couple of atoms and a dense grid in the tens of thousands of G-vectors; invisible on the two-atom silicon cell every phonon test uses.
- worth: Scales with `nsym * nat * ngm` on the dense grid. At `nat = 10`, `nsym = 24` and `ngm = 50 000` it is 36M complex128, about 576 MB per call; at silicon's two atoms and a few thousand G-vectors it is under 10 MB, which is why it has never shown. It is a transient inside one response iteration, so it raises the peak only if that iteration's other buffers are already close to the machine.
- how to confirm: Check first whether `Calculation.symmetrize_atom_displacement` is called from inside a jitted region; if it is, read `memory_analysis()` for that computation, and if it is not, the allocation is unconditional and `gathered.nbytes` is the number.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md / PERFORMANCE.md (grepped `symmetrize_atom_displacement_density` and `symmetrize_vector_density`, no hits)

### defumat/paw/onecenter.py:446 -- `_exchange_correlation`

**The unpolarized one-centre local XC evaluates the functional twice per sphere per pass -- `potential(density[0])` and then `energy_density(density[0])` -- where `potential_and_energy_density` exists to return both from the forward value of the single differentiated expression.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `xc-paw`
- mechanism: `Functional.potential` (functional.py:375) differentiates `sum(r * energy_density(r))`, so its forward pass already computes `e_xc` at exactly the point the next line asks for; `potential_and_energy_density` (:389) is `value_and_grad(..., has_aux=True)` written for precisely this and its docstring argues the case. `paw/onecenter.py:446-447` calls the unfused pair instead, so a cube root, a log and a square root per (direction, radius) are paid a second time, for the all-electron pass and again for the pseudo pass, for every PAW atom, every SCF iteration. PERFORMANCE.md's "One evaluation of the functional, not two" measured the identical LDA pair on the plane-wave grid at 7.3 -> 3.5 ms for `exchange_correlation` and 8.0 -> 5.5 ms for `v_of_rho`, which is the evidence that XLA does not common-subexpression this particular pair; the *GGA* pair at :121-122 of `paw/gradient.py` is a different pair and is already OPEN.md H1.
- reached by: Any PAW run at nspin = 1, which is every unpolarized PAW SCF, force, stress and response. Not reached at nspin = 2 or 4, where the branch calls `spin_potential`/`spin_energy_density` instead.
- worth: Scales as `nx * mesh` per atom per AE/PS pass -- PERFORMANCE.md's own figure is 120 x 1200 = 1.4e5 points per atom against silicon's 32^3 = 3.3e4 grid points, so the sphere's local-XC evaluation is already four times the grid's, and this doubles one of the two passes. Linear in natom and in the quadrature size, so it grows with a heavy species (larger `lmax_rho` means more directions) and vanishes for a cell with one light PAW atom. One line.
- how to confirm: Replace :446-447 with `potential_rad, energy_density = paw.functional.potential_and_energy_density(density[0])`, keeping the `[None]` on the potential, and time a compiled (not first-call) `_paw_onecenter` on `si4-paw` and `si10-paw-pbe`, median of several samples since the expected change is small. The values must be bit-identical, since the fused method evaluates the same expression at the same `jnp.abs(rho)`. If the timing does not move, XLA is fusing it and the finding is a null worth recording beside the grid measurement.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `_exchange_correlation`, `potential_and_energy_density`, `energy_density(`). Adjacent but distinct: OPEN.md H1 is the *GGA* pair and its note naming `paw/gradient.py` as "two sites the entry did not name" covers :121-122 there, not this LDA pair; MEMORY-AUDIT.md's withdrawn sub-item concerns the spin-polarized pair, which is a correctness choice rather than waste.

### defumat/hubbard/operator.py:82 -- `HubbardTerm.diagonal`

**The preconditioner's Hubbard diagonal contracts the potential matrix the wrong way round, so for a spinor run, whose `vns` is complex Hermitian rather than real, it returns the wrong number.**

- confidence: possible, subsystem: `hubbard-tddft-projwfc`
- mechanism: `matrix(ik)` at line 76 builds the term as `columns @ vns @ conj(columns).T`, whose diagonal is `sum_ij c_i v_ij conj(c_j)`. `diagonal(ik)` at line 84 computes `einsum('gi,ij,gj->g', conj(columns), vns, columns)`, which is `sum_ij conj(c_i) v_ij c_j` -- the same contraction with `v` transposed. For a real symmetric `vns`, which is what a collinear run has, the two agree and the trailing `jnp.real` hides the rest. For a spinor, `block_potential` (operator.py:44-47) lays the four spin quadrants into one complex Hermitian `vns`, and the two forms then differ: with `v = [[0, i], [-i, 0]]` and `c = (1, i)` the true diagonal is +2 and this expression gives -2. The trailing `jnp.real` does not repair it, because the error is a sign on a real number rather than a stray imaginary part.
- reached by: Noncollinear DFT+U, where `ns` and therefore `v_ns` have complex off-diagonal spin blocks -- that is any converged shell whose moment is not along the quantization axis. A collinear run is exact.
- worth: A cost and not a number: this feeds `hamiltonian/operator.py:326`'s `h_diag`, which is Davidson's preconditioner only, so the converged eigenvalues and the total energy are untouched and only the iteration count moves. How much is unmeasured; the Hubbard share of `h_diag` is small beside the kinetic term, so the effect is likely a few per cent of the eigensolver's steps rather than a factor. Scales with nk and nbnd through the solve, not with anything else.
- how to confirm: Read `operator.py:76-87` and compare `diagonal(ik)` with `jnp.diagonal(matrix(ik))` for a spinor `vns`; they differ wherever `vns` has an imaginary part. To size it: count Davidson iterations on `si-ldau-noncol.in` with the einsum written as `einsum('gi,ij,gj->g', columns, vns, conj(columns))` and without.
- against the record: not in OPEN.md / AUDIT-2026-09-18.md / MEMORY-AUDIT.md (grepped `HubbardTerm`, `diagonal(ik)`; OPEN.md:1604 discusses `hamiltonian/operator.py:306`'s `h_diag` construction but not the Hubbard term's contribution, and PERFORMANCE.md:1195 times only `HubbardTerm.apply`)

## Coverage, one line per subsystem

**`scf-driver`** (13 files). I read all 6099 lines of defumat/scf/driver.py in chunks rather than grepping, and followed six callees far enough to settle a mechanism: occupations.py (smearing_entropy, smeared_occupations), checkpoint.py (_refusal, _SCALARS), fields.py (feedback), batching.py (park/fetch_wavefunctions, resolve_projectors, resolve_wfc_store), projectors.py (ProjectorCore.at_positions and its lazy default), hubbard/energy.py (hubbard_potential), davidson.py/subspace.py (force_real_g0), noncollinear.py (SpinorHamiltonian.nk).

Four candidates were chased and dropped, recorded here so nobody re-derives them. (1) smearing_entropy is called with the two-Fermi-level result and branches on isinstance(ef, (tuple, list)); smeared_occupations returns a Python tuple there, so the shared-level branch is not taken and there is no broadcast against nbnd. (2) The Hubbard deband pairing at driver.py:5613, sum(ns_out * v_ns) without a conjugate, looked like the transposed-index trap and is correct: hubbard_potential's docstring establishes that the separable operator's expectation value is the unconjugated sum_ij v_ij N_ij and that taking the conjugate gives the transpose, measured at 4.2e-4 Ry on QE's lda+U_kind1_noncollin. The driver's comment beside it, which says the spinor case "needs the conjugate", is the stale half; a comment cannot change a number, so it is not a finding. (3) The gamma-only random top-up in starting_wavefunctions does not apply force_real_g0, but every consumer does (subspace.py:164 in rayleigh_ritz, davidson.py:475), so Im c(0) is forced before any inner product. (4) SpinorHamiltonian.nk halves for a spiral, so threshold_shape and the span check in starting_wavefunctions are on the physical k count.

Checked and found faithful: next_ethr against electrons.f90's reset-at-2 / MIN / floor schedule, the iteration-1 ethr_small retry and its CYCLE scf_step semantics, the eband/deband/ehart/etxc evaluation-density convention, default_nbnd against setup.f90 including the noncollinear doubling, the addusdens-then-sym_rho order in Calculation.density, and the newd potential including vltot and the field.

Nothing found in this file for four of the trap classes the checklist names: no hardcoded dtype literal on a jitted or differentiated path (_ns_dtypes and _mix read the dtype off ns deliberately), no abs at a forced zero, no band-by-band diagonal under a degenerate eigensolver, and no dropped leading spin axis on density/potential/becsum. The noncollinear newd running outside jit is OPEN.md M1 and is not repeated.

One perf item was measured against its own docstring and judged too small to rank: _iteration_scalars and _accuracy_split are each consumed as `(float(x) for x in <device array>)`, which is three slice dispatches plus three blocking transfers where the docstrings claim one transfer ("the loop syncs once", "One call, three numbers"). It is the same shape as the dispatch hazard _relative_residual's docstring records from OPEN.md Part IV item 1, and costs microseconds against a multi-second iteration. Also noted and not ranked: max_iterations=0 leaves `iteration` unbound and raises UnboundLocalError at `completed = iteration - 1 ...`, where checkpoint_every < 1 is validated by name.

What I could not establish by reading alone: whether a lazy Projectors set survives reverse-mode differentiation through at_strain and at_kcart, which is why finding 2's fix is stated as "honour the dial or refuse by name" rather than "pass lazy="; and whether a pinned-host jax.Array passed into a jitted computation transfers implicitly or raises on the accelerator backends, which is why findings 3 and 4 say "a full-set transfer or an incompatible-memory-kind error" without picking one.

**`scf-body`** (15 files). I read all seven files end to end, in chunks rather than by keyword. Several things that look like traps were checked against the reference source and are correct, so I am recording them as negatives rather than padding the list: `_noncollinear_xc`'s `abs(rho)` for `etxc` matches `v_of_rho.f90:577`'s `arho` exactly, including QE's zeroing of all four components below `vanishing_charge`; `local_tf_preconditioner` is a faithful transcription of `approx_screening2` down to the Coulomb metric, `agg0`'s unit conversion out of `tpiba2`, the restart from `vbest` and the `max(1e-12, 1e-6 dr2)` target; `AdaptiveMixer`'s `residual * previous >= 0` really is Elk's `>=` (mixadapt.f90:64), including the first-step doubling, so the docstring is right; `_reciprocal_kernel` matches `ewald.f90` term by term including where the `2 tpi/omega` factor stops; `_becsum_species` and `_spinor_becsum_species` have the index order the docstrings claim; the `(up, down)` vs `(charge, magnetization)` rotation in both preconditioners applies `beta` exactly once on every branch; and `_weights` in residual.py agrees with `Calculation.occupations` on `degspin`, so the for_spin trap is not present here. One dead branch I chose not to report: `hartree`, `scf_accuracy_terms` and `EwaldSum.gamma_factor` all carry a `gvectors.gamma_only` doubling, but `basis/builder.py:137` builds the dense set with `generate_gvectors`'s default `gamma_only=False` and nothing else ever constructs a half-sphere `GVectors`, so those branches are unreachable today; they are consistent with the halved set they were written for, so they are latent rather than wrong. I could not establish by reading alone how many lattice translations `build_ewald` actually produces on a vacuum-padded production cell, nor the share of `local-TF`'s 730 s that the host round-trips account for; both findings say what would settle them.

**`scf-occ-mag`** (15 files). Four findings, one bug and three costs. sourcefree.py and spin_torque.py yielded nothing: I checked that `longitudinal_field` masks `g2` at the argument the derivative is taken at rather than repairing the quotient, that `g.T * weight` is Hermitian under G -> -G for a real input field so the inverse transform is real by construction rather than by the `jnp.real`, that it uses `sum(g*g)` and not `GVectors.g2` (the unit trap its own comment records), and that `torque_of_result`'s positional call into `v_of_rho` matches that function's signature slot for slot (rho, gvectors, cell, rho_core, functional, rho_core_g, quantization_axis, tau, source_free).

Which transcriptions I checked against the vendored source and which from recall, since the method rule prefers the source: I read `opt_tetra_weights_only`'s degenerate-band average in `PW/src/tetra.f90:1021-1044` and `constrained_magnetization` in `PW/src/input.f90:1589-1594` and `efermig.f90:70-71` directly, and all three agree with what is here. `_average_degenerate`'s block partition matches QE's scan on a chain (I traced e = (0, 0.5e-6, 1.0e-6) through both and they give the same grouping), so OPEN.md B2's open request to check it against the tree is now answerable: it is right. The one difference is that QE's inner `DO jbnd` loop that runs to `nbnd` without an `EXIT` never assigns, so QE leaves the *last* degenerate group unaveraged where this code averages it; that conserves weight either way and this code's version is the rule D4 one, so it is not a finding. `wgauss`, `w1gauss`, `set_nelup_neldw`, `opt_tetra_init`'s 20-point stencil and `sumkt`'s four branches I compared from recall of the Fortran rather than by reading it, and they all matched line for line.

Two things I saw and could not establish by reading, so they are notes rather than findings. `_scatter`'s optimised branch builds `tetra.wlsm[order]` at `(ntetra, nbnd, 4, nntetra=20)`, which would be 614 MB on a 20^3 grid at nbnd = 20, but the einsum that consumes it is a reduction over a length-4 axis rather than a dot, so XLA plausibly fuses the gather into it and I cannot say from the source whether it is materialised. `MagneticField.potential` evaluates `field_energy` and `constraint_energy` a second time after `jax.grad` has already evaluated both in its forward pass, which is OPEN.md H1's shape one module over, but the work is a handful of grid reductions and is too small to be worth a slot.

Two things reading could not settle. What share of an SCF iteration the tetrahedron occupation step actually is: `PERFORMANCE.md` times the tetrahedron *DOS* (228 ms on `al-tetrahedra`) and never the per-iteration occupation call, so findings 3 and 4 are sized by the arithmetic of their shapes and not against a measured baseline. And `magnetization_components` (fields.py:268) returns `rho_r[1:]`, which is an empty leading axis when `rho_r.shape[0] == 1`, so `magnetization[0]` in `sphere_moments`/`total_moment` is an IndexError; I could not establish from the driver whether a noncollinear run carrying a field but no starting magnetization can reach `nspin_mag = 1`, and it is a crash rather than a wrong number, so I left it out.

**`continuation-facade`** (17 files). All three assigned files read whole, in chunks, not grepped. Context reads followed each mechanism to the consumer: driver.py's `continued_state` block and `Calculation.starting_wavefunctions`, the anisotropy and topology entry points the facade forwards into, `initial_ns_noncollinear`, `for_spin`, and the mixer's attribute set.

Negatives worth recording, because each was looked for and is not there. The `nspin=2` density really is stored `(up, down)` (`scf/density.py:261-275`), so `spin_components`/`from_spin_components` are right and the electron-count integral in `_check_grid` is not double-counting. `with_kpoints` does go through `for_spin`, which handles `nspin = 4` as well as 2, so the caller-built-k-set trap does not fire there. `_STATE_ARGUMENTS["field"] -> "magnetic_field"` was checked against every callee `_call_options` reaches: every parameter literally named `field` in `workflows/` and `response/` is the magnetic field, so nothing is injected under a collided name. The checkpoint's JSON payload is serialisable as written -- `terms` is built from `float(...)` at `driver.py:5669-5687` and `total = sum(terms.values())` is a Python float -- so `save_state` does not raise on a jax scalar. `save_mixer`/`save_optimizer` cannot hit their `NotImplementedError`: the Anderson and adaptive mixers hold numpy, not jax, arrays (`mixing.py:393-400`). `_check_grid`'s cell test uses `np.allclose(..., atol=1e-10)` with the default `rtol=1e-5`, so a linear strain below about 1e-5 slips past it -- but the density-integral check two lines down still fires, so what that costs is a message naming the pseudopotential for a strained cell, not a number; not reported for that reason.

Nothing in these three files is inside a `jit` or a `grad` boundary -- continuation and checkpointing are host-side setup, and the facade is a delegation layer -- so rule D4, `np.asarray`-in-grad, dtype literals, gamma-storage sums and wedge symmetrisation have no site here and I claim none. `checkpoint.py` yielded nothing beyond the host copy in finding 5: the field coverage is genuinely closed by `unhandled_fields`, the fingerprint's `types` list subsumes `natoms`, and the `.partial` -> `.npz` rename arithmetic is correct for a path with any suffix or none.

What I could not establish by reading: whether finding 1's loosened threshold actually moves a measured anisotropy, and by how much; whether finding 2's inconsistent starting `ns` survives to convergence or is relaxed away on a given cell. Both need a run.

**`solver-batching-ham`** (17 files). All ten files read end to end, davidson.py in two chunks. I traced the whole Davidson step by hand for the traps that would produce a wrong number and found none: the subspace-buffer bookkeeping is sound (rows past `nbase + notcnv` are exactly zero and are overwritten before they are ever inside a live width, so no stale entry reaches `hc`/`sc`), the width ladder always rounds *up* so `_at_width` can never drop a live row, the collapse's `diag(energies)`/identity is exact because `generalised_eigh` returns S-orthonormal vectors on both routes, the convergence test and the per-band `ethr` vector match `cegterg.f90:556-563`, and the gamma-storage rule is right in all three places it appears (`_becp`'s `2 Re - (G=0)`, `_extend_projection`'s `row_h`/`row_s`, `expansion`'s `gamma_inner` norm, with `force_real_g0` applied to the starting vectors and to every correction block). The einsum index orders in `diagonal`/`overlap_diagonal` differ between the collinear and spinor classes (`vkb.conj(),D,vkb` against `vkb,D,vkb.conj()`) but both are real and equal for Hermitian `D`, so that is not the transposed-pair trap. No `np.asarray` sits inside a differentiated path -- the two in `davidson_eigensolver_all` are the deliberate host branch, and `robust_retry=False` exists for a caller that traces through. I deliberately did not report: `project(evc)` outside the collapse `cond` (OPEN.md H5), `_becp` conjugating the large operand (M4), the dense `(nkb,nkb)` preconditioner contraction and its two spinor twins (M5), `return_steps` as a static argument (M6), `_chunk_sum`'s vmap stack (MEMORY-AUDIT A8), and the `potential`/`potential_wave` pair (MEMORY-AUDIT's own "judged not worth a finding" list). One candidate I dropped for lack of teeth: `Hamiltonian._local`'s gamma branch never takes the stick path, so a gamma-only run pays a fused whole-box FFT per band -- the code says so deliberately, and `basis/sticks.py`'s own measured gain falls from 1.13x at eight atoms to 1.02x at sixteen, so at production size it is worth about one per cent and the trend is against it. Nothing here can be confirmed by running, since the task is read-only; every `how_to_confirm` is stated rather than performed.

**`basis-radial`** (27 files). Four findings, two bugs and two costs. What I looked for and did NOT find, so that the negatives are on the record rather than left as silence.

Transcriptions I checked line by line against the vendored Fortran and found correct: `ylmr2`'s Q recursion, normalisation and lm ordering in `harmonics.py` (every branch, including the divided-through `sent` factor at `m = l` and the unused `q[2]`/`q[5]`/`q[7]` slots); `mesh_cutoff_index` against `read_pseudo`'s one-past-the-cutoff loop including the all-inside and odd-rounding edges; `vloc_of_g`'s erf splitting, its `q = 0` branch and the placement of `fpi/omega` on the analytic term; `ggen`'s `<= gcutm` against `grid_set`'s strict `< gcut`, both reproduced with the right inequality; `realspace_grid_init`'s two-step estimate and `allowed`'s 2-3-5 rule; `gk_sort`'s `eps8` snap; `ggens`' prefix property and the assertion that guards it.

Traps I went looking for in these files and did not find. The gamma half-sphere rule is carried correctly everywhere it is carried: `gamma_inner` is `2 Re(sum) - Re(conj(a_0) b_0)`, `scatter_to_box_gamma` writes `G = 0` once and skips entry 0 of `fft_index_minus`, `force_real_g0` is a select and not a scatter, and the Nyquist aliasing that would make `fft_index_minus == fft_index` for a nonzero G is excluded by the `[-(n-1)//2, (n-1)//2]` enumeration on an even box. The padding convention survives: `gather_from_box` and `r_to_sticks` both return the wrong thing at padded plane waves, but `Hamiltonian.apply` (operator.py:229) masks the result, so nothing downstream sees it -- I chased this one to its caller rather than reporting the shape. `build_sticks`' spare columns are provably disjoint from the occupied ones, so the `.set` in `sticks_to_r` cannot overwrite a live stick. The stick transforms' normalisation is exactly `g_to_r`/`r_to_g`'s. `_expand_dij`'s `lm_i == lm_j` reproduces `init_us_1`'s double delta. `Pseudopotential.lmax` is derived from the projectors rather than from the UPF header, so `lm_of` cannot index past the harmonics array.

Three candidates I built and then dropped because the mechanism did not survive. The sticks rebuild at `at_kpoints`/`at_spiral_q` is not waste -- the k-set genuinely changed. The `j_3` closed form does lose about four digits of relative accuracy just above its 0.05 threshold, by the same cancellation the `l = 4` comment describes, but `j_3(0.05)` is 1e-6 of `j_3(1)` so the absolute error is ~1e-11 and nothing can see it. `(-1j) ** np.asarray(l_of)` forcing `ProjectorCore.columns` to complex128 in single precision is real and is already MEMORY-AUDIT.md's dtype item 1, so I dropped it rather than restate it; its sibling at `atomic.py:125` is on a transient starting guess and is not worth a line of its own.

What I could not establish by reading alone: the size of finding 2's error on a real velocity matrix element (it needs one Gamma-point run with and without the guard), and whether the two `jnp.take` calls in `_apply_phases` fuse into the multiply on XLA CPU or materialise two full `(npwx, nkb)` temporaries -- which is why I did not report the latter.

**`augmentation-upf-soc`** (15 files). I found no silent wrong number in these four files, and that is a result rather than a gap: I checked the spin-orbit machinery term by term against the Fortran and it is right everywhere I could reach it. `rot_ylm`, `spinor` and `sph_ind` reproduce `init_us_1.f90:212-225`, `spinor.f90` and `sph_ind.f90` index for index; `_fcoef` matches the loop at `init_us_1.f90:168-186`, which does **not** carry an `indv` condition, so building `dvan_so` from the unmasked coefficients and masking afterwards is exactly QE's order at `:202-203` and the module docstring's trap is correctly avoided; `_sandwich` reproduces `transform_qq_so`'s single `is` sum and `newd_so`'s `(s, t)` pairing, and `pauli_blocks` reproduces `newd_so`'s four inline expressions including the sign of the `m_y` term; `becsum_so` and `becsum_transform` reproduce `add_becsum_so`'s four Pauli traces with the same index order, and the identity-`fcoef` limit reproduces the Fortran's `else` branches of all three routines. `augmentation_dipole` matches `compute_qdipol.f90` including `lp = 3, 4, 2` and the sign flip on z, `_expand_qij` matches `set_upf_q`'s triangle-and-parity copy and `setqfnew`'s polynomial with the Fortran-order `qfcoef` reshape, and `_renormalize_orbitals` matches `upf_check_atwfc_norm` including the zero-norm and negative-occupation order.

Two things I checked and deliberately did not report. `_read_augmentation` zeroes `qfuncl` beyond `cutoff_r_index` for any dataset that carries the attribute, where `read_upf_new.f90:502` does it only for PAW -- but of the 55 committed UPF files only the ten PAW ones carry `cutoff_r_index` at all, so no number moves. And `_renormalize_orbitals` integrates the beta-chi overlap with the full-mesh Simpson weights truncated at each projector's own cutoff, where QE calls `simpson(kkbeta, ...)` with the dataset-wide `kkbeta` -- the two differ only in the last one or two weights, where `beta` is zero by construction, and the correct-weights question belongs to `radial.py`, which another agent owns.

What I could not establish by reading: every memory figure below is a shape argument, not a measurement. I ran nothing, so the two perf items are sized from the array shapes and from figures `MEMORY-AUDIT.md` A9 already measured on the same cell for the sibling routine; neither is an A/B. Finding 1 is a crash I traced through five files but did not reproduce, and no committed input mixes a PAW dataset with a norm-conserving one, so nothing in the suite exercises it.

**`system-builder`** (23 files). What I checked and found clean, so that a later pass does not repeat it. `latgen` was read line by line against `Modules/latgen.f90` for every `ibrav` the table names, including the two that are easiest to get wrong: `ibrav = -13`, whose axes QE changed and whose `errormsg` in the Fortran is a warning rather than a stop, and `ibrav = 9` against `-9`, where the two descriptions differ only in which vector carries the minus sign. Every vector, every sign and every `celldm` range check agrees, and so do the `abs(ibrav) == 5` and `abs(celldm) < 1` bounds. `celldm_from_abc` agrees with `abc2celldm` entry for entry, including that `ibrav = 0` takes all three cosines and the `(-12, -13)` and `(5, -5, 12, 13)` splits. The default units of both cards were checked against `read_cards.f90` rather than assumed: `ATOMIC_POSITIONS` with no option is `alat` for `PW` (`card_atomic_positions`, the `prog == 'PW'` line), and `CELL_PARAMETERS` with no option is left to `cell_base_init`, which is the "alat when a lattice parameter was supplied, bohr otherwise" rule the builder implements.

Three candidates were dropped after establishing that the mechanism is not there. QE 7.3's rule that `|starting_magnetization| >= 1` is read as Bohr magnetons and the whole array divided by `zv` (`input.f90:1482`) is **not** applied in `build_system`, which looks like a silent misreading of a legal 7.5 input, but it is implemented one layer down at `Calculation.starting_magnetization` together with QE's clamp, and `System.local_moments` is documented as deliberately keeping the raw value for the symmetry filter and the constraint targets. `element_symbol` follows `atomic_number.f90`'s cases exactly. `pair_separation_bound`, `fold_radius` and `lattice_translations` are each sound: the body-diagonal bound is the right bound for a fractional coordinate in `[-1/2, 1/2)`, and the search box is the reciprocal projection rather than a cubic guess on `|a_i|`.

No perf finding beyond the one below. This is a pure setup module: nothing in it runs per SCF iteration, per k-point or inside a differentiated path, and the three candidates I sized are all too small to report. `Cell.to_crystal` and `k_to_crystal` dispatch an uncompiled `jnp.linalg.inv` where the module's own comment explains why `_volume`, `_bg` and `_scaled` were wrapped in `jax.jit`, but there are only five call sites and all are setup. `System.local_moments` is rebuilt on every one of the 51 `nspin_mag` reads, and only for a noncollinear run, which is microseconds against an FFT. `lattice_translations` materialises the whole search box before filtering, and the caller that makes it large (`vdw/grimme.py`) already documents the count. There is also no dtype literal in a differentiated or jitted path in these five files: `Cell`, `Structure` and the conversions all go through `precision.as_real` or `self.at.dtype`, and the two NumPy `float` arrays (`Structure.free`, `Structure.masses`) are consumed host-side.

Two things reading alone could not settle. For finding 1 I can state the displacement of the k-set in reciprocal-vector units but not what it costs in Rydbergs, since that needs a run at two cells. For finding 2 I could not establish whether any downstream charge check trips before the wrong density is used: the Fermi bisection with smearing would drive `ef` upward trying to reach `nelec` from half the weight, and with `occupations = 'fixed'` the density simply integrates to half, but whether either raises rather than converging is a property of the driver and not of my files.

**`symmetry-kpoints`** (14 files). All four files read in full. Only one bug survived; the rest of the subsystem is, as far as reading against the Fortran can establish, correct, and the checks that came back clean are worth recording because each is a convention that could have been wrong invisibly.

Verified sound against the vendored source, with the derivation rather than by pattern-matching:

* The G-space phase convention. `symmetry_maps` builds `permutations` as the Miller index `M m`, which is the Miller index of `S^T G`, and pairs it with `exp(-i G . f_S)` on the *original* G, where `sym_rho_serial` pairs `S G` with `exp(-i (SG) . f_S)`. The two look like opposite signs until QE's `ft` is read for what it is: `checksym` tests `eqvect(rau(na), xau(nb), ft_)`, so QE's operation is `r -> S r - ft` while this code's stored translation is the `+t` convention, `t = -ft_QE`. With that, `rho_G = e^{-i G . t_S} rho_{S^T G}` is exactly the invariance condition, the two formulas agree, and both are projectors.
* `symmetrize_vector`. Covariant components `w_i = a_i . v` transform with `M^{-1}` where the cartesian vector transforms with `R`, so pairing the integer matrix `M_s` with `irt[s, n]` is `(1/N) sum_s R_s^{-1} F(irt[s,a])`, which is the correct average. The same argument carries `symmetrize_atom_tensor`, `symmetrize_atom_pair_tensor` and `symmetrize_atom_cartesian_tensor`, and it is why those use `irt` while `symmetrize_atom_displacement_density`, which applies the cartesian `R` directly, needs `irt^-1`. Both are consistent.
* PAW `becsum`. `PAW_symmetrize` computes `D^T becsum(irt[s,a]) D`; this code computes `D becsum(irt^-1[s,a]) D^T`. Since `D` is a real orthogonal representation (`harmonic_rotations` defines it by `Y(R r) = D Y(r)`, which is a homomorphism), relabelling `s -> s^-1` turns one into the other exactly. The `nspin_mag = 4` branch matches too: QE's `s(kpol,is,invs(isym))` on `bg`-basis components is the same map as this code's forward cartesian `R` with the inverse atom permutation, and `det(R)` and `t_rev` are both invariant under `s -> s^-1`.
* `irreducible_wedge` against `kpoint_grid.f90`. The grid ordering, the `- nint` fold, the `t_rev` negation (applied before the fold here and after it in QE, which agrees because `np.rint` is odd, including at exact halves), the `eps = 1e-5` on-grid test and the `mod` index arithmetic all match.
* QE's `errore` guard that this code replaces with a silent `continue` (kpoints.py:152, 224) **cannot fire**, so its absence is harmless: for a Monkhorst-Pack grid `S k` is on the grid iff `(S - I) s/2` is integral, and that set is closed under composition, so the on-grid operations always form a group and the orbit walk is always a genuine equivalence. I checked this rather than reporting the missing guard.
* `expand_band_path` reproduces `generate_k_along_lines.f90` point for point, including the zero-count discontinuity (the docstring's `1 + sum(counts[:-1])` is wrong for a zero count, but the code is right and only the prose is off).
* `spiral_kpoints` re-normalises its weights and drops `spin_normalized`, which looked like the `for_spin` trap; it is not, because every consumer of `Calculation.basis_kpoints` uses it only to build the plane-wave sphere, the kinetic term and the projectors, and never reads its weights.
* QE's `symm_by_label` / `chem_symb` species matching (which would lump two `ityp` of one element together) is off by default (`symmetry_with_labels = .FALSE.`), so matching on `ityp` here is what `pw.x` does by default.

What I could not establish by reading: whether the memory figures in findings 2 to 5 actually move a measured peak, since each is a transient whose survival depends on what XLA fuses and on when the peak is sampled; each `how_to_confirm` says which measurement would settle it. I also could not price finding 1's energy consequence, only its mechanism and the group it removes.

No finding in the spiral geometry (`spiral.py` is 129 lines and does one thing, and `invariant_operations`' `M q = q` is the right condition for a Miller-like vector).

**`xc-paw`** (22 files). Read all nine assigned files in full, and cross-checked every load-bearing transcription against the vendored Fortran rather than trusting the docstrings. What came back exact, and is therefore a null worth recording: `radial_hartree` is `upflib/radial_grids.f90:hartree` line for line, including the `nk1 = nst - k - 1` offset that decides which fit coefficients become `c2`/`c3` and the `l >= 2` zeroing, the boundary folds `f1`/`fn`, the `c0` recovery and the final division by `sqrt(r)`; `radial_derivative` is `radial_gradient(iflag=0)` exactly, interior stencil, zero at the outer end and the linear extrapolation of the first point; `_gradient` reproduces `PAW_gradient`'s `(r, phi, theta)` ordering, its `dylmp`/`dylmt` assignment and its `rm3`, and correctly drops `lm = 0` from the angular sum; `_divergence` reproduces `PAW_divergence` including the separate `lm = 1` theta term (harmless here because `dylmp[:,0]` and `dylmt[:,0]` are identically zero for a constant `Y_00`) and the `h_lm[0, :nlm]` radial derivative at the output `lmax`; `build_angular_grid` matches `PAW_rad_init` in `nphi`, `ntheta`, the weights and the `lmax_rho = 0` special case, and `_angular_derivatives` matches the `vph`/`vth` cross-product projection; `_spin_exchange_energy`'s three-part gate matches `gcx_spin`'s `rnull` logic; `_spin_channels` plus `clamp_polarization` match `xc_lsda`'s clamp and `xc_`'s `(total, m)` and `(n, |m|/n)` forms; `_hartree` matches `PAW_h_potential` including the `nspin_lsda` sum and the factor of one half; `_truncated_weights`/`weights_full` match QE's split of `simpson(kkbeta)` for `ddd` against `simpson(mesh)` for the energies; and `_noncollinear_gradient` matches `compute_rho_spin_lm`'s `segni` convention and the `add_small_mag` ordering, which makes that function's "Not reproduced: add_small_mag" docstring stale while the code is right. What I could not establish by reading alone: whether `rho_rad[0]/r2 + core` actually goes negative on a pseudo PAW sphere (finding 2's magnitude), whether `xc_gcx_` ever hands `gcc_spin` a `|zeta| > 1` at a point with `rho > 1e-6` (finding 3 exists only if it does), and whether XLA fuses the one-centre LDA pair the way it fused the unpolarized GGA one (finding 4). One QE quirk I deliberately did not report as a defumat defect: `PAW_xc_potential:571-574` sets `e_rad = 0` at `nspin_mag = 4` wherever `amag <= eps12`, so QE drops the one-centre XC energy density at unmagnetised points of a noncollinear run and defumat keeps it; that is a disagreement with QE in defumat's favour. I also looked at and dropped `mgga.py:276`'s `sqrt(maximum(sigma, 0.0))`: `sigma` is a sum of squares so the `maximum` can only tie at a bit-exact zero, and the only differentiated path into `tb09_potential` is `residual.jvp` under the `autodiff` backend that `scf/solvers.py` records as having lost to finite differences, so both gates are closed by default and the trap name alone is not evidence. No dtype literal, no `np.asarray` in a differentiated path, no hand-rolled k loop and no per-call varying shape was found in these nine files.

**`forces-stress-relax`** (27 files). All twenty-one assigned files read in full (the four over 400 lines in chunks). Context reads: `scf/driver.py` at 900-1000 (`gamma_storage_is_consumable`, `_without_gamma_storage`), 2392-2445 and 2740-2990 (every `at_*` and its cache pops), 3197-3230 (`coefficients`), 6040-6070 (`SCFResult.potential`); `batching.py` 1-200 and 374-414; `pseudo/formfactors.py` 26-160; `basis/builder.py` 90-155; `workflows/anisotropy.py` 960-1060; `Modules/bfgs_module.f90` 330-375.

What I looked for and did NOT find, so these are negatives rather than gaps. (a) The BFGS transcription is faithful where it is easiest to get wrong: `bfgs.py:step`'s `gradient_error = max|force|` matches `bfgs_module.f90:359`, whose `MATMUL(TRANSPOSE(hinv_block), grad(1:n-NADD))` collapses to the cartesian force exactly and, like the Python, excludes the cell block; `cell_error` at `bfgs.py:376` matches `:365` including the column-major `RESHAPE` transpose; `_energy_error`'s first-step predicted reduction matches `:345`. (b) The D2 force and stress signs: I derived `F_a = -dE/dtau_a` and `sigma_cd = -(1/Omega) dE/deps_cd` by hand from `grimme._dispersion_kernel` and both reproduce `vdw/analytic._pair_coefficients` term for term, sign included, so the separation-direction trap that module's docstring warns about is not present. (c) All five `copy.copy(self)` sites in the driver against the four compiled-gradient caches: `_spiral_gradient`/`_spiral_gradient_chunk` are popped at every one that moves their sphere, `_analytic_terms`/`_strain_gradient`/`_strain_term_gradients` are identity-keyed and self-invalidate, and `_energy_gradient` legitimately survives `at_positions` because the positions are an argument. No staleness. (d) No rule-D4 band diagonal, no `np.asarray` inside a differentiated path, no clamp tangent at a tie, no dtype literal inside a jitted or differentiated path, and no hand-rolled `vmap` over k in any of these files.

Two items looked at and left below threshold. `torque.py:166` builds `jax.jit(jax.value_and_grad(chunk))` from a fresh closure on every `torque_at_angle` call, so a second call in the same process retraces the `k_batch`-unrolled spinor `apply`; `run_torque` is reached once per process from `Calculator.get_torque` and only a hand-written angle sweep would call it twice, so the cost is not paid on any recorded path. Alongside it, `torque.py:212` discards the energy the chunked pass already computed while `anisotropy.py:1025` recomputes the identical number un-chunked as `check` -- one extra forward `H|psi>` over the whole k axis, real but small. Also dropped deliberately: the stacked `@jax.jit` at `energy.py:697-698` (an extra dispatch layer and nothing else, and `_spinor_projector_energies` beneath it is inlined by the outer `jit(grad)` anyway), `compute_stress(terms=True)`'s extra reverse pass (documented as the chosen design), and `abs(psi)**2` in `stress/analytic._kinetic` and `spiral._kinetic_energy`, neither of which is differentiated with respect to `psi`.

The one structural statement that ties the two bugs together: the autodiff route carries the half-sphere correction at **every** plane-wave sum (`energy.py:_kinetic_energy`, `_norms`, `_becp_gamma`, `_constraint_energy`), while the two analytic transcriptions have zero gamma handling and zero refusal, and `tests/regression/test_gamma_only.py` exercises only the default method. I could not run anything, so no number is measured here.

**`sternheimer-core`** (16 files). All six assigned files read in full. No reachable wrong-number bug was found in them today, which is a real result rather than a gap: the trap list was walked site by site against the Fortran and each candidate came back handled. Checked and found correct by mechanism: `_smeared_projection`'s index order, its `mixed`/`ratio`/`shift` assignment and its `|deltae| <= 1e-5` limit branch all match `orthogonalize.f90:120-145` term for term, including the `jbnd <= nbnd_occ` cut on the `alpha_pv` piece and the fact that `ps` is built from the unweighted `dvpsi`; `Smearing.cutoff`'s `fac = 1/sqrt(small)` matches `setup_nbnd_occ.f90:57` (it is not QE's Fermi-Dirac `1/small - 1`, which I checked for); `_alpha_pv` matches `setup_alpha_pv` on both branches; `_pw`'s `2 Re - (G=0)` and its `zero` outer product have the right `[m,n]` orientation and `force_real_g0` is applied to every right-hand side; `_assemble`'s weights are `wg`, which equals `wk` on an insulator band and so matches `dielec.f90`'s `wk`, and the deficient channel of an `nspin = 2` insulator is killed by the row mask rather than by the weight; `_multiplier_response`'s separate row mask and column weight are right and are the one place QE cannot write the term down; `VelocityOperator.projectors`' `+1j*tau` sign is the correct removal of the structure factor's `-i tau`; `generalised_matrix_elements`' `gap[p,q] = e_ket - e_bra` pairs correctly with `augmentation_connection`'s `[bra,ket]`; `total_charge` handles `nspin_mag = 4`; `kpoints.weights` reaches the solver through `for_spin`, so the metal branch's `density_weights` carry the right `degspin`. Dismissed after weighing: `solve_at` performs one final operator application whose step is masked to zero, worth one part in `n_iter` and matching `cgsolve_all`'s own shape; `mixing.py:82`'s `np.asarray(a, dtype=float)` is a hardcoded float64 and a whole-array host round-trip per iteration, but it is host-side and outside any differentiated or jitted path, so by the task's own dtype rule it does not qualify. What I could not establish by reading alone: whether XLA drops the discarded primal of `jax.jvp` over the jitted `_newd`/`_paw_onecenter` at `_perturbed_coefficients` and `paw_response` (a `lower().compile().memory_analysis()` or a warm timing on `alas-epsilon-us.in` settles it, and the eager dispatch of a jitted callee argues it does not); the actual iteration gain from normalising the convergence test; and the size of the `newd` rebuild in `VelocityOperator._operator`, which needs an ultrasoft cell since the one recorded measurement of that call path was taken on a norm-conserving one. Finally, the convergence-test defect almost certainly has siblings at `phonon.py:621` and `strain.py:507`, the two other sites `OPEN.md` H4 lists for this same loop shape; they are not mine and I have not read them.

**`phonon-strain`** (14 files). All four files read end to end in chunks, plus the callers and helpers needed to establish each mechanism. **No hard bug was found in this subsystem**, and that is a result rather than a gap: the four files are unusually careful, and the five candidates that looked like traps were each traced to a source that makes them unreachable or correct. They are recorded here so the same reading is not repeated.

(1) `phonon.overlap_derivatives` (`:864`) and `strain.overlap_derivatives` (`:363`) build `becp = einsum("gc,ng->nc", vkb.conj(), psi)` with no `2 Re(sum) - (G = 0)` correction, which is the gamma-storage trap exactly, and `dynamical_matrix` does pass `gamma_ok=True`. It is unreachable: both functions return `None` unless `calculation.is_ultrasoft`, and `gamma_storage_is_consumable` (`driver.py:940-950`) returns False for any ultrasoft pseudopotential, so `calculation.gamma_only` is never True on the datasets that reach the einsum. `TwoSphereSolver._operator`/`project`/`_preconditioner` have the same uncorrected sums and are unreachable for the other half of the reason -- `dynamical_matrix_at_q` goes through `make_sternheimer` with `gamma_ok` at its default False.

(2) A **metal** at `q != 0` looked unrefused: `require_a_two_sphere_regime` names ultrasoft, spin, core correction, symmetry, spiral, meta-GGA, `nosource` and Hubbard, and no occupation scheme. It is refused, indirectly -- `make_sternheimer` is called with `metals` at its default False (`phononq.py:970`), which reaches `require_a_sternheimer_regime`'s `scheme != "fixed"` raise. The same call refuses gamma storage and `nspin = 2` a second time.

(3) `_core_charge_response` differentiates the whole of `calculation.potential(rho).v_scf` through `at_positions`, which would double-count `dV_loc/du` against `_bare_displacements` if `v_scf` carried the local pseudopotential. It does not (`scf/potential.py:72-76`, Hartree + XC only), so what the `jvp` returns is `f_xc(rho + rho_core) d(rho_core)/dtau` alone, which with the kernel's own `f_xc drho` reproduces `dv_of_drho(drho + drhoc)`.
