(4) `ewald_dynamical_matrix`'s phase and sign were checked line by line against `d2ionq.f90:233-262` rather than assumed: `rgen` returns `r = R - dtau`, so QE's `qrg = 2 pi q.(r + dtau)` is `2 pi q.R`, which is this code's `exp(1j * translations @ q)`; the unphased self term subtracted from the diagonal block, the `cos(arg)` in the reciprocal self term (the sine cancels over the `+-G` pair here), and QE's closing `dy3 = -dy3` against the leading minus on the transpose all line up. The three index conventions that meet in `frozen_force_constants` -- `_force_constants`'s `(nat,3,nat,3)`, `jax.hessian`'s, and the `(a,b,i,j) -> (a,i,b,j)` transpose -- agree.

(5) `_diagonalize` mass-weights with `np.repeat(masses, 3)` against `nat = matrix.shape[0]`, which would be wrong for a multi-species cell if `Structure.masses` were per species; it is `(nat,)` per atom (`structure.py:89`). The `atoms=` subset path was traced through `_symmetrize_modes`, `symmetrize_becsum_modes` and `symmetrize_atom_displacement`: all three are inert under the `nosym` the subset guard forces (`_symmetry_maps is None`, `_becsum_symmetry is None`), so the `(rows, 3)` stacks never reach an averager that would index `nat` atoms.

What could not be established by reading: whether the `q != 0` convergence-criterion difference (finding 5) actually costs a digit on P71's cells, since that needs a run at matched criteria; and the absolute size of findings 1 and 4, which needs `tools/benchmark.py` on a cell larger than the two-atom silicon these paths were validated on. Also examined and judged too small to ship: `strain._eigenvalue_response` (`:610-633`) has **no consumer anywhere in the package** (grep `deigenvalues` returns only its own definition, assignment and dataclass field) and is computed on every `strain_response` call, but it is six applications of a local operator against the ~1800 the solves pay, so it is dead work worth well under a per cent; `phononq.response_force_constants` forces `(3 nat)^2` separate `complex()` device syncs, but its own docstring pre-empts the trade and the flop count dominates the syncs; `hartree_at_q` rebuilds `dense.cartesian(cell)` and the whole `1/|G+q|^2` table on each of its `6 nat` calls per iteration, which is `O(ngm)` against the `v_of_rho` linearisation beside it that OPEN.md H4 already owns.

**`piezo-electrostriction`** (14 files). Both files read in full. Five perf findings, no bug findings: I could not establish a mechanism for a wrong number in either file, and the negatives below are the ones worth recording rather than padding.

The one bug candidate I traced all the way and then had to drop. `clamped_ion_piezoelectric`'s norm-conserving branch (piezo.py:763) returns without ever calling `_full_zone_field_response`, and the justification in the docstring -- "the third multiplies the frozen-state density response, which at frozen plane-wave coefficients is `-delta_ab rho` and therefore zero for every traceless strain" -- covers only traceless strains. For a diagonal component that factor is not zero, so on a symmetry-reduced k-set the mixed derivative does contract two raw wedge sums: `_raw_mixed_state`'s offset is a constant and fixes the primal alone, so the strain tangent is `-delta_ab rho_raw` where the full-zone object is `rho_sym`, and the residue is `-delta_ab int (rho_raw - rho_sym) K (drho/dE_k)_raw`. It is nevertheless harmless, and the reason is the index structure rather than the size: the term is `T_(k)ab = delta_ab w_k`, and `symmetrize_cartesian_tensor` sends it to `delta_ab ((1/N) sum_R R)_kk' w_k'`, whose matrix is exactly `polar_direction` -- the object `require_a_nonpolar_crystal` requires to vanish. So on every crystal this function admits, the average annihilates the term identically, raw or completed, and the true term is zero by the same argument. Quartz's `e_11` and any other diagonal component allowed by a non-polar class are untouched, because the error lands only on the field label and never on the `(a, b)` structure that makes them nonzero. The group the average uses is the run's (`Calculation.symmetries`) while `polar_direction` reads the crystal's, and the two coincide here because `require_a_symmetrisable_response` refuses a shifted grid and an unshifted one is closed under the crystal's group. The same `delta_ab w_k` structure appears in `_screened_strain_term` on a norm-conserving dataset and dies the same way, which is why neither route could ever have shown it.

Other negatives, each checked rather than assumed. The tensor-convention question between `jax.grad` over a full `(3, 3)` strain (the taped route) and a `jvp` along `strain_tangent(a, b) = (E_ab + E_ba)/2` (`constraint_strain_term`, `_multiplier_strain_term`, `_screened_strain_term`) is not a factor of two: the energy at frozen plane-wave coefficients is invariant under an infinitesimal rotation of the cell, so its matrix gradient is symmetric and equals the tensor-convention gradient, and both routes agree. The factors of 2 and the volume in `piezoelectric_zstar_eu_style`, `_screened_strain_term` (no volume, the quadrature's `Omega/N` cancels it) and `_multiplier_strain_term` (`/volume`) are each consistent with their own derivations, and the sign of `constraint_strain_term` is the same `-C/volume` in both routes. `StrainResponse.dpsi` is mirrored (`dpsi[a, b] is dpsi[b, a]`, strain.py:497), so `piezoelectric_from_strain_response`'s loop over all nine pairs reads no zeros. The raw plane-wave inner products in both files (`einsum("skng,skng->skn", ...)`) would be wrong under gamma-only storage and under a spinor, and both are refused upstream by `require_a_sternheimer_regime` (sternheimer.py:1225 for gamma, :1246 for noncollinear), so no gamma or `npol` finding applies here. `internals["weights"]` is the full `(nspin, nk, nbnd)` `wg` from `calculation.occupations`, which is what `clamped_ion_piezoelectric` documents and what `constraint_strain_term` slices with `[:, :nocc]`, so there is no `for_spin`/`degspin` hole at this boundary. `require_a_measured_dataset` nests the PAW refusal inside `if not calculation.is_ultrasoft: return`, and `is_paw`/`is_ultrasoft` are independent properties (driver.py:3073-3078); a PAW dataset with `augmentation is None` would slip past, but I could not find a construction path that produces one, so I did not report it.

Two things I could not fix from inside my own files. `_screened_strain_term` pays for `_frozen_density_response(..., ort=None)` (strain.py:405), whose loop runs all nine `(a, b)` pairs although `strain_tangent(a, b) == strain_tangent(b, a)`, so three of the nine full-zone density jvps are exact duplicates; the waste is real and the fix is in `strain.py`, which another agent owns. And `electrostriction`'s module docstring still says "This module refuses a symmetry-reduced k-set by name", where `_require_closed_for_elastic` refuses only the elastic half; that is prose drift with no number behind it.

**`sum-over-states`** (19 files). All eight assigned files were read whole, not grepped. Four findings, two of them bugs; the perf yield is deliberately thin and the reason is recorded below.

Negatives I actually established, rather than did not look for:

- `shg.py`'s whole assembly was checked index by index against `elkpy/vendor/elk/src/nonlinopt.f90` (the vendored Elk 11.x copy): `_bracket`'s `2E_l - E_n - E_m` resonance, `cc1`'s two accumulations at `(m,l)` and `(l,n)`, `cc2`, both halves of `ce1` (Eq. B13b), `ce2` (B13a), `cs1`'s `head - tail` (B17), and the two double sums (B12a, B16b) with their `-i` and `+i` phases. Every index, sign and numeric factor matches, including the `.T` that carries each accumulation to Elk's output index and the `f.T`/`inv2` gating. The resonance contraction and the `chi_II`/`eta_II`/`sigma_II` split match too. I also re-derived the Rydberg-to-Hartree factor term by term, including the two terms that carry `Delta` (a velocity, not a length), and 4 is uniform across all five coefficient matrices -- so `RYDBERG_TO_HARTREE_SQUARED` is right for a reason the docstring states only loosely.
- `conductivity.py`'s interband sum was checked against `elkpy/vendor/elk/src/dielectric.f90`: `t_nm`, the two resolvents, the conjugation, the `i w_k/Omega` prefactor, and the spin bookkeeping (`wk` sums to `DEGSPIN` here where Elk's `occsv` carries `occmax`, and the product is the same). Correct.
- `photocurrent.generalized_derivative`'s collapse of `sum_{p != n,m}` into `[v^c, g^a] - g^a D^c` was re-derived from scratch, including which excluded term each `1/w` already kills. Correct.
- `nesting.py`: `ifftn(|fftn(g)|^2)` really is the cyclic autocorrelation with numpy's `1/N`, the `np.roll(-q)` direct route is the same sum, the sum rule `mean(N) = D(E_F)^2` follows, and the spin factor is set correctly by the caller (`workflows/nesting.py:167`).
- `spectra.py`: `nonanal`, `mode_dipole`, `beta2` and `polar_mode_permittivity` match `rigid.f90`/`dynmat_sub.f90` in index order and units.
- `magnetoelectric.py:240`'s `lattice.T @ phase_derivative / volume` has no missing `2 pi`: `Polarization.total_phase` is already in units of `2 pi` and `polarization_e_bohr2` is the same contraction. `field_scale` is 1.0 unless `reducebf < 1`, which `builder.py:897` refuses, so dividing the phase step by the input `delta` is right.

What I could not establish by reading: the size of finding 1 on a real shift-current spectrum (only its analogue in `shg.py` is measured), and whether any production run has used `smearing = 'mv'` with `intraband = True` for finding 2.

Why the perf list is short. `OPEN.md` Part III's 2026-09-12 sweep explicitly cleared `magnetoelectric.py`, `effmass.py`, `nesting.py` and `spectra.py` ("their loops are over quantities that genuinely need a separate ground state or a separate diagonalisation. No repeated work to remove"), and `PERFORMANCE.md:4462-4471` already measured the one repeated-work candidate I found in `shg.py` -- the `truncation` diagnostic's second k-sum -- at about 1 s of 22.6, having itself corrected an earlier claim that it cost a whole extra tensor. So I dropped it rather than restate a measurement that refutes it. The remaining candidates I considered and dropped for want of a mechanism worth the reader's time: `_drude`'s full-k einsum running outside the `k_batch` dial that the interband half honours (the extra `(nk, nbnd, nbnd)` multiplet mask is single-digit MB at any production size), the duplicate `refined_states` call in `vibrational_spectrum` (priced at 0.03 s in `PERFORMANCE.md:1823`), and the `moveaxis` copies of `w` in `photocurrent.total`.

Also considered and dropped as documented behaviour: the scissors shift reaching `_drude`'s multiplet block through `_renormalise` (needs a metal, a scissor and a degenerate pair straddling `E_F` at once), and `magnetoelectric`'s branch guard raising on a base state that sits near the branch cut.

**`topology-transport`** (34 files). Read all 21 files of the subsystem in full, plus callers and helpers (workflows/topology.py's DFTSource, workflows/transport.py's _assemble and _assemble_momentum, response/velocity.py, system/cell.py, system/kpoints.py, pseudo/projectors.py, pseudo/augmentation.py, scf/driver.py's at_kpoints) to establish mechanisms.

ZERO silent-wrong-number bugs survived verification in this subsystem, which is a useful result rather than a gap: the overlap primitive and everything built on it is the most carefully written code I have read here. Specifically, I chased each of these to a mechanism and dismissed it:

- **The Miller-index tie in `states.py:_alignment`.** Padding entries all carry Miller (0,0,0) and collide with the real G = 0 in the packed key. The lookup survives because `order` is a *stable* argsort and `np.searchsorted` takes the left edge, so among the tied keys the smallest original index wins, and the real G = 0 sits at a position below `npw` while every padding entry sits at or above it. The same argument covers `parity_matrix`, whose `gather` therefore never lands on a padding row.
- **A 2 pi in `augmentation_at_q`.** `Cell.bg` is `2 pi inv(at).T` in 1/bohr and `KPoints.cartesian` is `coords * tpiba`, so `PlaneWaveStates._difference` and `parity_matrix`'s `kg` are both physical 1/bohr and `qmod` is the right argument for the radial transform.
- **A mixed ultrasoft/norm-conserving dataset in `_scalar_blocks`.** A norm-conserving species gives `nh = 0`, and `AugmentationCharge.block_matrix` writes a zero-width slice, i.e. nothing, leaving that atom's block zero -- which is the correct `q_ij(b)` for it, and `channel_offsets` is unaffected.
- **Block order in `_spinor_blocks`.** It builds `per_atom` in atom order; `_spin_block_diagonal` (driver.py:1371) lays blocks sequentially in atom order and `build_projector_core` (projectors.py:296) emits rows "atoms outermost", so the three agree.
- **The `degspin` trap in the polarization.** `workflows/polarization.py:139` and `:147` pass `int(system.nspin)`, which is 4 for a spinor run, so `combine_string_phases` does not double a spinor band and `polarization_quantum` collapses to 1. `PlaneMesh.kpoints`/`VolumeMesh.kpoints` do call `KPoints.from_crystal`, which applies `DEGSPIN` unconditionally, but nothing in the package calls those two methods -- DFTSource builds its own k-list.
- **Index order.** `green.transmission`'s `a^T S a*`, `spin_transmission`'s `M[s,s'] = a_s^T S a_{s'}^*`, and `momentum_weights`' `Tr[D S^exit D S^tip]` were each re-derived from the definition and each matches, including the `einsum("kij,kji->k")` that the module docstring flags as the trap.
- **Gamma storage reaching `_aligned_overlap`.** `Calculation.at_kpoints` rebuilds the plane-wave set without `gamma_only` and `_adopt_rebuilt_sphere` (driver.py:1397) resets `gamma_only`, `fft_index_minus` and `kplusg`; its docstring names a gamma SCF followed by a topology invariant as exactly the case it was written for.
- **`kubo_from_matrices`' `e_n` in both factors, the FHS plaquette orientation, `orbital_magnetization_sums`' dual states (`solve(M^T, transported)`), and `VolumeMesh.neighbour`'s shift sign** all check out against their stated expressions.

Two leads that live in files another agent owns, recorded here because they are real and no one else is reading my callers: (1) `workflows/topology.py:DFTSource.states` re-evaluates `calculation.potential(self.density, ...)` -- a full `v_of_rho` -- on every call, which is 78 calls for a six-plane Wilson Z2 at the default 24x13, though the density never moves between them; (2) `workflows/transport.py:885` passes `np.real(weights)` into `momentum_weights`, silently discarding the imaginary part on the `method='resolvent'` path, where `green.transmission` at `:750` keeps it complex.

One perf item I deliberately did not spend a slot on: `momentum_weights` (momentum.py:198-211) recomputes `channel_basis` and both `U^dag S U` rotations on every call although they depend only on the Gram matrices and the eigenvalues, which the caller holds fixed across its whole energy loop -- about 1 s on the graphene cell PERFORMANCE.md measures, so it is the same pattern as finding 2 at a tenth the size.

What reading alone could not settle: the exact live-block count inside `velocity.both`'s `jvp` (finding 1) needs `memory_analysis()` or `jax.live_arrays()`, and whether NumPy 2.4's `np.add.at` takes a fast path for a `(slice, slice, array)` index (finding 2) needs a timing.

**`hubbard-tddft-projwfc`** (34 files). I read all twenty-two assigned files in full, in chunks, and followed the mechanism of every candidate out into its callers and into the vendored Fortran where a convention was at stake. The negatives are worth as much as the findings, so here is what I looked for and did not find. `defumat/diffraction/` is clean: the `ifftn`-times-volume transform, the exact bound on the Miller box, the symmorphic-only star collapse and the `(alat/2 pi) B^T` relabelling all check out against the definitions, and the Nyquist guard is in place. `interaction.py`, `tensormoments.py` and `ftm.py` are clean: the `rot_ylm` restriction `u[LMAXX-l:LMAXX+l+1, :width]` is right because `rot_ylm`'s columns are indexed by the real-harmonic index inside a shell and its rows by `m` relative to the centre, and the contraction `a.T M conj(a)` is the same one `projwfc.angular_momentum.orbital_matrices` uses. `kernels.py` and `dyson.py` yielded nothing beyond MEMORY-AUDIT A10, which already records the every-frequency Dyson iteration and the broadcast `fxc`; the `X(1-X-FX)^-1` form is algebraically the same as `(1-X-XF)^-1 X`, and `alda_matrix`'s `fftn/N` is `(1/Omega) int f e^{-iK.r}` as the symmetrised kernel needs. In `chi0.py` rule D4 does **not** bite: each pair contributes `cw(omega) r r^dagger` with `cw` constant across a degenerate multiplet, so the multiplet sum is a trace and is invariant under the eigensolver's rotation; the antiresonant head's `-conj` and the body's `conj(...)[reflection]` are both right, and the scissors renormalisation `d_shifted/d_unshifted` is the Del Sole-Girlanda factor with the already-shifted eigenvalues, which is correct. The relativistic-spinor offset arithmetic I expected to break does not: `manifold._atomic_orbitals` and `projectors._spinor_channels` filter the same `kept` list in the same order with the same `j = l - 1/2` test, so `atomwfc_offsets = 2 (counter + position)` lands on the right columns. Three things I observed and am not reporting, per the audit's own rules. The Hubbard coefficient arrays in `coefficients_from_setup` and `jnp.asarray([1.0, -1.0])` in `_per_channel_energy` are float64 by construction, never through `cell.precision`, so the whole DFT+U energy and potential would stay float64 in a float32 run and `ns` would change dtype between `initial_ns` and `occupation_matrix` and retrace once; that is a dtype finding inside a differentiated path, but it blocks only a performance mode and changes no number in x64, so it is below the bar the audit sets. `slater_from_radial` at `lam = 0` forms `1/r**(k+1)` and would give `0 * inf = nan` on a mesh with `r[0] = 0`, which then silently disables the "U above the bare F^0" guard in `screening_length`; ld1.x meshes are logarithmic and never reach zero, so I could not make it reachable. And in `spinchi0._one_k_terms` the `singular` guard tests `|zomega + eps_up - eps_dn| < 1e-8`, which at `eta = 0` would also fire on a genuine pole if a requested frequency landed within 1e-8 Ry of a transition and would replace the resonance by `df/deps`; the trigger is measure-zero and at the one frequency the module asks for `eta = 0` (omega = 0, the Goldstone check) it fires exactly where it should, so I dropped it. What I could not establish by reading alone: the size of finding 1 on a converged helix (it needs an SCF), and whether `spin_rotations` differs from QE's `d_spin_ldau` by a conjugation -- `new_ns_nc:443-445` contracts `CONJG(d_spin_ldau(is1,is3)) ... d_spin_ldau(is2,is4)` where `build_ns_symmetry` builds `S x conj(S)`, and the module says that choice is P82's and was measured, so I left it alone rather than reporting a convention I could not settle from the source.

**`ultracell-io-sizing`** (29 files). All twenty files were read in full, in chunks, rather than grepped. The ultracell subpackage is where a wrong index would have hidden, so I traced the conventions rather than trusting the docstrings, and the following all check out and are *not* findings: `UltracellAugmentation.difference` is `difference_index.T`, so `[bra, ket]` really is the index of `Q_ket - Q_bra` and it matches `ultracell_becsum`'s `sum_index[q, d] = q + d` pairing and `augmentation_matrix`'s einsum; `becsum_per_copy` and `coefficients_per_difference` are exact inverses under numpy's `ifftn` sign convention, including the `* cells` factor; `ultracell_matrix`'s `columns.T` really is `[bra, ket]`; the `/ N` in `_occupy` pairs with the *unit cell's* volume in `ultracell_density` and with `ultracell.volume(cell)` in `ultracell_energy`, so the density is per cell and the energy per cell; `for_spin` is applied to both `k0` and the folded set before either is used, and the fixed-occupation branch carries `degeneracy = 1` for a spinor; every density site writes `Re(conj(z) z)` rather than `abs(z)**2`; `nspin`, `npol`, `nspin_mag` and `blocks` are kept apart everywhere, the leading spin axis survives on the density and the potential, and the padding mask is doubled for a spinor in the right order; `Ultracell.tile` and `cell_moments`'s reshape agree about which axis is the cell index; `ElkState.nri`'s mixed advanced indexing does reduce over atoms and not over radial points. Two things I considered and dropped. First, `Ultracell.g2` folds the box index at `box // 2`, so an entry at the very outermost dense-sphere Miller index with `q_i > n_i / 2` is labelled `n G + q - n Nd` rather than `n G + q`, giving the Hartree kernel and `dr2` a weight for the wrong representative; I dropped it because the two labels are the same frequency on the box's own grid, it touches only a handful of coefficients at the exact `ecutrho` edge, `miller_at`'s docstring already argues the other side, and it is the same family as `OPEN.md` Part X item 1. Second, `_magnetic_potential` at `nspin_mag = 1` writes `field.at[0].add(-b).at[1].add(b)` on an array whose leading axis has length one, and JAX drops the out-of-bounds scatter silently rather than raising; it is unreachable only because `_as_field` refuses a field at `nspin_mag = 1` two lines earlier (`driver.py:1035` before `:1041`), so it is a latent trap guarded by call order rather than by a check of its own, and I am reporting it here rather than as a finding because nothing reaches it today. I found no defect at all in `io/pwin.py`, `io/output.py`, `io/dynmat.py`, `io/elk.py` or `defumat/cli.py` beyond one wrong help string (`defumat size --k-batch` says "default: all" where `Calculator.estimate` resolves it to the run's own dial), and none in `sizing.py`: I checked the smooth-prefix assumption behind `_box_indices`, the `gamma_only` half-sphere being applied to `npw` and not to `ngm`, the `wf_spin` rather than doubled-eigensolver treatment of `nspin = 2`, and the `k_batch` resolution path through `Calculator.estimate`, and all four are right. I could not run anything, so every scaling figure below is arithmetic on the shapes rather than a measurement.

**`workflows-magnetic`** (22 files). I read all seven assigned files end to end in chunks, plus the callers and helpers needed to close each mechanism (fixed_density_states, System.with_spin/_respin_kpoints, Symmetries' stated rotation convention, build_basis, Calculation.potential/hubbard_terms, topology/invariants' streaming pattern). Every finding was grepped against OPEN.md, AUDIT-2026-09-18.md, MEMORY-AUDIT.md and PERFORMANCE.md by symbol name before being written.

What I looked for and did not find, said plainly so the negatives are on the record. No gamma-storage defect: all three real-space workflows (stm, transport, sts) call refuse_gamma_storage on gamma_storage_is_consumable rather than on the input flag, which is the right test. No missing for_spin: magnons' two sites carry it since the 2026-09-18 fix, whole_grid and denser_grid both apply it, and every fixed_density_states call in these files passes kpoints through the 4th positional slot where nscf.py applies it. No rule-D4 diagonal survives in transport: _top_multiplet_mask takes the whole multiplet in channel_basis' own grouping, and _top_multiplet_share is a function of eigenvalues alone, so it is not a diagonal of an operator and D4 does not reach it. No np.asarray inside a differentiated path and no hardcoded dtype that would change a number: these seven files are host-side assembly, nothing here is jitted or differentiated except the delegations into forces/torque.py and forces/spiral.py, which are another agent's files. No unsymmetrised wedge in run_stm (it sums through Calculation.density, which symmetrises) and none in run_sts or transport (both refuse is_reduced by name). heisenberg_exchange's q.R is correct: spiral_q is in lattice (fractional reciprocal) coordinates per system/spiral.py and builder.py:204, so 2 pi q_frac . R_frac needs no metric, despite the plot label saying "2 pi / a". pdos' split_spin_columns, lowdin_charges and the total_eigenvalues split all check out shape by shape.

What I could not establish by reading alone: for finding 1 I verified the matrix algebra by hand on the hexagonal C3 (M(C3) = [[0,1,0],[-1,-1,0],[0,0,1]], whose transpose is neither C3 nor C3^2 and so is outside the group), but I did not run cardinal_directions on a real hexagonal cell; for finding 2 the size of the error in run_torque on a symmetry-reduced cell is not sized, only its existence; and no timing or memory figure below was measured, each is stated from the shapes and from bounds already in OPEN.md H3/H6.

**`workflows-core`** (31 files). Five findings out of sixteen files, two of them bugs that produce a number and one a crash. What I looked for in every file and did not find: a hand-rolled vmap over k (all of these walk k through `map_k`/`sum_k` or through `Calculation`), a dtype literal inside a differentiated path, an `np.asarray` inside a gradient (every `np.asarray` in these files is on a host-side result after the traced work has finished), a Miller-index wrap between two k-spheres, and a gamma-storage `2 Re - (G=0)` site (these workflows hold no plane-wave sums of their own).

Five candidates I chased and cleared, so they do not need chasing again. (1) `run_ultracell_stm`'s `integral = field.mean() * result.cell_volume` looks short by `N` because `_occupy` divides the weights by `N`, and it is right: `ultracell_band_density` is handed the *unit cell's* volume rather than the ultracell's (`ultracell/states.py:266`), and the two factors cancel exactly. (2) `run_absorption` (tddft.py:217-220) substitutes a caller's k-set with `eqx.tree_at` and no `for_spin`, and never passes `kpoints=` down to `fixed_density_states`, so the boundary at nscf.py:155 never fires -- the same shape as AUDIT-2026-09-18's magnons defect -- but `require_a_sum_over_states_regime` refuses `nspin != 1` and `noncolin` before it can matter, and `for_spin` is the identity at `nspin = 1`. (3) `relax._extrapolate` rebuilds `becsum` only under `previous.is_ultrasoft`, which is `augmentation is not None` and so covers PAW.
